Twisted Triple Product p p -adic L L -function for Finite Slope Families of Hilbert Modular Forms
Ananyo Kazi

Abstract
. Let L L be a totally real field, and p p be a rational prime that is unramified in L L . We construct overconvergent families of classes of relative de Rham cohomology of the universal abelian scheme over Hilbert modular varieties associated to L L . We show that these classes come equipped with Gauss–Manin connection. We prove convergence for p p -adic iteration of this connection, improving upon a technique due to Andreatta–Iovita. We use this to construct a p p -adic twisted triple product L L -function associated to finite slope families of Hilbert modular forms, extending work of Blanco-Chacon–Fornea for Hida families.



2020 Mathematics Subject Classification
Primary 11F33, 11F41, 14G35; Secondary 11F67

1.
Introduction

The theory of
p
-adic
L
-functions has been an important object of study due to its numerous arithmetic applications, most notably towards the Birch and Swinnerton–Dyer conjecture and its generalizations. Katz’s
p
-adic Kronecker limit formula
[
Kat76
]
relates special values of the two variable
p
-adic
L
-function associated to a quadratic imaginary field at finite order Hecke characters to
p
-adic logarithms of elliptic units. The work of Bertolini–Darmon–Prasanna
[
BDP13
]
relates the central critical values of a certain
p
-adic Rankin
L
-function associated to a cusp form
f
and a quadratic imaginary field
K
to
p
-adic Abel–Jacobi images of generalized Heegner cycles. The article of Darmon and Rotger
[
DR14
]
proves a
p
-adic Gross–Zagier formula relating the special values of the
p
-adic Garrett–Rankin triple product
L
-function attached to a triple of Hida families of modular forms to
p
-adic Abel–Jacobi images of certain generalized Gross–Kudla–Schoen cycles in the product of three Kuga–Sato varieties.

The general technique of constructing these
p
-adic
L
-functions consists of two ingredients: firstly, a theory of
p
-adic modular forms and secondly, a way to
p
-adically iterate differential operators on the space of modular forms. For example, in the case of triple product
L
-functions due to the work of Harris–Kudla
[
HK91
]
, Ichino
[
Ich08
]
and others, one expects that the special values of classical
L
-functions that one wishes to
p
-adically interpolate is a meaningful algebraic number upto multiplication by a transcendental period. More precisely, one expects the algebraic number to be expressed as the square of a Petersson inner product of nearly holomorphic modular forms, possibly arising as the image of a holomorphic modular form under the Shimura–Maass operator. Working with Hida families, as in the case of
[
DR14
]
, the
p
-adic analogue of the Shimura–Maass operator is the
\theta
-operator of Serre, which acts as
\theta=q\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}q}
on
q
-expansions. The analogy between the Shimura–Maass operator
\delta
and Serre’s
\theta
operator rests on the fact that they both can be viewed as the application of the Gauss–Manin connection followed by a projection to the space of modular forms. In the case of
\delta
the projection comes from the splitting induced by the Hodge decomposition which is a special property of Kahler manifolds and in the case of
\theta
this is given by the unit root splitting, which is a uniquely
p
-adic phenomenon.

It is then natural to adapt these techniques for finite slope families. However, for finite slope families, the finite slope projector does not converge on
p
-adic modular forms, since the
U
operator is not compact. One can remedy this by working with overconvergent families, following the work of Coleman
[
Col96
]
,
[
Col97
]
. However, a separate problem of working with overconvergent families is the unavailability of the unit root splitting. One approach to solve this problem is to instead
p
-adically iterate the Gauss–Manin connection itself. This is the technique employed in the recent work of Andreatta–Iovita
[
AI21
]
, where they construct triple product
p
-adic
L
-function attached to finite slope families. Moreover, as a consequence of working beyond the ordinary locus, they manage to construct Katz–BDP type
p
-adic
L
-function in the case
p
is non-split in the quadratic imaginary field
[
AI19
]
.

In this paper, we construct a twisted triple product
p
-adic
L
-function associated to finite slope families of Hilbert modular forms, in the case when
p
is unramified in the totally real fields. This generalizes earlier work of Blanco-Chacon and Fornea
[
BF20
]
for the case of Hida families of Hilbert modular forms. Let us now introduce the relevant actors to state the theorem.

Fix a totally real field
L
of degree
g
over
\mathbb{Q}
. Let
\mathfrak{d}
be the different ideal of
L/\mathbb{Q}
. Fix an integer
N\geq 4
and a prime
p\nmid N
that is unramified in
L
. Fix a finite unramified extension
K
of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
that splits
L
. Assume for simplicity
p>2
. Let
\Sigma
be the set of embeddings of
L
in
K
.

The notion of Hilbert modular forms has a slight ambiguity in the literature. Namely, one can talk either about geometric Hilbert modular forms, or about arithmetic Hilbert modular forms. This discrepancy arises from the fact that the Shimura variety associated to the group
G\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\Res_{\mathcal{O}_{L}/\mathbb{Z}}\mathbf{GL}_{2}
at the usual principal
N
level or
\mu_{N}
level doesn’t have an interpretation as a fine moduli of abelian varieties. Instead its connected components, which are parametrized by elements in the strict class group are finite étale quotients of fine moduli spaces of abelian varieties with polarization data and level structure. The geometric Hilbert modular forms are the sections of modular sheaves on the moduli scheme of abelian varieties, whereas the arithmetic Hilbert modular forms are the automorphic forms on the Shimura variety associated to
G
. The arithmetic Hilbert modular forms are necessary for a good Hecke theory. The geometric modular forms, as the name suggests are much more suitable for use in geometric constructions. For the purpose of this introduction we will ignore this discrepancy.

Let
\mathfrak{W}=\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt\mathcal{O}_{K}\llbracket(\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}\rrbracket=\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt\Lambda
be the formal weight space and let
\mathfrak{W}^{0}=\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt\Lambda^{0}
be the connected component of the trivial character.
\Lambda^{0}
is a local ring and let
\mathfrak{m}
be its maximal ideal. Let
\mathcal{W}^{0}
be the adic analytic fibre of
\mathfrak{W}^{0}
, and let
\mathcal{W}^{0}_{p}
be the affinoid open where
|t|\leq|p|\neq 0
for all
t\in\mathfrak{m}
. Let
\mathfrak{W}^{0}_{p}=\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt\mathcal{O}^{+}_{\mathcal{W}^{0}_{p}}
. Let
k^{0}
be the universal weight on
\mathfrak{W}^{0}_{p}
, which is known to be analytic on
1+p(\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})
(Lemma
2.1
). Due to analyticity,
k^{0}
extends to a character on
1+p^{n}\Res_{\mathcal{O}_{L}/\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{G}_{a}\simeq\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma}1+p^{n}\mathbb{G}_{a}
for any
n\geq 1
. Fix one such
n
. Thus we may view
k^{0}
as a product of characters
k^{0}=\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma}k^{0}_{\sigma}
on
1+p^{n}\Res_{\mathcal{O}_{L}/\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{G}_{a}
.

Consider the
p
-adic completion of
M(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})
and let
\mathfrak{X}
be its base change to
\mathfrak{W}^{0}_{p}
. Consider the blow-up spaces
\mathfrak{X}_{r}
obtained by blowing up the
\mathrm{Hdg}
ideal and taking the open where the inverse image ideal is generated by
\mathrm{Hdg}^{p^{r+1}}
. Here
\mathrm{Hdg}
is the ideal generated locally by lifts of the Hasse invariant and
p
. These blow-up spaces are formal models for the rigid analytic overconvergent locus where
|\mathrm{Hdg}^{p^{r+1}}|\geq|p|
. The main results of this work are the following.

Theorem A
.

For suitable choice of
r
, there are interpolation sheaves
\mathfrak{w}^{0}_{k}
and
\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k}
on
\mathfrak{X}_{r}
, that interpolates modular forms and symmetric powers of de Rham classes for weight
k^{0}
respectively. The sheaf
\mathbb{W}_{k}^{0}
on
\mathfrak{X}_{r}
is equipped with an increasing filtration
\{\mathrm{Fil}_{i}\}_{i\geq 0}
. The filtered pieces are locally free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{r}}
-modules and
\mathbb{W}_{k}^{0}
is the completed colimit of
\mathrm{Fil}_{i}\mathbb{W}_{k}^{0}
. The zeroth filtered piece
\mathrm{Fil}_{0}\mathbb{W}_{k}^{0}=\mathfrak{w}_{k}^{0}
coincides with the modular sheaf of weight
k^{0}
.
[§
4
]

Theorem B
.

The Gauss–Manin connection on
H^{1}_{\textrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A})
induces a connection
\nabla
on
\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k}
over the generic fibre that satisfies Griffiths’ transversality with respect to the filtration mentioned above. Moreover, let
k=\prod k_{\sigma}=\prod\exp(u_{\sigma}\log(\cdot))
and
s=\prod s_{\sigma}=\prod\exp(v_{\sigma}\log(\cdot))
be two analytic weights such that
u_{\sigma},v_{\sigma}\in\mathcal{O}^{+}_{\mathcal{W}^{0}_{p}}
. Then for any
g
of weight
k
, with
U_{\mathfrak{P}}(g)=0
for every prime
\mathfrak{P}
in
\mathcal{O}_{L}
lying over
p
,
\nabla^{s}(g)
makes sense as an overconvergent de Rham class of weight
k+2s
.
[§
5
]

To talk about
p
-adic iteration of the Gauss–Manin connection, one needs to consider families of modular forms for
p
-adically varying weights, as well as families of de Rham classes for varying weights. The first object, i.e. the sheaf of overconvergent modular forms has been geometrically constructed and studied for quite some time
[
AIP15
]
,
[
AIP16
]
,
[
AIP18
]
. The novelty of
[
AI21
]
has been the construction of a sheaf
\mathbb{W}_{k}
that interpolates symmetric powers of
H^{1}_{\textrm{dR}}
of the universal elliptic curve for analytic weights
k
, and to iterate the Gauss–Manin connection for
p
-adic weights. This construction is based on the theory of
vector bundles with marked sections
. This approach has been used fruitfully by Graziani
[
Gra20
]
to define interpolation sheaves of de Rham classes. This was used by Aycock
[
Ayc20
]
to interpolate the Gauss–Manin connection
\nabla
in the setting of Hilbert modular forms, following
[
HX14
]
and
[
Liu19
]
in the elliptic and Siegel setting respectively. However he didn’t address the problem of iteration of the connection.

The article
[
Mol21
]
of Molina marks a significant improvement on this technique. One crucial step in the construction of the
p
-adic iteration of
\nabla
is the proof of its convergence. In
[
AI21
]
, the authors need to carry out extremely long and complicated computations to prove this. However, in
[
Mol21
]
, Molina uses a refined version of vector bundles with marked sections to simplify the computations to a large extent. The key idea of his work relies on
[
Mol21
, Lemma 5.1]
which proves that the modified integral model
\mathcal{H}^{\sharp}_{0}
of the de Rham sheaf that one uses to define the interpolation sheaf
\mathbb{W}
, admits a splitting modulo a small power of
p
. Using this one can restrict to a certain well-defined open subspace in the adic geometric vector bundle with marked sections associated to
\mathcal{H}^{\sharp}_{0}
, such that the Gauss–Manin connection converges faster on the sections of this open subspace. With this the author has been able to construct triple product
p
-adic
L
-functions associated with families of quarternionic automorphic forms in Shimura curves over totally real fields in the finite slope situation.

The main idea of our work is similar to that of
[
Mol21
]
, in the sense that we use a refined version of vector bundles with marked sections, using a canonical splitting of our integral model of de Rham sheaf
\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}
modulo a small power of
p
, and prove that the partial Gauss–Manin connection
\nabla(\sigma)
associated to any embedding
\sigma\colon L\to\bar{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
of our fixed totally real field
L
converges fast enough, and that
\nabla(\sigma)
commutes with
\nabla(\tau)
for
\sigma\neq\tau
. In the following, we will describe this in more detail.

Let us describe our approach to proving Theorem
A
. The theory of canonical subgroup tells us that there exists a canonical subgroup of level
n
for all
1\leq n\leq r
. Then over the partial Igusa tower
\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r}
that classifies generic trivializations
\mathcal{O}/p^{n}\mathcal{O}\simeq H_{n}^{\vee}
of the dual of the canonical subgroup, one can define an integral model
\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}
of the modular sheaf
\omega_{\mathcal{A}}
as the submodule generated by all lifts of the image
\dlog(P^{\textrm{univ}})
of the universal generator
P^{\textrm{univ}}
of
H_{n}^{\vee}
under the
\dlog
map. This is an
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r}}
-line bundle that is equipped with a marked section
\dlog(P^{\textrm{univ}})
by construction. Then using the theory of vector bundles with marked section one can define the modular sheaf
\mathfrak{w}_{k}^{0}
of weight
k^{0}
as in
[
AI21
]
. This definition coincides with previous definitions in
[
AIP16a
]
,
[
Gra20
]
and
[
Ayc20
]
.

As mentioned before the key construction in this theory is the definition of interpolation sheaves of de Rham classes. Such a definition appears in
[
Gra20
]
and
[
Ayc20
]
. The definition due to Graziani follows closely the analogous definition in the elliptic case due to Andreatta–Iovita. One has to choose a suitable integral model
\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}
of
H^{1}_{\textrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A})
such that the induced Hodge filtration identifies the zeroth filtered piece with
\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}
. Our choice of the integral model
\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}
differs from that due to Graziani. Let
\underline{\xi}
be the invertible
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r}}
ideal that satisfies
\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}=\underline{\xi}\omega_{\mathcal{A}}
. Let
\widetilde{HW}
be the invertible
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r}}
ideal generated by local lifts of the Hasse-Witt matrix. The first key result towards the goal of defining the interpolation sheaf for de Rham classes is the following.

Theorem C
.

For suitable choice of
r,n
as above, there exists a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r}}
sheaf
\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}\subset H^{1}_{\textrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A})
of rank 2, such that the induced Hodge filtration is

0\to\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}\to\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}\to\underline{\xi}\widetilde{HW}\omega_{\mathcal{A}}^{\vee}\to 0.

Moreover the Hodge filtration admits a canonical splitting modulo
p/\mathrm{Hdg}^{p^{2}}
that coincides with the unit root splitting over the ordinary locus. The splitting is functorial for the lift of Verschiebung.

The proof of this result is the content of §
3
and §
4.1
. We remark that in the case of
\mathbf{GL}_{2,\mathbb{Q}}
one has an analogous splitting of the integral model
\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{E}}
modulo a small power of
p
for the definition of
\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{E}}
used in
[
AI21
]
.

Let
\mathcal{Q}
be the kernel of the splitting in the theorem above. Using this definition of
\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}
, we define a notion of vector bundle with marked sections and marked splitting, similar to the definition of Molina
[
Mol21
, §4.2]
as follows.

\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},\dlog(P^{\textrm{univ}}),\mathcal{Q})(R)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\left\{f\colon\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}(R)\to\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes R\,|\,f\textrm{ is }\mathcal{O}_{L}\textrm{-linear},f(\dlog({P^{\textrm{univ}}})=1),f(\mathcal{Q})=0\right\}

We prove that this vector bundle is representable. In fact as an adic space this has a very simple local description. Let
s
be a local lift of
\dlog(P^{\textrm{univ}})
and
t
be a lift of a local generator of
\mathcal{Q}
. Let
\beta_{n}
be the small power of
p
such that
\dlog(P^{\textrm{univ}})
is a section of
\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}/\beta_{n}
. Let
\eta=p/\mathrm{Hdg}^{p^{2}}
. Then locally as adic spaces,

\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O_{L}}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},\dlog(P^{\textrm{univ}}),\mathcal{Q})=\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}})\left\langle\frac{s-1}{\beta_{n}},\frac{t}{\eta}\right\rangle.

Here
\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}})
is the usual vector bundle whose sections over
R
are
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes R
-linear maps
\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}(R)\to\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes R
. (Note since we assume that
p
is unramified in
L
it is fine to take
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes R
as the codomain instead of
\mathfrak{d}^{-1}\otimes R
.) We then define the interpolation sheaf
\mathbb{W}_{k}^{0}
using this refined version of VBMS and prove the rest of the statements in Theorem
A
. The proof of these results concerns the rest of §
4
.

The next section §
5
is where we define the Gauss–Manin connection on
\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k}
, and show that it can be
p
-adically iterated for analytic weights. The strategy is exactly similar to
[
AI21
]
and
[
Mol21
]
. Using Grothendieck’s description of connections we prove the first part of Theorem
B
:

Theorem
.

The Gauss–Manin connection on
H^{1}_{\textrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A})
induces a connection
\nabla
on
\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k}
over the generic fibre that satisfies Griffiths’ transversality with respect to the filtration mentioned above.

The definition of
p
-adic iteration of
\nabla
follows the strategy of
[
AI21
]
. For each embedding
\sigma\in\Sigma
, the Kodaira–Spencer class corresponding to
\sigma
gives a partial connection
\nabla(\sigma)\colon\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k}[1/p]\to\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k+2\sigma}[1/p]
. We first study the convergence properties of
\nabla(\sigma)
over the ordinary locus using
q
-expansions and local coordinates. Here we realize that
\nabla(\sigma)
behaves exactly like the connection
\nabla
in the case of elliptic curves
[
AI21
]
. However, owing to our use of VBMS with marked splitting, we get faster convergence estimates. This is really the key improvement to the technique of
[
AI21
]
and mimics the results obtained by Molina. In particular
[
AI21
, Proposition 4.10]
states that for any
p
-depleted
g\in H^{0}(\mathfrak{IG}^{\textrm{ord}}_{n},\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k})
,

(\nabla^{p-1}-\textrm{id})^{p}(g)\in pH^{0}(\mathfrak{IG}^{\textrm{ord}}_{n},\mathbb{W}^{0}).

Here
\mathbb{W}^{0}
is the direct image of the structure sheaf of
\mathbb{V}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{E}},\dlog(P^{\textrm{univ}}))\to\mathfrak{IG}^{\textrm{ord}}_{n}
. Instead, if we work with
\mathbb{V}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{E}},\dlog(P^{\textrm{univ}}),\mathcal{Q})
, and let
\mathbb{W}^{0}
be the direct image of its structure sheaf, then we can prove that

(\nabla^{p-1}-\textrm{id})(g)\in pH^{0}(\mathfrak{IG}^{\textrm{ord}}_{n},\mathbb{W}^{0}).

An exactly analogous statement holds for
\nabla(\sigma)
for all
\sigma
in the Hilbert case.
Then for any analytic weight
s=\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma}s_{\sigma}=\prod\exp(v_{\sigma}\log(\cdot))
and any Hilbert modular form
g
of weight
k
that is depleted at all primes above
p
, satisfying the conditions on
u_{\sigma},v_{\sigma}
as mentioned in Theorem
B
, we show that
\nabla(\sigma)^{s_{\sigma}}(g)
defined using a formal expression

\nabla(\sigma)^{s_{\sigma}}(g)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\exp\left(\frac{v_{\sigma}}{p^{f_{\sigma}}-1}\log\nabla(\sigma)^{p^{f_{\sigma}}-1}\right)(g)

is actually the limit of a Cauchy sequence in
\mathbb{W}^{0}
over the ordinary locus. Finally we manage to show that by shrinking the initial radius of overconvergence, it is possible to realize
\nabla(\sigma)^{s_{\sigma}}(g)
as an overconvergent de Rham class. We can also check on
q
-expansions that
\nabla(\sigma)
and
\nabla(\tau)
commutes for different
\sigma,\tau
. Then the definition of
\nabla^{s}=\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma}\nabla(\sigma)^{s_{\sigma}}
is obvious. This then proves the second part of Theorem
B
.

Theorem
.

Let
k=\prod k_{\sigma}=\prod\exp(u_{\sigma}\log(\cdot))
and
s=\prod s_{\sigma}=\prod\exp(v_{\sigma}\log(\cdot))
be two analytic weights such that
u_{\sigma},v_{\sigma}\in\mathcal{O}^{+}_{\mathcal{W}^{0}_{p}}
. Then for any
g
of weight
k
, depleted at all primes above
p
,
\nabla^{s}(g)
makes sense as an overconvergent de Rham class of weight
k+2s
.

We remark that while we prove the splitting of
\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}
in Theorem
C
modulo a small power of
p
, with a little more work it is possible to prove such a splitting modulo higher powers, under the assumption that the radius of overconvergence is small enough (i.e.
r
in
\mathfrak{X}_{r,I}
is large enough). One important consequence of working with the refined version of VBMS is that we manage to relax the condition on analyticity of the weight
s
slightly, compared to that in
[
AI21
]
. Moreover, working with splitting of
\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}
modulo higher powers allows one to relax this condition even further to whatever extent desired, at the cost of shrinking the radius of overconvergence. While preparing this manuscript, we came to know about the recent work of Andrew Graham, Vincent Pilloni and Joaquín Rodrigues Jacinto
[
GPJ23
]
. Their work in the elliptic case uses a similar idea of working with rational subdomains of the form
\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}})\left\langle\frac{s-1}{\beta_{n}},\frac{t}{\eta}\right\rangle
. More precisely, they first construct strict neighbourhoods
\mathcal{U}_{\mathrm{HT},n}
(in their notation) of the Igusa tower at
\infty
(they work with the Igusa tower which classifies trivializations of both the connected and étale part of the
p
-divisible group) for all
n\geq 1
, inside the torsor
P^{\text{an}}_{\text{dR},\mathcal{X}_{\text{ord}}}
of trivializations of the de Rham homology, over the ordinary locus. Base changed to the Igusa tower (along the projection to
\mathcal{X}_{\text{ord}}
), the strict neighbood
\mathcal{U}_{\mathrm{HT},n}
is a vector bundle which is a torsor for the formal group (upto connected components)

\mathcal{B}=\begin{pmatrix}1+p^{n}\mathbb{G}_{a}&0\\
p^{n}\mathbb{G}_{a}&1+p^{n}\mathbb{G}_{a}\end{pmatrix}.

Next they construct overconvergent extensions
\mathcal{U}_{n,r}
of these strict neighbourhoods over the overconvergent locus
\mathcal{X}_{r}
for each
r\geq 1

[
GPJ23
, §3.4]
. These extensions form a cofinal system of strict neighbourhoods of the Igusa tower inside
P^{\text{an}}_{\text{dR}}
which is the torsor of trivializations of the de Rham homology over the entire modular curve. Since they define their space of nearly overconvergent modular forms as the direct limit
\varinjlim_{n,r}\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{U}_{n,r})
, this allows them to define
\nabla^{s}
without any conditions on the analyticity on
s
. We remark that by using splittings of
\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}
modulo higher powers of
p
, one should be able to show that for
r
large enough (so as to allow for the existence of the level
n
canonical subgroup
H_{n}
), the base change of
\mathcal{U}_{n,r}
to the level
n
partial Igusa tower is a vector bundle which is a torsor for
\mathcal{B}
as above. We do not explore this direction in this work because we are interested in applying our theory to the construction of Katz–BDP type
p
-adic
L
-functions in the future. In such constructions it is necessary to control the radius of overconvergence of the nearly overconvergent form that one obtains by applying a
p
-adic iterate of
\nabla
to an overconvergent form. By what we said above, relaxing the condition on analyticity comes at the cost of shrinking the radius of overconvergence. We still encourage the readers to look at
[
GPJ23
]
.

1.1.
Application to construction of
p
-adic
L
-function

In their article
[
BF20
]
, Blanco-Chacon and Fornea construct a twisted triple product
p
-adic
L
-function associated to two nearly ordinary families. In the special case of a real quadratic extension
L/\mathbb{Q}
, they relate the special values of their
L
-function to syntomic Abel–Jacobi images of
generalized Hirzebruch–Zagier cycles
. In this paper we construct a twisted triple product
p
-adic
L
-function associated to finite slope families of Hilbert modular forms, generalizing the definition of
[
BF20
]
using the theory of
p
-adic iteration of Gauss–Manin connection.

Let
L/F
be a quadratic extension of totally real fields. The weights of the arithmetic Hilbert modular forms associated to the group
G_{L}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\Res_{\mathcal{O}_{L}/\mathbb{Z}}\mathbf{GL}_{2}
are parametrized by pairs
(v,n)\in\mathbb{Z}[\Sigma_{L}]\times\mathbb{Z}
. These forms correspond to weight
k=2v+nt_{L}
geometric Hilbert modular forms on which the centre
\mathbb{A}_{L}^{\times}
acts via a Hecke character with infinity type
-nt_{L}
, where
t_{L}=\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}\sigma
is the parallel weight of weight 1. Let
g,f
be arithmetic Hilbert modular forms of weight
(v,n)\in\mathbb{Z}[\Sigma_{L}]\times\mathbb{Z}
and
(w,2n)\in\mathbb{Z}[\Sigma_{F}]\times\mathbb{Z}
for the groups
G_{L}=\Res_{\mathcal{O}_{L}/\mathbb{Z}}\mathbf{GL}_{2}
and
G_{F}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\Res_{\mathcal{O}_{F}/\mathbb{Z}}\mathbf{GL}_{2}
respectively. The natural inclusion of the Shimura varieties induced by
\mathbf{GL}_{2}{(\mathbb{A}_{F})}\to\mathbf{GL}_{2}{(\mathbb{A}_{L})}
induces a diagonal restriction map
\zeta^{*}\colon S^{G_{L}}(K_{1}(N),(v,n),\mathbb{C})\to S^{G_{F}}(K_{1}(N),(v_{|F},2n),\mathbb{C})
on the space of arithmetic cuspforms of weight
(v,n)
.

Assume now that
g,f
are primitive eigenforms of level
N_{g},N_{f}
respectively, such that the automorphic representation
\Pi
of
\mathbf{GL}_{2}{(\mathbb{A}_{L\times F})}
defined by their unitarization has trivial central character. Assume also that there exists
t\in\mathbb{N}[\Sigma_{L}]
such that
(v+t)_{|F}=w
. This condition is called
F
-dominated weights in
[
BF20
]
, and generalizes the notion of unbalanced triple of weights in the triple product
L
-function case. Then under some assumptions, Ichino’s formula relates the central value of the twisted triple product
L
-function associated to
\Pi
to a Petersson product of cuspforms (Lemma
7.8
)

L\left(\frac{1}{2},\Pi,r\right)=(\ast)\left|\langle\zeta^{*}(\delta^{t}\breve{g}),\breve{f}^{*}\rangle\right|^{2}.

Here
\breve{g},\breve{f}
are oldforms coming from
g,f
respectively at some level
\mathfrak{A}
that is a common multiple of
N_{g},N_{f}
,
\breve{f}^{*}
denotes the form whose Fourier coefficients are complex conjugates of that of
\breve{f}
, and
(\ast)
are some constants which can be made to be non-zero by suitable choice of
\breve{g}
and
\breve{f}
. The operator
\delta
is the Maass–Shimura operator. The value
\langle\zeta^{*}(\delta^{t}\breve{g}),\breve{f}^{*}\rangle/\langle f^{*},f^{*}\rangle
is algebraic, and can be interpolated. Let
\omega_{g},\omega_{f}
be overconvergent cuspforms interpolating
g,f
of weight
(v_{g},n_{g})\in\mathcal{W}^{G_{L}}(\Lambda_{g})
and
(w_{f},m_{f})\in\mathcal{W}^{G_{F}}(\Lambda_{F})
, such that there exists
r\in\mathcal{W}^{G_{L}}(\Lambda_{g})
satisfying
((v_{g}+r)_{|F},{n_{g}}_{|F})=(w_{f},m_{f})
. Then under some analyticity conditions on the weights
v_{g},r
(Assumption
7.10
), and for any unramified prime
p
in
L
that does not divide the level
\mathfrak{A}
, so that
f
has slope
\leq a
, we define a
p
-adic
L
-function (Definition
7.14
),

\mathscr{L}^{g}_{p}(\breve{\omega}_{g},\breve{\omega}_{f})=\frac{\langle H^{\dagger,\leq a}(\zeta^{*}\nabla^{r}\breve{\omega}_{g}^{[\mathcal{P}]}),\breve{\omega}_{f}^{*}\rangle}{\langle\omega_{f}^{*},\omega_{f}^{*}\rangle}.

This
p
-adic
L
-function is meromorphic on the space
\mathrm{Spm}(\Lambda_{g}\hat{\otimes}_{\Lambda^{G_{F}}}\Lambda_{f})
.

Theorem D
.

Let
((x,c),(y,2c))\in\mathrm{Spm}(\Lambda_{g}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda_{f})
be classical weights, such that
(x,c)
and
(y,2c)
are
F
-dominated, i.e. there exists
t\in\mathbb{N}[\Sigma_{L}]
such that
(x+t)_{|F}=y
, and such that the specialization of the overconvergent sheaf
\mathfrak{w}_{k}
at
(y,2c)
consists entirely of classical forms, and the fibre of the eigenvariety at
(y,2c)
is étale. Assume the specializations
g_{x}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}={\omega_{g}}_{x}
, and
f_{y}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}={\omega_{f}}_{y}
are eigenforms for Hecke operators outside
\mathfrak{A}
, and the Hecke polynomial of
T_{\mathfrak{p}}
is separable for all primes
\mathfrak{p}|p
. Assume
f_{y}
has slope
\leq a
. Then

\mathscr{L}^{g}_{p}(\breve{\omega}_{g},\breve{\omega}_{f})((x,c),(y,2c))=\mathrm{E}\cdot\frac{\langle\zeta^{*}(\delta^{t}\breve{g}_{x}),\breve{f}^{*}_{y}\rangle}{\langle f^{*}_{y},f^{*}_{y}\rangle},

where
E
is some Euler factor depending on
g_{x},f_{y}
. Moreover, this
p
-adic
L
-function recovers the
p
-adic
L
-function constructed in
[
BF20
]
for Hida families, at all points where they both are defined.

In upcoming work with Ting-Han Huang, we will use this
p
-adic
L
-function to prove a
p
-adic Gross-Zagier formula similar to
[
BF20
]
.

1.2.

2.
The setup

Notation.
Let
L
be a totally real number field of degree
[L\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbb{Q}]=g>1
. Denote by
\mathfrak{d}
the different ideal of
\mathcal{O}_{L}
. Fix an integer
N\geq 4
. Let
p\nmid N
be a prime which is unramified in
L
. Suppose
p
splits as
p=\mathfrak{P}_{1}\cdots\mathfrak{P}_{h}
and let their inertia degree be
f(\mathfrak{P}_{i}|p)=f_{i}
. Fix a finite unramified Galois extension
K
of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
where
L
is split and let
\Sigma\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\{\sigma\colon L\to K\}
be the set of embeddings of
L
in
K
. Let
q=p
if
p\neq 2
and
q=4
otherwise.

2.1.
The weight space

Let
\mathbb{T}=\Res_{\mathcal{O}_{L}/\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{G}_{m}
. Then
\mathbb{T}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})=(\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}
. Denote by
\Lambda\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathcal{O}_{K}\llbracket\mathbb{T}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rrbracket
the base change of the Iwasawa algebra
\mathbb{Z}_{p}\llbracket\mathbb{T}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rrbracket
to
\mathcal{O}_{K}
. Then
\Lambda=\mathcal{O}_{K}[\Delta]\llbracket\mathbb{T}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})_{\textrm{tf}}\rrbracket\simeq\mathcal{O}_{K}[\Delta]\llbracket T_{1},\dots,T_{g}\rrbracket
, where
\Delta\subset\mathbb{T}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
is the torsion subgroup and we choose an isomorphism
\mathbb{T}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})_{\textrm{tf}}\simeq\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{g}
of the torsion free part with
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{g}
. Under this isomorphism, the standard basis elements
e_{i}
of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{g}
are sent to
1+T_{i}
. Let
\Lambda^{0}=\mathcal{O}_{K}\llbracket\mathbb{T}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})_{\textrm{tf}}\rrbracket
be the quotient of
\Lambda
that sends
\Delta\mapsto 1
, and let
\mathfrak{m}=(p,T_{1},\dots,T_{g})
be its maximal ideal.

Let
\mathfrak{W}=\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{\Lambda}
and
\mathfrak{W}^{0}=\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{\Lambda^{0}}
. Let
\mathcal{W}=\Spa(\Lambda,\Lambda)^{\textrm{an}}
be the analytic adic space associated to
\mathfrak{W}
and similarly define
\mathcal{W}^{0}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\Spa(\Lambda^{0},\Lambda^{0})^{\textrm{an}}
.
\mathcal{W}
satisfies the following universal property: for any complete Huber pair
(R,R^{+})
over
(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
,

\Hom_{\Spa(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})}\big(\Spa(R,R^{+}),\mathcal{W}\big)=\Hom_{\textrm{gp}\mathchar 45\relax\textrm{sch}}\big({\mathbb{T}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}_{R^{+}},{\mathbb{G}_{m}}_{R^{+}}\big)=\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\textrm{cont}}\big(\mathbb{T}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}),{(R^{+})}^{\times}\big).

\mathcal{W}^{0}
satisfies a similar universal property with respect to
\mathbb{T}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})_{\textrm{tf}}
, i.e.

\Hom_{\Spa(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})}\big(\Spa(R,R^{+}),\mathcal{W}^{0}\big)=\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\textrm{cont}}\big(\mathbb{T}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})_{\textrm{tf}},(R^{+})^{\times}\big).

Let
\widetilde{\mathfrak{W}^{0}}
be the admissible blow-up of
\mathfrak{W}^{0}
along
\mathfrak{m}
. For
\alpha\in\mathfrak{m}\setminus\mathfrak{m}^{2}
, let
\mathfrak{W}^{0}_{\alpha}
be the open in
\widetilde{\mathfrak{W}^{0}}
where
\mathfrak{m}
is generated by
\alpha
. Suppose
\mathfrak{W}^{0}_{\alpha}=\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{B^{0}_{\alpha}}
. Let
\mathcal{W}^{0}_{\alpha}
be the rational open in
\mathcal{W}^{0}
obtained by taking the generic fibre of
\mathfrak{W}^{0}_{\alpha}
. This is given by the affinoid adic
\Spa(B^{0}_{\alpha}[1/\alpha],B^{0}_{\alpha})
and is thus the adic spectrum of a Tate ring. The
\mathfrak{W}^{0}_{\alpha}
cover
\widetilde{\mathfrak{W}^{0}}
for varying
\alpha
and hence their adic generic fibre cover
\mathcal{W}^{0}
. In particular, the map induced by
\widetilde{\mathfrak{W}^{0}}\to\mathfrak{W}^{0}
on the associated analytic adic spaces is an isomorphism.

The natural inclusion
\Lambda^{0}\to\Lambda
is finite flat and the induced finite flat morphism
\mathcal{W}\to\mathcal{W}^{0}
realizes
\mathcal{W}
as a disjoint union of copies of
\mathcal{W}^{0}
indexed by
\Delta
. For
\alpha\in\mathfrak{m}\setminus\mathfrak{m}^{2}
we let
\mathcal{W}_{\alpha}
be the inverse image of
\mathcal{W}^{0}_{\alpha}
under this morphism.

We remark that all classical weights can be realized as points in
\mathcal{W}_{p}
.

For
I=[p^{a},p^{b}]
with
a\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{-\infty\}
and
b\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
, let
\mathcal{W}^{0}_{\alpha,I}\subset\mathcal{W}^{0}_{\alpha}
be the rational open subset defined as follows:

\mathcal{W}^{0}_{\alpha,I}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\{x\in\mathcal{W}^{0}_{\alpha}\,\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\,|p(x)|\leq|\alpha(x)|^{p^{a}}\neq 0,\ |\alpha(x)|^{p^{b}}\leq|p(x)|\neq 0\}.

Let
\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\Gamma(\mathcal{W}^{0}_{\alpha,I},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}^{0}_{\alpha,I}}^{+})
and
\Lambda_{\alpha,I}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\Gamma(\mathcal{W}_{\alpha,I},{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{\mathcal{W}_{\alpha,I}})
. Let
\mathfrak{W}^{0}_{\alpha,I}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}}
and
\mathfrak{W}_{\alpha,I}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{\Lambda}_{\alpha,I}
.

For
\alpha
varying in
\mathfrak{m}\setminus\mathfrak{m}^{2}
, the different
\mathfrak{W}^{0}_{\alpha,I}
glue together to form a formal scheme
{\mathfrak{W}^{0}_{I}}
with adic generic fibre
\mathcal{W}^{0}_{I}
.
\mathcal{W}^{0}_{I}
can be described as follows.

\mathcal{W}^{0}_{I}=\{x\in\mathcal{W}^{0}\,\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\,(\exists\alpha\in\mathfrak{m},|p|_{x}\leq|\alpha^{p^{a}}|_{x}\neq 0)\land(\forall\alpha\in\mathfrak{m},|\alpha^{p^{b}}|_{x}\leq|p|_{x}\neq 0)\}

Then
\mathcal{W}^{0}_{[0,1]}=\mathcal{W}^{0}_{p}
and
\mathcal{W}^{0}_{[1,\infty]}=\mathcal{W}^{0}
. At the level of formal models,
\mathfrak{W}^{0}_{[0,1]}=\mathfrak{W}^{0}_{p}
and
\mathfrak{W}^{0}_{[1,\infty]}=\widetilde{\mathfrak{W}^{0}}
.

We fix one such
\alpha
. For the purpose of defining the
p
-adic iteration of the Gauss–Manin connection, which is the technical heart of this work (§
5.4
) we need to assume
\alpha=p
. For everything before that section we can choose any
\alpha
. But we should also mention that the construction of the main objects of this work, i.e. the interpolation sheaves of modular forms and de Rham classes of varying weight
k
, all take place over a weight space where
p\neq 0
. In particular we do not study the perfect overconvergent modular forms of
[
AIP18
]
or
[
AIP16a
]
.

Analyticity of the universal character.

Let
k^{\textrm{un}}\colon\mathbb{T}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to\Lambda
be the universal character. Denote by
k^{0}\colon\mathbb{T}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to\Lambda\to\Lambda^{0}
the character obtained by composing the universal character with the projection onto the component of the trivial character, and let
k^{0}_{\alpha,I}\colon\mathbb{T}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to\Lambda^{0}\to\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}
be its restriction to
\mathcal{W}^{0}_{\alpha,I}
.

Lemma 2.1
.

For
I\subset[0,q^{-1}p^{n}]
, the restriction of
k^{0}_{\alpha,I}
to
1+qp^{n-1}(\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})
is analytic. Thus it extends to a character

k^{0}_{\alpha,I}\colon\mathcal{W}^{0}_{\alpha,I}\times\mathbb{T}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})(1+qp^{n-1}\Res_{\mathcal{O}_{L}/\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{+})\to\mathbb{G}_{m}^{+}

which restricts to a character

k^{0}_{\alpha,I}\colon\mathcal{W}^{0}_{\alpha,I}\times(1+qp^{n+m-1}\Res_{\mathcal{O}_{L}/\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{+})\to 1+qp^{m}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{+}.

Proof.

This is an adaptation of the proof of
[
AIP18
, Proposition 2.1]
. See also
[
AIP16a
, Proposition 2.8]
.
∎

Since
k^{0}_{\alpha,I}
is analytic, and
1+qp^{n-1}\Res_{\mathcal{O}_{L}/\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{G}_{a}\simeq\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma}1+qp^{n-1}\mathbb{G}_{a}
, the universal character splits into components
k^{0}_{\alpha,I}=\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma}k_{\sigma}
.

Remark 2.2
.

Note that the analyticity of the character does not depend on
\alpha
. So we can glue the different
k^{0}_{\alpha,I}
together to obtain a character
k^{0}_{I}\colon\mathcal{W}^{0}_{I}\times\mathbb{T}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})(1+qp^{n-1}\Res_{\mathcal{O}_{L}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{+})\to\mathbb{G}_{m}^{+}
.

2.1.1.
The weight space for the group
\Res_{L/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbf{GL}_{2,L}

In the literature there are two distinct notions of Hilbert modular forms. The first one is realized as sections of a modular sheaf on moduli of abelian varieties with real multiplication and additional data. The associated weight space is the one we defined above. There is also the notion of arithmetic Hilbert modular forms which are sections of automorphic line bundles on the Shimura variety associated to the group
G\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\Res_{L/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbf{GL}_{2,L}
. It is necessary to consider these modular forms for arithmetic applications. The relation between these two different notions will be clarified in the next section. Here we will define the weight space associated to the group
G
.

Let
\Lambda^{G}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathcal{O}_{K}\llbracket\mathbb{T}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\rrbracket
, and let
\mathfrak{W}^{G}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{\Lambda^{G}}
. Let
\mathcal{W}^{G}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\Spa(\Lambda^{G},\Lambda^{G})^{\textrm{an}}
be the associated analytic adic space. There is a natural morphism
\mathfrak{W}^{G}\to\mathfrak{W}
given by the map
\Lambda\to\Lambda^{G}
induced by
(\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}\to(\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
sending
t\mapsto(t^{2},\mathrm{N}_{L/\mathbb{Q}}(t))
. This induces a map on the corresponding analytic adic spaces. On classical points this map can be described as sending
(v,w)\in\mathcal{W}^{G}(\mathbb{C}_{p})\mapsto v^{2}\cdot(w\circ\mathrm{N}_{L/\mathbb{Q}})
, where
v\colon(\mathcal{O}_{L}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{p}
is a continuous character and so is
w\colon\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{p}
. Denote the universal character by
k^{\textrm{un}}_{G}\colon(\mathcal{O}_{L}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\to(\Lambda^{G})^{\times}
.

2.2.
The Hilbert modular variety

Let
\mathfrak{c}
be a fractional ideal of
L
and let
\mathfrak{c}^{+}
be the cone of totally positive elements. Let
M(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})
be the moduli scheme over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
classifying tuples
(A_{/S},\iota,\lambda,\psi)
consisting of (1) an abelian scheme
A\to S
for any
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-scheme
S
, (2) an embedding
\iota\colon\mathcal{O}_{L}\xhookrightarrow{}\End_{S}(A)
, (3) if
P\subset\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{L}}(A,A^{\vee})
is the étale sheaf of symmetric
\mathcal{O}_{L}
-linear homomorphisms from
A
to its dual
A^{\vee}
, and
P^{+}
is the cone of polarizations, then an isomorphism
\lambda\colon(P,P^{+})\simeq(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{c}^{+})
of étale sheaves of invertible
\mathcal{O}_{L}
-modules with a notion of positivity such that the induced map
A\otimes\mathfrak{c}\xrightarrow{\sim}A^{\vee}
is an isomorphism (the Deligne–Pappas condition), and (4) a closed immersion
\psi\colon\mu_{N}\otimes\mathfrak{d}^{-1}\xhookrightarrow{}A[N]
compatible with
\mathcal{O}_{L}
-action.

Let
[\mathfrak{c}]\in\textrm{Cl}^{+}(L)
be the class of
\mathfrak{c}
in the strict class group of
L
. Any two representatives of a class are related via multiplication by a totally positive unit, and hence the corresponding moduli problems are isomorphic. Using this, we henceforth fix
\mathfrak{c}
coprime to
p
. That will ensure that upon a choice of a generator of
\mathfrak{c}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}
as an
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-module, the
p
-divisible groups of
\mathfrak{c}
-polarized abelian varieties satisfying the Deligne–Pappas condition are principally polarized.

Let
\bar{M}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})
be a toroidal compactification of
M(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})
and let
M^{*}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})
be the minimal compactification. There is a semi-abelian scheme
\pi\colon\mathcal{A}\to\bar{M}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})
which restricts to the universal abelian scheme over
M(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})
and degenerates to a torus at the cusps. Let
\omega_{\mathcal{A}}
be the canonical extension of the sheaf of invariant differentials of the universal abelian scheme to the cusps. There is a largest open subscheme
M^{R}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})\subset\bar{M}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})
, called the Rapoport locus where
\omega_{\mathcal{A}}
is an invertible
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{M^{R}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})}
-module. Since we assume
p
is unramified in
L
, the complement of the Rapoport locus is empty and
\bar{M}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})
is projective and smooth over
\textrm{Spec}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}

[
DP94
]
. The boundary
D\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\bar{M}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})\setminus M(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})
is a relative normal crossings divisor. The minimal compactification
M^{*}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})
is normal and projective.

Henceforth denote
\bar{M}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})
by
X
. Let
\mathfrak{X}
be its
p
-adic completion. Let
\mathrm{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
be the canonical extension of the relative de Rham sheaf of the universal abelian scheme to the cusps. It is a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}}
-module of rank
2
and it is endowed with an integrable connection
\nabla\colon\mathrm{H}_{\mathcal{A}}\to\mathrm{H}_{\mathcal{A}}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{X}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\log(D))
called the Gauss–Manin connection. It also fits in an exact sequence

(1)

0\to\omega_{\mathcal{A}}\to\mathrm{H}_{\mathcal{A}}\to\omega^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}^{\vee}}\to 0

which defines the Hodge filtration. The maps in the sequence are
\mathcal{O}_{L}
-linear. We also use the principal polarization of the
p
-divisible group of
\mathcal{A}
to henceforth identify
\omega_{\mathcal{A}^{\vee}}
and
\omega_{\mathcal{A}}
.

Fix
\alpha\in\mathfrak{m}\setminus\mathfrak{m}^{2}
and
I
as above. Let
\mathfrak{X}_{\alpha,I}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathfrak{X}\times_{\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.63885pt{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathfrak{W}^{0}_{\alpha,I}
. Since
L
splits in
K
,
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\alpha,I}}\simeq\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\alpha,I}}
. Later it will be useful to collect the
\sigma
’s in
h
different groups according to the valuation they induce on
\mathcal{O}_{L}
(recall
p=\mathfrak{P}_{1}\cdots\mathfrak{P}_{h}
in
L
). Hence over
\mathfrak{X}_{\alpha,I}
, the exact sequence (
1
) splits into
h
exact sequences

0\to\omega_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma)\to\mathrm{H}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma)\to\omega^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma)\to 0

where each
\omega_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma)
is an invertible
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\alpha,I}}
-module and
\mathrm{H}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma)
is a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\alpha,I}}
-module of rank 2.

Let
\xi\colon\mathfrak{X}_{\alpha,I}/(\alpha)\xhookrightarrow{}\mathfrak{X}_{\alpha,I}
be the closed subscheme defined by
\alpha=0
. The Hasse invariant is a section
\mathrm{Ha}\in(\Lambda^{g}(\xi^{*}\omega_{\mathcal{A}}))^{\otimes(p-1)}
. Define the Hasse ideal to be
\underline{\mathrm{Ha}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathrm{Ha}\cdot(\Lambda^{g}(\xi^{*}\omega_{\mathcal{A}}))^{\otimes(1-p)}
.

Theorem 2.3
.

The Hasse invariant vanishes with multiplicity one along the irreducible components of its divisor.

Proof.

[
AG05
, Corollary 8.18]
.
∎

With the notation above, for each
r\geq 1
, consider the inverse image of
\underline{\mathrm{Ha}}^{p^{r+1}}
under the map
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\alpha,I}}\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\alpha,I}/(\alpha)}
and call this ideal
\mathrm{Hdg}_{r}
. We call a local lift of a generator of
\underline{\mathrm{Ha}}
as
\mathrm{Hdg}
. Then locally
\mathrm{Hdg}_{r}
is the ideal
(\alpha,\mathrm{Hdg}^{p^{r+1}})
.

Recall a classical weight is an element of
\mathbb{Z}[\Sigma]
. The Hilbert modular sheaf of a classical weight
k=\sum n_{\sigma}\sigma
is defined as

\omega_{\mathcal{A}}^{k}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\hat{\bigotimes_{\sigma}}\omega_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma)^{n_{\sigma}}.

Note that
\omega^{k}_{\mathcal{A}}
is obtained as the image of
\omega_{\mathcal{A}}
under the map induced by change of the structural group

H^{1}(\mathfrak{X}_{\alpha,I},(\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\alpha,I}})^{\times})\xrightarrow{k}H^{1}(\mathfrak{X}_{\alpha,I},\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\alpha,I}}).

2.2.1.
The Shimura variety associated to
G

Let
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G)
be the Shimura variety associated to the group
G=\Res_{L/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbf{GL}_{2,L}
and level subgroup
K=K_{1}(N)
,

K_{1}(N)=\left\{\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\mathbf{GL}_{2}(\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{L})\,|\,d\equiv 1,c\equiv 0\textrm{ mod }N\right\}

whose complex points are
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G)(\mathbb{C})=G(\mathbb{Q})\backslash(\mathfrak{h}^{\pm})^{\Sigma}\times G(\mathbb{A}^{\infty})/K
. Here
\mathfrak{h}
is the Poincaré upper-half plane endowed with the usual action of
\mathbf{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
via Möbius transformation. The Shimura variety
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G)
is defined over its reflex field
\mathbb{Q}
.

The Shimura variety
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G)
classifies abelian varieties with real multiplication upto isogeny in the following sense. Let
\mathbf{A}_{L}^{\mathbb{Q}}
be the fibred category over
\mathbb{Q}
-
\mathbf{Sch}
, whose objects are abelian varieties with real multiplication by
\mathcal{O}_{L}
, and whose morphisms are
\Hom_{F}^{\mathbb{Q}}(A,A^{\prime})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{L}}(A,A^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
. Then
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G)(\mathbb{C})
classifies triples
(A,{\lambda},\eta K)
upto isomorphism, where
A
is an abelian variety with real multiplication by
\mathcal{O}_{L}
,
{\lambda}
is an
\mathcal{O}_{L}
-linear polarization
\lambda\colon A\to A^{\vee}
, and
\eta K
is the
K
-orbit of a trivialization
\eta\colon(\mathbb{A}_{L}^{\infty})^{2}\xrightarrow{\sim}H_{1}(A,\mathbb{A}^{\infty})
of the rational Tate module
[
Hid04
, 133]
. Two objects
(A,\lambda,\eta K)
and
(A^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime},\eta^{\prime}K)
are isomorphic if
A\simeq A^{\prime}
in
\mathbf{A}^{\mathbb{Q}}_{L}
, with the isomorphism taking
\lambda
to an
F^{\times}_{+}
-multiple of
\lambda^{\prime}
, and
\eta K
to
\eta^{\prime}K
. The isomorphism class
[(A,\bar{\lambda},\eta K)]
of a triple consists of special representatives
(A_{0},{\lambda},\eta K)
for which
\eta(\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{2}_{L})=H_{1}(A_{0},\hat{\mathbb{Z}})=\prod_{\ell}T_{\ell}(A_{0})(\mathbb{C})
. The complex variety
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G)(\mathbb{C})
can then be viewed as classifying isomorphism classes of triples
(A,\bar{\lambda},\eta)
, where
A
is a complex abelian variety with real multiplication by
\mathcal{O}_{L}
,
\bar{\lambda}
is the
\mathcal{O}^{\times,+}_{L}
-class of a polarization
\lambda\colon A\to A^{\vee}
, and
\eta\colon\mu_{N}\otimes\mathfrak{d}^{-1}\xhookrightarrow{}A[N]
is a
\Gamma_{1}(N)
-level structure. An isomorphism of triples
(A,\bar{\lambda},\eta)\simeq(A^{\prime},\bar{\lambda}^{\prime},\eta^{\prime})
is induced by an actual isomorphism
A\simeq A^{\prime}
of abelian varieties (as opposed to an isogeny).

Concretely, corresponding to the point
[(\tau,g)]\in G(\mathbb{Q})\backslash(\mathfrak{h}^{\pm})^{\Sigma}\times G(\mathbb{A}^{\infty})/K
, one gets the triple
(A,\bar{\lambda},\eta)
as follows. To give the abelian variety
A
, it’s enough to give its period lattice
H_{1}(A,\mathbb{Z})\subset\mathbb{C}^{\Sigma}
. Let
H_{1}(A,\mathbb{Q})=L\cdot\tau+L\cdot 1
, i.e. we choose a basis
a\colon H_{1}(A,\mathbb{Q})\to L^{2}
, where
a(\tau)=e_{1}
, and
a(1)=e_{2}
. The element
g\in\mathbf{GL}_{2}{(\mathbb{A}_{L}^{\infty})}
induces an isomorphism

a^{-1}\circ(g^{t})^{-1}\colon(\mathbb{A}_{L}^{\infty})^{2}\xrightarrow{(g^{t})^{-1}}(\mathbb{A}_{L}^{\infty})^{2}\xrightarrow{a^{-1}}H_{1}(A,\mathbb{Q})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{A}^{\infty}.

The period lattice is then given by
H_{1}(A,\mathbb{Z})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=H_{1}(A,\mathbb{Q})\cap a^{-1}\circ(g^{t})^{-1}(\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{2}_{L})
. The class of
a^{-1}\circ(g^{t})^{-1}(e_{2})\mod N\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{L}
determines the level structure
\eta
. Finally, the polarization module
(\ker{\lambda})^{-1}
is given by the fractional ideal
[\det(g)]\mathfrak{d}^{-1}
, where
[\det(g)]
is the ideal generated by
\det(g)
.

The determinant map
\det\colon G\to\Res_{L/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}
gives a bijection between the set of geometrically connected components of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G)
and the strict class group
\mathrm{Cl}^{+}(L)

[
TX16
, §2.3]
. For any fractional ideal
\mathfrak{c}
coprime to
p
, let
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathfrak{c}}_{K}(G)
be the connected component of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G)
corresponding to the class of
\mathfrak{cd}
. This space is related to the moduli of
\mathfrak{c}
-polarized abelian varieties in the following manner.

The moduli scheme
M(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})
is defined over
\mathbb{Z}[1/N]
. Consider the action of
\mathcal{O}_{L}^{\times,+}
on
M(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})
defined on points by
\epsilon\cdot(A,\iota,\lambda,\psi)=(A,\iota,\epsilon\lambda,\psi)
. Notice that for
\epsilon=\eta^{2}
, with
\eta\in U_{N}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=1+N\mathcal{O}_{L}
, the isomorphism
\eta\colon A\to A
induces an isomorphism
\epsilon\cdot(A,\iota,\lambda,\psi)=\eta^{*}(A,\iota,\lambda,\psi)\simeq(A,\iota,\lambda,\psi)
. Thus the action of
\mathcal{O}_{L}^{\times,+}
factors through the finite quotient
\Gamma\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathcal{O}_{L}^{\times,+}/U_{N}^{2}
. We have then the following proposition.

Proposition 2.4
.

There exists an isomorphism between the quotient
M(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})(\mathbb{C})/\Gamma
and
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathfrak{c}}_{K}(G)(\mathbb{C})
. In other words
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathfrak{c}}_{K}(G)(\mathbb{C})
is a coarse moduli space over
\mathbb{C}
of
\mathfrak{c}
-polarized abelian varieties with real multiplication by
\mathcal{O}_{L}
. Moreover the quotient map
p\colon\bar{M}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})\to\bar{M}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})/\Gamma
is finite étale with Galois group
\Gamma
.

Proof.

For the first part see
[
TX16
, Proposition 2.4]
. For the étaleness of the quotient see
[
AIP16a
, Lemma 8.1]
.
∎

Choosing a set of representatives and letting
\mathbf{GL}_{2}(L)_{+}
the subgroup of matrices with totally positive determinant, one can write

\mathbf{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{L}^{\infty})=\bigsqcup_{[\mathfrak{c}]\in\mathrm{Cl}^{+}(L)}\mathbf{GL}_{2}(L)_{+}\begin{pmatrix}\mathfrak{c}&0\\
0&\mathfrak{d}\end{pmatrix}K_{1}(N).

Let
x_{\mathfrak{c}}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathfrak{c}&0\\
0&\mathfrak{d}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
. Let
\Gamma_{1}(\mathfrak{c},N)=x_{\mathfrak{c}}K_{1}(N)x_{\mathfrak{c}}^{-1}\cap\mathbf{GL}_{2}(L)_{+}
, and let
\Gamma_{1}^{1}(\mathfrak{c},N)=x_{\mathfrak{c}}K_{1}(N)x_{\mathfrak{c}}^{-1}\cap\mathbf{SL}_{2}(L)
. Then one can identify
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathfrak{c}}_{K}(G)(\mathbb{C})=\Gamma_{1}(\mathfrak{c},N)\backslash\mathfrak{h}^{g}
, and
M(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})(\mathbb{C})=\Gamma_{1}^{1}(\mathfrak{c},N)\backslash\mathfrak{h}^{g}
, where the action of the congruence subgroups on
\mathfrak{h}^{g}
is via the usual Möbius action.

The map
\rho_{\epsilon}\colon M(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})(\mathbb{C})\to M(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})(\mathbb{C})
sending
(A,\iota,\lambda,\psi)\mapsto(A,\iota,\epsilon\lambda,\psi)
corresponds to
\tau\mapsto\epsilon^{-1}\tau
for any
\tau\in\mathfrak{h}^{g}
.

2.2.2.
Hilbert modular forms for the group
G

Associated to a weight
(v,w)\in\mathbb{Z}[\Sigma]\times\mathbb{Z}
we define an integral model for the sheaf of Hilbert modular forms of weight
(v,w)
as follows. Let
L^{\textrm{Gal}}
be the Galois closure of
L
. Let
R
be an
\mathcal{O}^{\textrm{Gal}}_{L,(p)}
-algebra. Let
t_{L}=\sum_{\tau\in\Sigma}\tau
. For
k=2v+wt_{L}
, consider the sheaf
\omega^{(k,v)}_{\mathcal{A}}=\omega^{k}_{\mathcal{A}}\otimes(\wedge^{2}\mathrm{H}_{\mathcal{A}})^{-v}
on
\bar{M}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})_{R}
. Here the wedge product is taken as
\mathcal{O}_{\bar{M}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})_{R}}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{L}
-modules. The sheaf
\wedge^{2}\mathrm{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
is canonically isomorphic to
\mathcal{O}_{\bar{M}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})_{R}}\otimes\mathfrak{c}{\mathfrak{d}}^{-1}
on
\bar{M}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})_{R}
, as can be seen from the Hodge filtration

0\to\omega_{\mathcal{A}}\to\mathrm{H}_{\mathcal{A}}\to\omega_{\mathcal{A}}^{\vee}\otimes\mathfrak{c}\to 0.

Over
\mathbb{C}
, this canonical basis of
\wedge^{2}\mathrm{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
corresponds to the canonical ordered basis
(\tau,1)
of
H_{1}(A_{\tau},\mathbb{Z})
, with
A_{\tau}=\mathbb{C}^{g}/\mathfrak{c}^{-1}\cdot\tau+\mathfrak{d^{-1}}
, for any
\tau\in\mathfrak{h}^{g}
, under the Betti–de Rham isomorphism. For any
\epsilon\in\mathcal{O}_{L}^{\times,+}
, the map
\rho_{\epsilon}\colon\bar{M}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})_{\mathbb{C}}\to\bar{M}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})_{\mathbb{C}}
, sending
(A,\iota,\lambda,\psi)\mapsto(A,\iota,\epsilon\lambda,\psi)
induces a natural map
\rho_{\epsilon}^{*}\colon\wedge^{2}\mathrm{H}_{\mathcal{A}}\to\wedge^{2}\mathrm{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
via pullback. If
h_{\text{can}}
is the canonical basis of
\wedge^{2}\mathrm{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
, then
\rho_{\epsilon}^{*}h_{\text{can}}=\epsilon h_{\text{can}}
. This gives an action of
\mathcal{O}_{L}^{\times,+}
on
\omega_{\mathcal{A}}^{(k,v)}
which factors through
\Gamma
. We want to define the sheaf of Hilbert modular forms for the group
G
to be the invariants
(p_{*}\omega_{\mathcal{A}}^{(k,v)})^{\Gamma}
under this action, with
p\colon\bar{M}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})_{R}\to\bar{M}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})_{R}/\Gamma
the quotient map.

Using the trivialization of
\wedge^{2}\mathrm{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
, the sheaves
\omega_{\mathcal{A}}^{(k,v)}
and
\omega_{\mathcal{A}}^{k}
are isomorphic as
\mathcal{O}_{\bar{M}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})_{R}}
-modules. Define an action of
\mathcal{O}_{L}^{\times,+}
on
\omega^{k}_{\mathcal{A}}
as follows. For a section
f
of
\omega^{k}_{\mathcal{A}}
, define
\epsilon\cdot f
as the section whose evaluation at points
(A_{/R},\iota,\lambda,\psi,\omega)
for an
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes R
-generator
\omega
of
\omega_{\mathcal{A}}
satisfies

(\epsilon\cdot f)(A,\iota,\lambda,\psi,\omega)=\epsilon^{-v}f(A,\iota,\epsilon\lambda,\psi,\omega).

We can then check that the action of
U_{N}^{2}
is trivial on
\omega^{k}_{\mathcal{A}}
. Indeed, for
\eta\in U_{N}
and
\epsilon=\eta^{2}
we have

f(A,\iota,\lambda,\psi,\omega)=f(A,\iota,\epsilon\lambda,\psi,\eta\omega)=\eta^{-k}f(A,\iota,\epsilon\lambda,\psi,\omega)=\epsilon^{-v}f(A,\iota,\epsilon\lambda,\psi,\omega)

which proves the claim. We remark that this action only depends on
k
and not the pair
(v,w)
.

Definition 2.5
.

Define the sheaf of Hilbert modular forms for the group
G
of tame level
\mu_{N}
,
\mathfrak{c}
-polarization and weight
(v,w)
with coefficients in
R
to be

\underline{\omega}_{R}^{(v,w)}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(p_{*}\omega^{k}_{\mathcal{A},R})^{\Gamma}

where
p\colon\bar{M}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})_{R}\to\bar{M}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})_{R}/\Gamma
is the quotient map. Alternatively we will sometimes call them arithmetic Hilbert modular forms.

Let
\mathrm{M}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c},k;R)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=H^{0}(\bar{M}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})_{R},\omega^{k}_{\mathcal{A}})
be the space of geometric Hilbert modular forms. Let
\mathrm{M}^{G}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c},(v,w);R)=H^{0}(\bar{M}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})_{R}/\Gamma,\underline{\omega}_{R}^{(v,w)})
be the space of arithmetic Hilbert modular forms. If
\#\Gamma\in R^{\times}
, then
\mathrm{M}^{G}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c},(v,w);R)=\mathrm{M}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c},k=2v+wt_{L};R)^{\Gamma}
can be realized as the image of the projector:

e\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{1}{\#\Gamma}\sum_{\epsilon\in\Gamma}\epsilon.

Let
\textrm{Frac}(L)^{(p)}
be the group of fractional ideals which are coprime to
p
and let
\textrm{Princ}(L)^{+,(p)}
be the group of principal ideals generated by totally positive elements which are coprime to
p
. Then we have
\textrm{Frac}(L)^{(p)}/\textrm{Princ}(L)^{+,(p)}\simeq\mathrm{Cl}^{+}(L)
. Let
x\in L^{\times,+}
be coprime to
p
. The map
(A,\iota,\lambda,\psi)\mapsto(A,\iota,x\lambda,\psi)
induces a natural isomorphism
\rho_{(x,\mathfrak{c})}\colon\bar{M}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})_{R}\to\bar{M}(\mu_{N},x\mathfrak{c})_{R}
. Over
\mathbb{C}
, this induces an isomorphism
\rho_{(x,\mathfrak{c})}\colon\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathfrak{c}}_{K}(G)(\mathbb{C})\to\mathrm{Sh}^{x\mathfrak{c}}_{K}(G)(\mathbb{C})
, where

\displaystyle\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathfrak{c}}_{K}(G)(\mathbb{C})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{GL}_{2}(L)_{+}\backslash\mathfrak{h}^{g}\times\mathbf{GL}_{2}(L)_{+}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathfrak{c}&0\\
0&\mathfrak{d}\end{smallmatrix}\right)K_{1}(N)/K_{1}(N)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Sh}^{x\mathfrak{c}}_{K}(G)(\mathbb{C})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{GL}_{2}(L)_{+}\backslash\mathfrak{h}^{g}\times\mathbf{GL}_{2}(L)_{+}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}x\mathfrak{c}&0\\
0&\mathfrak{d}\end{smallmatrix}\right)K_{1}(N)/K_{1}(N).

This isomorphism is given by
\left[\left(\tau,\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathfrak{c}&0\\
0&\mathfrak{d}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)\right]\mapsto\left[\left(x\tau,\left(\begin{smallmatrix}x\mathfrak{c}&0\\
0&\mathfrak{d}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)\right]
. The Shimura variety
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G)(\mathbb{C})
can then be written as

\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G)(\mathbb{C})=\left(\bigsqcup_{\mathfrak{c}\in\text{Frac}(L)^{(p)}}\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathfrak{c}}_{K}(G)(\mathbb{C})\right)/\text{Princ}(L)^{+,(p)}.

Let
\omega_{\mathcal{A}}^{(k,v)}(\mathfrak{c})=\omega_{\mathcal{A}}^{k}\otimes(\wedge^{2}\mathrm{H}_{\mathcal{A}})^{-v}
on
\bar{M}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})_{\mathbb{C}}
. Then the identity

[1]\colon A_{\tau,\mathfrak{c}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{\mathbb{C}^{g}}{\mathfrak{c}^{-1}\cdot\tau+\mathfrak{d^{-1}}}\to\frac{\mathbb{C}^{g}}{x^{-1}\mathfrak{c}^{-1}\cdot x\tau+\mathfrak{d^{-1}}}=\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}A_{x\tau,x\mathfrak{c}}

induces a map
[1]^{*}\colon\rho^{*}_{(x,\mathfrak{c})}\omega_{\mathcal{A}}^{(k,v)}(x\mathfrak{c})\to\omega_{\mathcal{A}}^{(k,v)}(\mathfrak{c})
via pullback. The canonical differential
\mathrm{d}\underline{z}
of
\omega_{A_{x\tau,x\mathfrak{c}}}
maps to the section
\mathrm{d}\underline{z}
of
\omega_{A_{\tau,\mathfrak{c}}}
, and the canonical basis
h_{x\tau,x\mathfrak{c}}
of
\wedge^{2}\mathrm{H}_{A_{x\tau,x\mathfrak{c}}}
maps to the section
x^{-1}h_{\tau,\mathfrak{c}}
of
\wedge^{2}\mathrm{H}_{A_{\tau,\mathfrak{c}}}
. In particular,

[1]^{*}(\mathrm{d}\underline{z}^{k}\otimes h_{x\tau,x\mathfrak{c}}^{-v})=x^{v}(\mathrm{d}\underline{z}^{k}\otimes h_{\tau,\mathfrak{c}}^{-v}).

This motivates the following definition. Consider the isomorphism
L_{(x\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{c})}\colon\mathrm{M}(\mu_{N},x\mathfrak{c},k;R)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{M}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c},k;R)
given by

L_{(x\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{c})}(f)(A,\iota,\lambda,\psi,\omega)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=x^{v}f(A,\iota,x\lambda,\psi,\omega).

This map descends to an isomorphism
L_{(x\mathfrak{c},x\mathfrak{c})}\colon\mathrm{M}^{G}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c},(v,w);R)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{M}^{G}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c},(v,w);R)
.

Definition 2.6
.

Define the
R
-module of Hilbert modular forms for
G
of tame level
N
and weight
(v,w)
to be

\mathrm{M}^{G}(\mu_{N},(v,w);R)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\left(\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{c}\in\textrm{Frac}(L)^{(p)}}\mathrm{M}^{G}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c},(v,w);R)\right)/\left(L_{(x\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{c})}(f)-f\right)_{x\in\textrm{Princ}(L)^{+,(p)}}.

Upon choosing representatives
\mathfrak{c}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{c}_{h^{+}_{L}}
of
\mathrm{Cl}^{+}(L)
in
\textrm{Frac}(L)^{(p)}
we have a non-canonical isomorphism

\mathrm{M}^{G}(\mu_{N},(v,w);R)\simeq\bigoplus_{i=1}^{h^{+}_{L}}\mathrm{M}^{G}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c}_{i},(v,w);R)

which shows that
\mathrm{M}^{G}(\mu_{N},(v,w);R)
is a finite
R
-module.

Remark 2.7
.

For
\mathfrak{c}=\mathfrak{d}^{-1}
, the moduli scheme
M(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{d}^{-1})
is an integral model for the Shimura variety associated to the group
G^{*}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=G\times_{\Res_{L/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}}\mathbb{G}_{m}
where the arrow
G\to\Res_{L/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}
is the determinant and
\mathbb{G}_{m}\to\Res_{L/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}
is the diagonal embedding
[
Rap78
]
.

2.2.3.
Adelic Hilbert modular forms

Let
f\in\mathrm{M}^{G}(\mu_{N},(v,w);\mathbb{C})=(f_{\mathfrak{c}_{i}})_{i=1}^{h^{+}_{L}}
be a Hilbert modular form as above. Then we can define an automorphic form
\phi_{f}\colon\mathbf{GL}_{2}(L)\backslash\mathbf{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{L})/K_{1}(N)\to\mathbb{C}
corresponding to
f
as

\phi_{f}\left(u_{\infty},\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathfrak{c}_{i}&0\\
0&\mathfrak{d}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)=f_{\mathfrak{c}_{i}}(u_{\infty}\cdot\mathbf{i})j_{k,v}(u_{\infty},\mathbf{i})^{-1}

where
u_{\infty}\in\mathbf{GL}_{2}(L\otimes\mathbb{R})_{+}
,
\mathbf{i}=(i,\dots,i)\in\mathbb{C}^{g}
, and for
\gamma=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\mathbf{GL}_{2}(L\otimes\mathbb{R})
,
j_{k,v}(\gamma,z)=(ad-bc)^{-v}(cz+d)^{k}
.

The function
\phi_{f}
satisfies the following properties:
[
BF20
, §2.1]

•

For every finite adelic point
x\in\mathbf{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{L}^{\infty})
, the well-defined function
f_{x}\colon\mathfrak{h}^{g}\to\mathbb{C}
given by
f_{x}(z)=\phi_{f}(u_{\infty}x)j_{k,v}(u_{\infty},\mathbf{i})
is holomorphic, where for each
z\in\mathfrak{h}^{g}
, we choose
u_{\infty}\in\mathbf{GL}_{2}(L\otimes\mathbb{R})_{+}
such that
u_{\infty}\cdot\mathbf{i}=z
.

•

For all adelic points
x\in\mathbf{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{L})
, and for all additive measures on
L\backslash\mathbb{A}_{L}
, we have

\int_{L\backslash\mathbb{A}_{L}}\phi_{f}\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&a\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)x\right)da=0.

Let
\phi_{f}
be an automorphic form as above. Choosing a set of representatives

t_{i}=\begin{pmatrix}c_{i}^{-1}&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},\quad i=1,\dots,h_{L}^{+}

for
\mathbf{GL}_{2}(L)_{+}\backslash\mathbf{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{L})/K_{1}(N)
, we get modular forms
f_{i}\colon\mathfrak{h}^{g}\to\mathbb{C}
defined as

f_{i}(z)=\phi_{f}(u_{\infty}t_{i})j_{k,v}(u_{\infty},\mathbf{i}).

Each
f_{i}
is a Hilbert modular form of
\mathfrak{c}_{i}\mathfrak{d}^{-1}
-polarization in the sense of Definition
2.5
, where
\mathfrak{c}_{i}
is the ideal generated by
c_{i}
. The modular form
f_{i}
has a Fourier expansion, which is the same as the
q
-expansion associated to the Tate object
\text{Tate}_{\mathfrak{d}^{-1},\mathfrak{c}_{i}}(q)=\mathbb{G}_{m}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{L}/\underline{q}(\mathfrak{c}_{i})
(see §
5.3
) with level structure being given by the point
\exp(2\pi i/N)\otimes 1\in\mathbb{G}_{m}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{L}/\underline{q}(\mathfrak{c}_{i})
.

f_{i}(z)=\sum_{\xi\in(\mathfrak{c}_{i}\mathfrak{d}^{-1})_{+}}a(\xi,f_{i})e_{L}(\xi z),\quad e_{L}(\xi z)=\exp(2\pi i\sum_{\tau\in\Sigma}\tau(\xi)z_{\tau}).

Fix a finite extension
L_{0}
of
L^{\textrm{Gal}}
such that for any ideal
\mathfrak{a}\subset\mathcal{O}_{L}
, and any embedding
\tau\in\Sigma
,
\mathfrak{a}^{\tau}\mathcal{O}_{L_{0}}
is principal. Choose a generator
\{\mathfrak{q}^{\tau}\}\in\mathcal{O}_{L_{0}}
of
\mathfrak{q}^{\tau}\mathcal{O}_{L_{0}}
for each prime ideal
\mathfrak{q}
, and extend it to all fractional ideals multiplicatively. Fix an idele
\mathrm{d}\in\mathbb{A}_{L}^{\infty,\times}
whose ideal is the different
\mathfrak{d}
of
L/\mathbb{Q}
.

Every idele
y\in\mathbb{A}_{L,+}^{\times}=\mathbb{A}_{L}^{\infty,\times}L_{\infty,+}^{\times}
can be written as
y=\xi c_{i}^{-1}\mathrm{d}u
for
\xi\in L_{+}^{\times}
and
u\in\det{K_{1}({N})}L_{\infty,+}^{\times}
. Define two functions
c(\cdot,\phi_{f})\colon\mathbb{A}_{L,+}^{\times}\to\mathbb{C}
and
c_{p}(\cdot,\phi_{f})\colon\mathbb{A}_{L,+}^{\times}\to\bar{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
as follows.

c(y,\phi_{f})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=a(\xi,f_{i})\{y^{v-t_{L}}\}\xi^{t_{L}-v}|c_{i}|_{\mathbb{A}_{L}}\quad\quad c_{p}(y,\phi_{f})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=a(\xi,f_{i})y_{p}^{v-t_{L}}\xi^{t_{L}-v}\mathcal{N}_{L}(c_{i})^{-1}

if
y\in\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{L}L_{\infty,+}^{\times}
and
0
otherwise. Here
\mathcal{N}_{L}
is defined by
y\mapsto y_{p}^{-t_{L}}|y^{\infty}|_{\mathbb{A}_{L}}^{-1}
. The function
c_{p}(\cdot,\phi_{f})
makes sense only if the coefficients
a(\xi,f_{i})
are algebraic for all
i
. Moreover, for our choice of the
\mathfrak{c}_{i}
as being coprime to
p
, we have

c_{p}(y,\phi_{f})=c(y,\phi_{f})\{y^{t_{L}-v}\}y_{p}^{v-t_{L}}.

Theorem 2.8
.

Consider the map
e_{L}\colon\mathbb{C}^{\Sigma}\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
defined by
e_{L}(z)=\exp(2\pi i\sum_{\tau\in\Sigma}z_{\tau})
and the unique additive character of the adeles
\chi_{L}\colon\mathbb{A}_{L}/L\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
which satisfies
\chi_{L}(x_{\infty})=e_{L}(x_{\infty})
. Each Hilbert cuspform of weight
(v,w)
has an adelic
q
-expansion of the form

\phi_{f}\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}y&x\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)=|y|_{\mathbb{A}_{L}}\sum_{\xi\in L_{+}}c(\xi y\mathrm{d},\phi_{f})\{(\xi y\mathrm{d})^{t_{L}-v}\}(\xi y_{\infty})^{v-t_{L}}e_{L}(\mathbf{i}\xi y_{\infty})\chi_{L}(\xi x)

for
y\in\mathbb{A}_{L,+}^{\times}
,
x\in\mathbb{A}_{L}^{\times}
, where
c(\cdot,\phi_{f})\colon\mathbb{A}_{L,+}^{\times}\to\mathbb{C}
vanishes outside
\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{L}L_{\infty,+}^{\times}
and depends only on the coset
y^{\infty}\det{K_{1}({N})}L_{\infty,+}^{\times}
. The adelic
q
-expansion agrees with the Fourier expansions of the
f_{i}
in the following sense.

\phi_{f}\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}y_{\infty}&x_{\infty}\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)t_{i}\right)=y_{\infty}^{v}\sum_{\xi\in(\mathfrak{c}_{i}\mathfrak{d}^{-1})_{+}}a(\xi,f_{i})e_{L}(\xi z).

Moreover, formally replacing
e_{L}(\mathbf{i}\xi y)\chi_{L}(\xi x)
by
q^{\xi}
, and
(\xi y_{\infty})^{v-t_{L}}
by
(\xi\mathrm{d}_{p}y_{p})^{v-t_{L}}
, we have the
q
-expansion

\phi_{f}=\mathcal{N}(y)^{-1}\sum_{\xi\in L_{+}}c_{p}(\xi y\mathrm{d},\phi_{f})q^{\xi}.

Proof.

[
Hid91
, Theorem 1.1]
.
∎

Atkin–Lehner involution.

Let
f\in\mathrm{M}^{G}(\mu_{N},(v,w);\mathbb{C})
be a modular form as above. The Atkin–Lehner involution is described in the language of adelic Hilbert modular forms as follows
[
Shi78
, 655]
. For any
2\times 2
matrix
x
, let
x^{\iota}
be the adjugate matrix, i.e.
xx^{\iota}=x^{\iota}x=\det{x}
.

Definition 2.9
.

The Atkin–Lehner involution
w_{N}
on the space
\mathrm{M}^{G}(\mu_{N},(v,w);\mathbb{C})
of Hilbert modular forms is an operator, such that if
\phi_{f}\colon\mathbf{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{L})\to\mathbb{C}
is a Hilbert modular form, then

(\phi_{f}|w_{N})(x)=\phi_{f}\left(x^{-\iota}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&-1\\
N&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right).

One can give a geometric definition of the Atkin–Lehner operator, which allows it to be defined for families of Hilbert modular forms.

Fix a base ring
R
that is an
\mathcal{O}_{L,(p)}^{\mathrm{Gal}}
algebra containing a primitive
N
-th root
\zeta_{N}
of
1
. Let
\mathfrak{c}
be an integral ideal coprime to
N
, and let
M(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c}^{-1}\mathfrak{d}^{-1})/\textrm{Spec}\hskip 1.00006pt{R}
be the Hilbert modular variety of
\mathfrak{c}^{-1}\mathfrak{d}^{-1}
-polarized abelian varieties over
R
. The Atkin–Lehner involution is induced via pullback by a morphism
w_{N}\colon M(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c}^{-1}\mathfrak{d}^{-1})\to M(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{cd}^{-1}N)
which is defined on points as follows.

Let
(A,\iota,\lambda,\psi)\in M(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c}^{-1}\mathfrak{d}^{-1})
. Suppose
P=\psi(1)\in A[N]
. Let
A^{\prime}=A\otimes\mathfrak{c}^{-1}/(P)
, i.e. the quotient of
A\otimes\mathfrak{c}^{-1}
by the cyclic subgroup generated by the image of
P
. We note that
\lambda
induces a
\mathfrak{cd}^{-1}N
-polarization
\lambda^{\prime}
on
A^{\prime}
. Since
A\otimes\mathfrak{c}^{-1}=A^{\vee}\otimes\mathfrak{d}
, the Weil pairing

A[N]\xrightarrow{\sim}\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{L}}(A^{\vee}[N],\mu_{N}\otimes\mathfrak{d}^{-1})=\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{L}}(A^{\vee}\otimes\mathfrak{d}[N],\mu_{N}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{L})

defines a point
P^{\vee}\in A^{\vee}\otimes\mathfrak{d}[N]
, such that
\langle P,P^{\vee}\rangle=\zeta_{N}\otimes 1\in\mu_{N}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{L}
. Then
P^{\vee}
defines a level
N
structure
\psi^{\prime}
on
A^{\prime}
, which thus gives a point
(A^{\prime},\iota^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime},\psi^{\prime})\in M(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{cd}^{-1}N)
. Letting
\pi\colon A\to A^{\prime}
be the projection, the Atkin–Lehner operator is defined as

\displaystyle w_{N}\colon\mathrm{M}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{cd}^{-1}N,(v,w);R)

\displaystyle\to\mathrm{M}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c}^{-1}\mathfrak{d}^{-1},(v,w);R)

\displaystyle(f|w_{N})(A,\iota,\lambda,\psi,\omega)

\displaystyle=f(A^{\prime},\iota^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime},\psi^{\prime},(\pi^{*})^{-1}\omega).

This operator commutes with the action of
\Gamma
and
\text{Princ}(L)^{+,(p)}
, and thus defines an operator
w_{N}
on
\mathrm{M}^{G}(\mu_{N},(v,w);R)
.

Theorem 2.10
.

If
f\in\mathrm{M}^{G}(\mu_{N},(v,w);\mathbb{C})
is a primitive newform, then
f|w_{N}
is a constant times
f^{*}
where
f^{*}
is the modular form whose adelic
q
-expansion coefficients are the complex conjugates of
f
.

Proof.

[
Shi78
, Proposition 2.10]
.
∎

2.3.
The partial Igusa tower

Fix an
I=[p^{a},p^{b}]
and
\alpha\in\mathfrak{m}\setminus\mathfrak{m}^{2}
. We now work over
\mathfrak{X}_{\alpha,I}
. Recall the ideal
\mathrm{Hdg}_{r}
was given locally by
(\alpha,\mathrm{Hdg}^{p^{r+1}})
for a local lift
\mathrm{Hdg}
of the Hasse invariant. Let
g_{r}\colon\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}\to\mathfrak{X}_{\alpha,I}
be the open subscheme of the blow-up of
\mathfrak{X}_{\alpha,I}
with respect to the ideal
\mathrm{Hdg}_{r}
, where the inverse image ideal is generated by
\mathrm{Hdg}^{p^{r+1}}
. For any integer
n
with
1\leq n\leq r
if
I=[0,1]
and
1\leq n\leq a+r
let
\lambda=\mathrm{Hdg}^{\frac{p^{n}-1}{p-1}}
. Note that
\frac{p}{\lambda}\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}}
.

Proposition 2.11
.

For
I,r,n,\alpha
as above the semiabelian scheme
\mathcal{A}\to\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
has a canonical subgroup
H_{n}
of order
p^{n}

[
AIP18
, Appendice A]
. This is a finite, locally free subgroup scheme that satisfies the following properties:

(1)

H_{n}
lifts
\ker{F^{n}}
modulo
\frac{p}{\lambda}
.

(2)

For any
\alpha
-adically complete admissible
\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}
-algbera
R
, together with a morphism
f\colon\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}\to\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
,

H_{n}(R)=\{s\in\mathcal{A}[p^{n}](R)\ |\ s\ \mathrm{mod}\ \frac{p}{\lambda}\in\ker{F^{n}}\}.

(3)

Suppose
L_{n}=\mathcal{A}[p^{n}]/H_{n}
. Then
\omega_{L_{n}}
is killed by
\lambda
, and we have
\omega_{L_{n}}\simeq\omega_{\mathcal{A}}/\lambda\omega_{\mathcal{A}}
.

(4)

\mathcal{A}^{\vee}[p^{n}]/H_{n}(\mathcal{A}^{\vee})\simeq H_{n}^{\vee}
through the Weil pairing and it is étale over the adic generic fibre
\mathcal{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
of
\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
.

Proof.

[
AIP18
, Appendice A]
.
∎

Definition 2.12
.

For every
r,n
as above define
\mathcal{IG}_{n,r,I}\to\mathcal{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
to be the adic space classifying isomorphisms
\mathcal{O}_{L}/p^{n}\mathcal{O}_{L}\xrightarrow{\sim}H_{n}^{\vee}
of the group scheme
H_{n}^{\vee}\to\mathcal{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
. Define
\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}\to\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
to be the normalization of
\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
in
\mathcal{IG}_{n,r,I}
.

Proposition 2.13
.

\mathcal{IG}_{n,r,I}\to\mathcal{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
is an étale, Galois morphism with Galois group
(\mathcal{O}_{L}/p^{n}\mathcal{O}_{L})^{\times}
. The morphism
\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}\to\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
is finite and is endowed with an action of
(\mathcal{O}_{L}/p^{n}\mathcal{O}_{L})^{\times}
induced by the action on the generic fibre.

Proof.

[
AIP16a
, §3.3.1]
.
∎

(Note we suppressed the index
\alpha
in our notation for the partial Igusa tower to avoid clumsiness.)

3.
Splitting of de Rham sheaf

In the following we put an overline on the names of objects (abelian schemes, sheaves etc.) to denote they are obtained by base change along the closed immersion
i\colon X_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\xhookrightarrow{}\mathfrak{X}
. Denote by
\bar{\pi}\colon\bar{\mathcal{A}}\to X_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}
the base change of
\pi
along
i
. Let
\bar{\omega}_{\mathcal{A}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=i^{*}\omega_{\mathcal{A}}=\bar{\omega}_{\mathcal{A}}
. The Verschiebung
V\colon\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{(p)}\to\bar{\mathcal{A}}
induces a map on the Lie algebra
HW\colon\omega^{\vee}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{(p)}}\to{\omega}^{\vee}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}}
, whose determinant is the Hasse invariant
\mathrm{Ha}\in(\Lambda^{g}\bar{\omega}_{\mathcal{A}})^{\otimes(p-1)}
. Let
\underline{\mathrm{Ha}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathrm{Ha}\cdot(\Lambda^{g}\bar{\omega}_{\mathcal{A}})^{\otimes(1-p)}
be the ideal generated by the values of
\mathrm{Ha}
. This is an invertible ideal with zeroes of order
1
along each of the prime divisors that appear in
\mathrm{Div}(\underline{\mathrm{Ha}})
(Theorem
2.3
). Denote by
\bar{\mathrm{H}}_{\mathcal{A}}
the pullback of
\mathrm{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
along
i
. Let
j\colon X_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}^{\textrm{ord}}\xhookrightarrow{}X_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}
be the ordinary locus, which is the open subscheme of
X_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}
where
\underline{\mathrm{Ha}}=\mathcal{O}_{X_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}}
.

Let
\varphi\colon X_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\to X_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}
be the Frobenius. The Frobenius induces a
\varphi
-linear endomorphism of
\bar{\mathrm{H}}_{\mathcal{A}}
.

Proposition 3.1
.

Over the ordinary locus
X^{\textrm{ord}}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}
we have the unit root splitting which is a canonical splitting
\psi_{\textrm{Frob}}\colon j^{*}\bar{\mathrm{H}}_{\mathcal{A}}\to j^{*}\bar{\omega}_{\mathcal{A}}
of the Hodge filtration on
\bar{\mathrm{H}}_{\mathcal{A}}
, that respects the Frobenius action. The kernel of
\psi_{\textrm{Frob}}
is called the unit root subspace. It is characterized by the property that it is stable under the Frobenius action and Frobenius acts invertibly on it.

Proof.

Suppose
\textrm{Spec}\hskip 1.00006pt{R}\subset X_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}
is a local chart for which
\bar{\omega}_{\mathcal{A}},\bar{\mathrm{H}}_{\mathcal{A}}
are trivial and choose a basis compatible with the Hodge filtration. With respect to such a basis we can write the matrix of the Frobenius action on
\bar{\mathrm{H}}_{\mathcal{A}}
in
g\times g
blocks as follows.

\textrm{Frob}=\begin{pmatrix}0&&C\\
0&&HW.\end{pmatrix}

Here we abuse notation to write
HW
for the matrix corresponding to the
\varphi
-linear map induced on
\bar{\omega}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\vee}
by
\mathrm{F}_{\textrm{abs}}\colon\bar{\mathcal{A}}\to\bar{\mathcal{A}}
. For the base change of
j_{*}j^{*}\bar{\mathrm{H}}_{\mathcal{A}}(R)=\bar{\mathrm{H}}_{\mathcal{A}}(R)[1/\mathrm{Ha}]
given by the matrix

P=\begin{pmatrix}\mathrm{Id}&&C\cdot HW^{-1}\\
0&&\mathrm{Id}\end{pmatrix},

the matrix of Frobenius becomes

P^{-1}\textrm{Frob}P=\begin{pmatrix}0&&0\\
0&&HW\end{pmatrix}

Note that
P
is only defined over the ordinary locus. Hence we have a splitting
\psi_{\textrm{Frob}}\colon j^{*}\bar{\mathrm{H}}_{\mathcal{A}}\to j^{*}\bar{\omega}_{\mathcal{A}}
of the Hodge filtration over the ordinary locus that respects the Frobenius action. The kernel of this splitting is
uniquely
characterized by the fact that it is stable under the Frobenius action and Frobenius acts invertibly on it.
∎

Consider the map
\psi\colon\bar{\mathrm{H}}_{\mathcal{A}}\to j_{*}j^{*}\bar{\mathrm{H}}_{\mathcal{A}}\xrightarrow{\psi_{\textrm{Frob}}}j_{*}j^{*}\bar{\omega}_{\mathcal{A}}
. Then let
\bar{\mathrm{H}}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{A}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\psi^{-1}\bar{\omega}_{\mathcal{A}}
. The inclusion
\bar{\omega}_{\mathcal{A}}\to\bar{\mathrm{H}}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{A}}
admits a retraction given by the map
\psi
. As a subsheaf of
\bar{\mathrm{H}}_{\mathcal{A}}
containing
\bar{\omega}_{\mathcal{A}}
,
\bar{\mathrm{H}}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{A}}
is equipped with the induced Hodge filtration. In the following lemma we describe the 1st graded piece of this Hodge filtration.

Lemma 3.2
.

The sheaf
\bar{\mathrm{H}}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{A}}
sits in the following split exact sequence.

(2)

0\to\bar{\omega}_{\mathcal{A}}\to\bar{\mathrm{H}}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{A}}\to HW(\omega^{\vee}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{(p)}})\to 0.

Proof.

Choose a local chart
\textrm{Spec}\hskip 1.00006pt{R}
as above. Suppose
e_{1},\dots,e_{g},f_{1},\dots,f_{g}
form an
R
-basis of
\bar{\mathrm{H}}_{\mathcal{A}}
such that
e_{1},\dots,e_{g}
span
\bar{\omega}_{\mathcal{A}}
and the images of
f_{1},\dots,f_{g}
span
\bar{\omega}^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}}
. Assume that the matrix of Frobenius with respect to this basis is given as

\textrm{Frob}=\begin{pmatrix}0&&C\\
0&&HW.\end{pmatrix}

Then the matrix of
\psi_{\text{Frob}}
with respect to this basis is given by
\psi_{\text{Frob}}\colon R[1/\mathrm{Ha}]^{2g}\to R[1/\mathrm{Ha}]^{g}

\psi_{\text{Frob}}=\begin{pmatrix}1&-C\cdot HW^{-1}\end{pmatrix}.

Thus
\bar{\mathrm{H}}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{A}}
is given by all elements
(x_{1},\dots,x_{2g})^{t}
such that
(\begin{smallmatrix}1&-C\cdot HW^{-1}\end{smallmatrix})(x_{1},\dots,x_{2g})^{t}\in R^{g}
. In particular, we want to solve for
v=(x_{g+1},\dots,x_{2g})\in R^{g}
, such that
C\cdot HW^{-1}(v)\in R^{g}
. Letting
HW^{\iota}
be the matrix such that
HW\cdot HW^{\iota}=HW^{\iota}\cdot HW=\mathrm{Ha}
, we then have
C\cdot HW^{-1}(v)\in R^{g}\iff C\cdot HW^{\iota}\in\underline{\mathrm{Ha}}\cdot R^{g}
. Therefore,
HW^{\iota}(v)
lies in the kernel of
C\textrm{ mod }\underline{\mathrm{Ha}}
as well as in the kernel of
HW\textrm{ mod }\underline{\mathrm{Ha}}
. Thus in particular, denoting by
\textrm{Spec}\hskip 1.00006pt{k(y)}\to Y_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}
a generic point of a prime divisor of
\underline{\mathrm{Ha}}
,

\begin{pmatrix}0&C\\
0&HW\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}0\\
HW^{\iota}(v)\end{pmatrix}\otimes_{R}k(y)=0.

But the image of Frobenius over
k(y)
has rank
g
, and hence its kernel is precisely
\omega_{\bar{A}_{k(y)}}
. Thus
HW^{\iota}(v)\in\underline{\mathrm{Ha}}\cdot R^{g}
, implying
v\in HW(R^{g})
. This proves the lemma.
∎

Corollary 3.3
.

The sheaf
\bar{\mathrm{H}}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{A}}
is stable under the
\mathcal{O}_{L}
action. It is a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}}
-module of rank 2 and
\mathrm{H}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{A}}=\bar{\omega}_{\mathcal{A}}\oplus\textrm{Frob}(\bar{\mathrm{H}}_{\mathcal{A}})
.

Proof.

The subsheaf
\textrm{Frob}(\bar{\mathrm{H}}_{\mathcal{A}})
is the image of the map
F^{*}_{\mathcal{A}}\colon\mathrm{H}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{(p)}}\to\bar{\mathrm{H}}_{\mathcal{A}}
induced by the relative Frobenius. It is killed by the unit root splitting and maps surjectively onto
HW(\omega^{\vee}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{(p)}})
. Hence
\bar{\mathrm{H}}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{A}}=\bar{\omega}_{\mathcal{A}}\oplus\textrm{Frob}(\bar{\mathrm{H}}_{\mathcal{A}})
. Since the relative Frobenius commutes with the
\mathcal{O}_{L}
-action,
HW(\omega^{\vee}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{(p)}})
is stable under the
\mathcal{O}_{L}
