-action and hence so is
\bar{\mathrm{H}}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{A}}
. Moreover
HW\colon\omega^{\vee}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{(p)}}\to\bar{\omega}_{\mathcal{A}}
is an
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}}
-linear map of invertible
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}}
-modules such that
\mathrm{N}_{\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}}/\mathcal{O}_{X_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}}}HW=\det{HW}=\mathrm{Ha}
is a non-zero divisor. Hence
HW(\omega^{\vee}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{(p)}})
is an invertible
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}}
-module.
∎

In the following we will construct a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}}
subsheaf
\mathrm{H}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{A}}\subset\mathrm{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
of rank 2, together with the induced Hodge filtration such that its reduction modulo a small power of
p
will give us the split exact sequence (
2
).

Let
i\colon\mathfrak{X}_{\alpha,I}/(p)\xhookrightarrow{}\mathfrak{X}_{\alpha,I}
be the base change of
X_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\xhookrightarrow{}\mathfrak{X}
to
\mathfrak{X}_{\alpha,I}
. Let
i_{0}\colon\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}/(p\mathrm{Hdg}^{-1})\xhookrightarrow{}\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
be the closed subscheme defined by the ideal
\frac{p}{\mathrm{Hdg}}
. Thus we have a commutative diagram as follows.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}/(p\mathrm{Hdg}^{-1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{X}_{\alpha,I}/(p)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{X}_{\alpha,I}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}

Let
\bar{\omega}^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A},0}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=HW(\omega_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{(p)}})
where we now denote by
\bar{\mathcal{A}}
the pullback of
\mathcal{A}
along
i\colon\mathfrak{X}_{\alpha,I}/(p)\xhookrightarrow{}\mathfrak{X}_{\alpha,I}
. Let
\tilde{\omega}^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(i^{\sharp})^{-1}(i_{*}\bar{\omega}^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A},0})
where
i^{\sharp}\colon\omega^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}}\to i_{*}i^{*}\omega^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}}
is the unit of the adjunction. Note that
\tilde{\omega}^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}}
is stable under the
\mathcal{O}_{L}
-action.

Lemma 3.4
.

\omega^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A},0}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\im{(g_{r}^{*}{\tilde{\omega}^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}}}\to g_{r}^{*}\omega^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}})}
is a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}}
-module of rank
1
.

Proof.

Choose a local chart
\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}=U\subset\mathfrak{X}_{\alpha,I}
, such that
\omega^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}}
is free as an
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes R
-module over
\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}
, and
\underline{\mathrm{Ha}}
is free over
\textrm{Spec}\hskip 1.00006pt{R/(p)}
. Let
v
be an
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes R
basis of
{\omega^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}}}_{|U}
and let
(\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes R)\cdot v=\oplus_{i=1}^{g}Rv_{i}
be the decomposition induced by the splitting of
\mathcal{O}_{L}
in
R
. Let
\bar{v}
be the image of
v
in
\bar{\omega}^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=i^{*}{\omega}^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}}
. Let
\bar{w}=HW(\bar{v})
and pick a lift
w
of
\bar{w}
. Let
w=(w_{i})_{i}
be its components. Then
{\tilde{\omega}^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}}}=\sum_{i=1}^{g}\mathcal{O}_{U}w_{i}+p\omega^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}}
. Consider the
\mathcal{O}_{L}
-linear map
\widetilde{HW}
which sends
v\mapsto w
and which reduces to
HW
mod
p
. Then
\det{\widetilde{HW}}
is a lift of
\mathrm{Ha}=\det{HW}
. Keeping with previous notation, we will call this lift
\mathrm{Hdg}
. Consider now
V=g_{r}^{-1}(U)=\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R\langle\frac{\alpha}{\mathrm{Hdg}^{p^{r+1}}}}\rangle
. Using
\frac{p}{\mathrm{Hdg}}\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{r,I}}
, we see that over
\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R\langle\frac{\alpha}{\mathrm{Hdg}^{p^{r+1}}}\rangle}
,
p\cdot g_{r}^{*}\omega^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}}\subset\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{O}_{V}g_{r}^{*}(w_{i})
as submodules of
g_{r}^{*}\omega^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}}
. Thus
\omega^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A},0}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{O}_{V}g_{r}^{*}(w_{i})
. The sum is direct because
\mathrm{Hdg}
is a non-zero divisor in
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{r,I}}
. It is clearly stable under the
\mathcal{O}_{L}
-action. Since locally
HW
can be seen as a non-zero divisor in
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\alpha,I}/(p)}
, the same is true of the lift
\widetilde{HW}
, and since
\omega^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A},0}=\widetilde{HW}\cdot\omega^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}}
, the lemma follows.
∎

Following the proof of Lemma
3.4
, let
\widetilde{HW}
be the
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}}
-ideal sheaf defined by
\omega^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A},0}=\widetilde{HW}\cdot\omega^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}}
.

Definition 3.5
.

Define
\mathrm{H}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{A}}
to be the inverse image of
\omega^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A},0}
in
\mathrm{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
under the projection coming from the Hodge filtration.

We have the following commutative diagram of sheaves over
\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{\mathcal{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A},0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{\mathcal{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}_{\mathcal{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}

Proposition 3.6
.

There is an isomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}/(p\mathrm{Hdg}^{-1})}
-modules
i_{0}^{*}\omega^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A},0}\xrightarrow{\sim}q^{*}\bar{\omega}^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A},0}
, that commutes with the induced maps to
\bar{\omega}^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}}
. (We abuse notation to denote
q^{*}\bar{\omega}^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}}
by
\bar{\omega}^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}}
.)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{0}^{*}\omega^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A},0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{*}\bar{\omega}^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A},0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{\omega}^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{\omega}^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}

Proof.

In fact we prove the following. The natural surjective map
i^{*}{\tilde{\omega}^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}}}\to\bar{\omega}^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A},0}
induces by pullback a surjective map
q^{*}i^{*}{\tilde{\omega}^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}}}=i_{0}^{*}g_{r}^{*}{\tilde{\omega}^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}}}\to q^{*}\bar{\omega}^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A},0}
that commutes with the induced maps to
\bar{\omega}^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}}
. We will show that this map factors naturally as
i_{0}^{*}g_{r}^{*}{\tilde{\omega}^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}}}\to i_{0}^{*}\omega^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A},0}\to q^{*}\bar{\omega}^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A},0}
, and the last two sheaves being both locally free of rank
g
, the last arrow is an isomorphism. Also since
i_{0}^{*}g_{r}^{*}\tilde{\omega}^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}}\to q^{*}\bar{\omega}^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A},0}
is
\mathcal{O}_{L}
-linear and
i_{0}^{*}g_{r}^{*}\tilde{\omega}^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}}\to i_{0}^{*}\omega^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A},0}
is surjective
\mathcal{O}_{L}
-linear, the induced isomorphism is
\mathcal{O}_{L}
-linear too. This will be the isomorphism claimed in the proposition.

We use the notation of the proof of Lemma
3.4
, except that to avoid clumsiness we write
v_{i}
(resp.
w_{i}
) instead of
g_{r}^{*}(v_{i})
(resp.
g_{r}^{*}(w_{i})
). Since
{\tilde{\omega}^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}}}
is generated by the
w_{i}
and
pv_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,g
, there is a surjective map
\mathcal{O}_{V}^{2g}\to g_{r}^{*}{\tilde{\omega}^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}}}
that sends
e_{i}\mapsto w_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq g
, and
e_{j}\mapsto pv_{j}
for
g+1\leq j\leq 2g
. Using the basis
\bar{w}_{i}
for
\bar{\omega}^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A},0}
, we have a surjective map
M\colon\mathcal{O}^{2g}_{i_{0}^{-1}V}\to\mathcal{O}^{g}_{i_{0}^{-1}V}
given by
e_{i}\mapsto e_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq g
, and
e_{j}\mapsto 0
for
g+1\leq j\leq 2g
and which induces the map
i_{0}^{*}g_{r}^{*}{\tilde{\omega}^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}}}\to q^{*}\bar{\omega}^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A},0}
. On the other hand, the images of
g_{r}^{*}(w_{i})
form a basis for
\omega^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A},0}
. With respect to this basis,
pv_{i}=\frac{p}{\mathrm{Hdg}}\mathrm{adj}(\widetilde{HW})(e_{i})
. Thus the surjective map
g_{r}^{*}{\tilde{\omega}^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}}}\to\omega^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A},0}
is induced by the map
N\colon\mathcal{O}^{2g}_{V}\to\mathcal{O}^{g}_{V}
that sends
e_{i}\mapsto e_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq g
, and
e_{i+g}\mapsto\frac{p}{\mathrm{Hdg}}\mathrm{adj}(\widetilde{HW})(e_{i})
for
1\leq i\leq g
. Suppose
(a_{1},\dots,a_{2g})\in\ker{N}
. Since
N\big(\sum_{i=g+1}^{2g}\mathcal{O}_{V}e_{i}\big)\subset\frac{p}{\mathrm{Hdg}}\cdot\mathcal{O}^{g}_{V}
, we see that
a_{i}\in\frac{p}{\mathrm{Hdg}}
for
1\leq i\leq g
. Thus
M
kills the kernel of the pullback of
N
to
\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}/(p\mathrm{Hdg}^{-1})
,
i_{0}^{*}N\colon\mathcal{O}^{2g}_{i_{0}^{-1}V}\to\mathcal{O}^{g}_{i_{0}^{-1}V}
. This proves the proposition.
∎

Proposition 3.7
.

The pullback of the exact sequence

0\to\omega_{\mathcal{A}}\to\mathrm{H}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{A}}\to\omega^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A},0}\to 0

along
i_{0}\colon\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}/(p\mathrm{Hdg}^{-1})\xhookrightarrow{}\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
admits a canonical splitting induced by the splitting of (
2
) which moreover commutes with the splitting induced by the decomposition
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}}\simeq\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}}
.

Proof.

This is immediate from Proposition
3.6
and the
\mathcal{O}_{L}
-linearity of the splitting.
∎

4.
p
-adic interpolation of modular and de Rham sheaves

Henceforth fix
n
a positive integer. Fix
I=[p^{a},p^{b}]
such that
k^{0}_{\alpha,I}
is analytic on
1+p^{n-1}(\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})
and
r
such that
H_{n}
is defined on
\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
. Depending on the two cases
I=[0,1]
(i.e.
\alpha=p
) or
I=[p^{a},p^{b}]
for
a,b\in\mathbb{N}
, these conditions are satisfied if

(1)

I=[0,1]
,
r\geq 2
if
p\neq 2
and
2\leq n\leq r
, or
r\geq 4
if
p=2
and
4\leq n\leq r
,

(2)

I=[p^{a},p^{b}]
with
a,b\in\mathbb{N}
,
r\geq 1
and
r+a\geq b+2
if
p\neq 2
and
b+2\leq n\leq a+r
, or
r\geq 2
and
r+a\geq b+4
if
p=2
, and
b+4\leq n\leq r+a
.

In this section we construct overconvergent modular and de Rham sheaves, denoted
\mathfrak{w}_{k,\alpha,I}
and
\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}
on the Hilbert modular scheme
\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}\times_{\mathfrak{W}^{0}_{\alpha,I}}\mathfrak{W}_{\alpha,I}
for the universal weight
k=k^{\textrm{un}}\colon(\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}\to\Lambda^{\times}_{\alpha,I}
. The modular sheaf interpolates
\omega_{\mathcal{A}}^{k}
for classical weights
k
. The construction can be summarized as follows. By passing to a partial Igusa tower depending on
n
, we construct a modified modular sheaf
\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}
and a modified de Rham sheaf
\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}
together with a modified unit root splitting. We decompose the universal character into its components induced by the splitting of
\mathcal{O}_{L}
in
\mathcal{O}_{K}
. We decompose
\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}
and
\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}
likewise. On each component we carry out the construction of interpolation for the corresponding component of the universal weight following the technique developed in
[
AI21
]
. Finally we define
\mathfrak{w}_{k,\alpha,I}
and
\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}
by taking the tensor product of these individual components.

The construction of
\mathfrak{w}_{k,\alpha,I}
appears in the joint work of Andreatta, Iovita, Pilloni and Stevens
[
AIS14
]
,
[
AIP16
]
,
[
AIP16a
]
. Our construction is similar to
[
AIP16a
]
. However using the theory of vector bundles with marked sections we make it more explicit by actually constructing sections that generate
\mathfrak{w}_{k,\alpha,I}
locally. This is inspired by
[
AI21
]
. At the end of the section we compare our construction of
\mathfrak{w}_{k,\alpha,I}
with the construction in
[
AIP16a
]
and show why they are isomorphic. The definition of
\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}
is new, but as we will see it is inspired by
[
AI21
]
and the improved technique of using modified unit root subspace. The main theorem of this section is the following.

Theorem
.

For
n,r,\alpha,I
as above and
k=k^{\textrm{un}}
the universal weight on
\mathfrak{W}_{\alpha,I}
, there are formal sheaves
\mathfrak{w}_{k,\alpha,I}
and
\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}
on
\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{r,\alpha,I}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}\times_{\mathfrak{W}^{0}_{\alpha,I}}\mathfrak{W}_{\alpha,I}
. For
\alpha=p
and
I=[0,1]
, viewing a classical weight
\kappa
as a point of
\mathcal{W}_{p,[0,1]}(\mathbb{C}_{p})=\mathcal{W}_{p}(\mathbb{C}_{p})
, the restriction of the sheaf
\mathfrak{w}_{k,\alpha,I}[1/p]_{|k\mapsto\kappa}
on the associated analytic adic space
{\mathcal{X}_{r,p}}\times_{\mathcal{W}^{0}_{p}}{\mathcal{W}_{p}}_{|\kappa}
gives the sheaf
\omega_{\mathcal{A}}^{\kappa}
of classical Hilbert modular forms of weight
\kappa
. The sheaf
\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}
is equipped with a filtration by coherent
\mathcal{O}_{\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{r,\alpha,I}}
-modules
\{\mathrm{Fil}_{i}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}\}_{i\geq 0}
, and
\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}
is the
\alpha
-adic completion of
\varinjlim_{i}\mathrm{Fil}_{i}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}
. Moreover
\mathrm{Fil}_{0}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}=\mathfrak{w}_{k,\alpha,I}
.

4.1.
The sheaves
\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}
and
\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}

The trivialization of
H^{\vee}_{n}
on
\mathcal{IG}_{n,r,I}
induces an equality of groups
H^{\vee}_{n}(\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I})=H^{\vee}_{n}(\mathcal{IG}_{n,r,I})\simeq\mathcal{O}_{L}/p^{n}\mathcal{O}_{L}
. Let
P^{\textrm{univ}}
be the image of
1\in\mathcal{O}_{L}/p^{n}\mathcal{O}_{L}
in
H^{\vee}_{n}(\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I})
. We have a map of
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}
-modules,

(3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{\mathcal{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{\vee}_{n}(\mathcal{IG}_{n,r,I})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}}=H^{\vee}_{n}(\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\omega}_{H_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{\mathcal{A}}/p^{n}\mathrm{Hdg}^{-\frac{p^{n}-1}{p-1}}\omega_{\mathcal{A}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dlog\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xrightarrow{\sim}}

Definition 4.1
.

Define the sheaf
\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}
to be the inverse image under the map
\omega_{A}\to{\omega}_{H_{n}}
of the image of
\dlog\otimes 1
. We call this the modified modular sheaf.

Proposition 4.2
.

The sheaf
\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}
is a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}}
sheaf of rank
1
. The cokernel of
\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}\subset\omega_{\mathcal{A}}
is killed by
\mathrm{Hdg}^{\frac{1}{p-1}}
. Moreover
\dlog
induces an isomorphism

\dlog\otimes 1\colon H^{\vee}_{n}(\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}}/p^{n}\mathrm{Hdg}^{-\frac{p^{n}}{p-1}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Omega_{A}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}}/p^{n}\mathrm{Hdg}^{-\frac{p^{n}}{p-1}}.

Proof.

[
AIP16a
, Proposition 4.1]
.
∎

Since
\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}\subset\omega_{\mathcal{A}}
is an invertible
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}}
- module, there exists an invertible ideal
\underline{\xi}\subset\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}}
, such that
\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}=\underline{\xi}\omega_{\mathcal{A}}
. Lemma
4.5
shows that
\det\underline{\xi}=\mathrm{Hdg}^{\frac{1}{p-1}}
.

Definition 4.3
.

Define the sheaf
\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\underline{\xi}\mathrm{H}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{A}}
. We call this the modified de Rham sheaf.

Corollary 4.4
.

We have a short exact sequence of locally free
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}}
-modules

(4)

0\to\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}\to\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}\to\underline{\xi}\omega^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A},0}\to 0

which splits upon pulling back via
i_{n}\colon\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}/(p\mathrm{Hdg}^{-1})\xhookrightarrow{}\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}
.

Proof.

This is immediate from Proposition
3.7
.
∎

Letting
s=\dlog(P^{\textrm{univ}})\in\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}/p^{n}\mathrm{Hdg}^{\frac{-p^{n}}{p-1}}\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}
, and taking its
\sigma
-components
s_{\sigma}
for
\sigma\in\Sigma
, we get a vector bundle with marked sections
(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}},\{s_{\sigma}\}_{\sigma\in\Sigma})
. Associated to this pair, one can consider the geometric vector bundle with marked sections
\mathbb{V}_{0}(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}},\{s_{\sigma}\}_{\sigma\in\Sigma})
in the sense of Definition
4.21
. We will show that the points of this geometric vector bundle have a natural interpretation as
\mathcal{O}_{L}
-linear functions on
\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}
that evaluate to
1
on
s
. But before that we need to study functoriality of the sheaf
\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}
with respect to the
U
correspondence. Note that we are not interested in
\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}
solely for its structure as a vector bundle with marked sections, but we are also interested in the splitting modulo some small power of
p
. We have already seen above that such a splitting exists modulo
p\mathrm{Hdg}^{-1}
. In studying functoriality of
\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}
for the
U
correspondence, we will pin down the small power of
p
for which the splitting is functorial too.

Let
\beta_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=p\mathrm{Hdg}^{-\frac{p^{n}}{p-1}}
.

4.1.1.
Functoriality

Consider the projection
\lambda\colon\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{A}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathcal{A}/H_{1}
. Let
\lambda^{\prime}\colon{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{A}
be the isogeny such that
\lambda^{\prime}\circ\lambda=[p]
. Then
\lambda^{\prime}
maps
H_{n}(\mathcal{A}^{\prime})
to
H_{n}(\mathcal{A})
and induces an isomorphism of canonical subgroups
H_{n}(\mathcal{A}^{\prime})\simeq H_{n}(\mathcal{A})
on the generic fibres. The generic trivialization of
H_{n}^{\vee}(\mathcal{A}^{\prime})
induced by this isomorphism defines a map
\tilde{F}\colon\mathfrak{IG}_{1,r,I}\to\mathfrak{IG}_{1,r-1,I}
that sends
\mathcal{A}\mapsto\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
together with this trivialization of the generic fibre of the dual of the canonical subgroup. Note that a priori if
\mathcal{A}
is
\mathfrak{c}
-polarized then
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
is
p\mathfrak{c}
-polarized. But since multiplication by
p
induces a canonical isomorphism
M(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})\to M(\mu_{N},p\mathfrak{c})
, we indeed get a map
\tilde{F}
as above. By abuse of notation we also denote by
\tilde{F}
the map
\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}\to\mathfrak{X}_{r-1,\alpha,I}
induced by sending
\mathcal{A}\mapsto\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
. The following diagram is commutative with
h
and
h^{\prime}
being the usual projections.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{IG}_{1,r,I}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{IG}_{1,r-1,I}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{X}_{r-1,\alpha,I}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{F}}

The functoriality of the
\dlog
map provides the following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{\mathcal{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\lambda^{\prime})^{*}}

The map
(\lambda^{\prime})^{*}
is the adjoint of a lift of the Hasse-Witt map
HW\colon\omega^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}/(p\mathrm{Hdg}^{-1})}\to\omega^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}/(p\mathrm{Hdg}^{-1})}
modulo
p\mathrm{Hdg}^{-1}
because
\lambda^{\prime}
is a lift of the Verschiebung. As a map between invertible
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{1,r,I}}
-modules,
(\lambda^{\prime})^{*}
corresponds to multiplication by an invertible
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{1,r,I}}
ideal
{\widetilde{HW}}
. Since
\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}=\underline{\xi}\omega_{\mathcal{A}}
, we have the following relation between
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{1,r,I}}
ideals.

Lemma 4.5
.

{\tilde{F}}^{*}\underline{\xi}=\underline{\xi}{\widetilde{HW}}
.

Proof.

Follows from the discussion above.
∎

Definition 4.6
.

Define the
\sigma
-components
\widetilde{HW}(\sigma)
of
\widetilde{HW}
as the partial Hasse ideals.

Corollary 4.7
.

\prod_{\sigma}\widetilde{HW}(\sigma)=\mathrm{Hdg}
.

Proof.

Immediate as the determinant of
\widetilde{HW}
is
\mathrm{Hdg}
.
∎

We will prove a result relating the
\sigma
-components of
\underline{\xi}
and
\widetilde{HW}
. For that we need to choose a numbering of
\Sigma
. Recall
p
splits as
p=\mathfrak{P}_{1}\cdots\mathfrak{P}_{h}
in
\mathcal{O}_{L}
with their inertia degree
f(\mathfrak{P}_{i}|p)=f_{i}
. For each
i
, let
\Sigma_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}
be the subset of embeddings that induce the
\mathfrak{P}_{i}
-adic valuation on
L
. Choose a bijection
\Xi_{i}\colon\{i_{1},\dots,i_{f_{i}}\}\simeq\Sigma_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}
such that
\Xi_{i}(i_{n+1})=\Xi_{i}(i_{n})\circ\varphi_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}
, where
\varphi_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}
is the lift of the Frobenius in
D(\mathfrak{P}_{i})
.

Corollary 4.8
.

(\tilde{F}^{*}\omega_{\mathcal{A}})(i_{j})=\tilde{F}^{*}(\omega_{\mathcal{A}}(i_{j-1}))
. In particular,
\tilde{F}^{*}(\underline{\xi}(i_{j-1}))=\underline{\xi}(i_{j})\widetilde{HW}(i_{j})
.

Proof.

Recall that for any
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{1,r,I}}
-module
\mathcal{F}
,
\mathcal{F}(i_{j})
is the component on which
\mathcal{O}_{L}
acts via
\Xi_{i}(i_{j})
. The claim follows immediately by noting that modulo
p\mathrm{Hdg}^{-1}
,
\tilde{F}
induces a morphism such that the following diagram commutes with
\varphi
being the Frobenius.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{IG}_{1,r,I}/(p\mathrm{Hdg}^{-1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{IG}_{1,r-1,I}/(p\mathrm{Hdg}^{-1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{X}_{\alpha,I}/(p)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{X}_{\alpha,I}/(p)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}

∎

Lemma 4.9
.

\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
and
\mathfrak{IG}_{1,r,I}
are normal schemes.

Proof.

For the case of
\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
see
[
AIP16a
, Corollary 3.8]
. The map
\mathcal{IG}_{1,r,I}\to\mathcal{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
is finite étale. Hence
\mathcal{IG}_{1,r,I}
is normal. Thus
\mathfrak{IG}_{1,r,I}
is normal being the normalization of
\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
in
\mathcal{IG}_{1,r,I}
.
∎

Lemma 4.10
.

For all
i_{j}
,
\tilde{F}^{*}(\underline{\xi}(i_{j}))
is a
p
-th power at all height 1 localizations.

Proof.

Since this is an equality of ideals in a normal scheme it is enough to check the statement locally at height 1 primes. So choose an affine open
U
in
\mathfrak{X}_{\alpha,I}
such that the pullback of
\omega_{\mathcal{A}}
to
U/(p)
is trivial as an
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\alpha,I}}/(p)
-module. Let
\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R_{r-1}}
and
\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R_{r}}
be its inverse image in
\mathfrak{X}_{r-1,\alpha,I}
and
\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
respectively. Let
S_{r-1}
and
S_{r}
be their respective inverse images in
\mathfrak{IG}_{1,r-1,I}
and
\mathfrak{IG}_{1,r,I}
. So
\tilde{F}
induces a commutative diagram as follows.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{r-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{r-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{F}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{F}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}

Pick a height 1 prime
\mathfrak{p}\in\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{S_{r}}
that contains a local generator of
\underline{\xi}(i_{j})
. Since
\prod_{i,j}\underline{\xi}(i_{j})^{p-1}=\mathrm{Hdg}

[
AIP18
, Proposition A.3]
,
\mathfrak{p}\in V(\mathrm{Hdg})
. Let
\mathfrak{q}=h^{-1}\mathfrak{p}
. Then
\mathfrak{q}
is a height 1 prime containing
\mathrm{Hdg}
. In particular
\mathfrak{q}{R_{r}}_{q}
is generated by
\mathrm{Hdg}
as
\mathrm{Hdg}
has simple zeroes along degree 1 divisors in
\mathfrak{X}_{\alpha,I}/(p)
(Theorem
2.3
). Let
\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}=(\tilde{F}^{*})^{-1}\mathfrak{p}
and
\mathfrak{q}^{\prime}=(\tilde{F}^{*})^{-1}\mathfrak{q}
. Localizing at the primes gives a diagram as follows.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{S_{r-1}}_{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{S_{r}}_{\mathfrak{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{R_{r-1}}_{\mathfrak{q}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{R_{r}}_{\mathfrak{q}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{F}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{F}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}

All the rings are DVR. The bottom arrow has ramification index
p
.
h
and
h^{\prime}
are tamely ramified. This forces the upper arrow to be ramified of index
p
. This proves the lemma.
∎

Remark 4.11
.

It seems that in fact
\tilde{F}^{*}(\underline{\xi}(i_{j}))=\underline{\xi}(i_{j})^{p}
. This would imply that
\underline{\xi}(i_{j-1})^{p}=\underline{\xi}(i_{j})\widetilde{HW}(i_{j})
which reflects the fact that the partial Hasse invariant of degree
i_{j}
is of weight
(p,-1)
concentrated at degree
(i_{j-1},i_{j})

[
Gor01
, Theorem 2.1]
. Moreover this shows a posteriori that
\prod_{i,j}\underline{\xi}(i_{j})^{p-1}=\mathrm{Hdg}
. But as of now we are not able to prove this.

Proposition 4.12
.

There exists an
r
large enough such that the map
(\lambda^{\prime})^{*}\colon\mathrm{H}_{\mathcal{A}}\to\mathrm{H}_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}
restricts to a well-defined map
(\lambda^{\prime})^{*}\colon\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}\to\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A^{\prime}}}
that sends marked sections to marked sections. Moreover, let
\mathcal{Q}\subset\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}/p\mathrm{Hdg}(\mathcal{A})^{-(p+1)}
be the kernel of the marked splitting, and let
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\subset\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A^{\prime}}}/p\mathrm{Hdg}(\mathcal{A})^{-(p+1)}
be the same for
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
. Then
(\lambda^{\prime})^{*}
sends
\mathcal{Q}
to
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Since
(\lambda^{\prime})^{*}
maps
\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}
isomorphically onto
\Omega_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}
sending the marked section to the marked section, it is enough to show that the induced map
\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}/\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}\to\mathrm{H}_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}/\Omega_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}
factors through the inclusion
\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A^{\prime}}}/\Omega_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}\xhookrightarrow{}\mathrm{H}_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}/\Omega_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}
. Choosing suitable local generators
\xi
of
\underline{\xi}({\mathcal{A}})
and
\widetilde{HW}
of
\widetilde{HW}({\mathcal{A}})
respectively as
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}}
-modules, we have a diagram as follows.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A^{\prime}}}/\Omega_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{F}^{*}(\underline{\xi}({\mathcal{A}})\widetilde{HW}({\mathcal{A}}))\cdot\omega^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}/\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{H}_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}/\Omega_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{H}_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}/\omega_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}\simeq\omega^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\xi}({\mathcal{A}})\widetilde{HW}({\mathcal{A}})\cdot\omega^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\lambda^{\prime})^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p\widetilde{HW}^{-1}\xi\widetilde{HW}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{F}^{*}(\xi\widetilde{HW})}

Here the bottom right diagonal arrow is the map induced on the Lie algebra by
\lambda\colon\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
. Choosing basis of
\omega^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}}
and of
\omega^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}
, this
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}}
-linear map is multiplication by
p\widetilde{HW}^{-1}
upto a unit. Hence we get a description of the arrow as in the diagram.

Let
\mathrm{Hdg}=\mathrm{Hdg}({\mathcal{A}})
in this proof. Since multiplication by
p\xi
is injective, we see that the image of
\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}/\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}
under
(\lambda^{\prime})^{*}
does not intersect
\omega_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}/\Omega_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}
. We first show that
\pi\circ(\lambda^{\prime})^{*}
factors through the submodule
\tilde{F}^{*}(\underline{\xi}({\mathcal{A}})\widetilde{HW}({\mathcal{A}}))\omega^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}\subset\omega^{\vee}_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}
. For this it is enough to show that
p\underline{\xi}({\mathcal{A}})\subset\tilde{F}^{*}(\underline{\xi}({\mathcal{A}})\widetilde{HW}({\mathcal{A}}))
. Using Lemma
4.5
this reduces to showing
p/(\widetilde{HW}\tilde{F}^{*}(\widetilde{HW}))\subset\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}}
which can be ensured by choosing large enough
r
since
\det{\widetilde{HW}}=\mathrm{Hdg}
. This proves that the map
(\lambda^{\prime})^{*}-i^{-1}\circ\pi\circ(\lambda^{\prime})^{*}
factors through
\omega_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}/\Omega_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}
which is a torsion module killed by
\tilde{F}^{*}(\underline{\xi}({\mathcal{A}}))=\underline{\xi}({\mathcal{A}})\widetilde{HW}({\mathcal{A}})
(Lemma
4.5
). Now since
\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}=\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}+\underline{\xi}({\mathcal{A}})\widetilde{HW}({\mathcal{A}})\cdot\mathrm{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
, the difference
(\lambda^{\prime})^{*}-i^{-1}\circ\pi\circ(\lambda^{\prime})^{*}=0
. This proves the first claim.

The second claim follows from a local computation. Choose an open affine
\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}=U\subset\mathfrak{X}_{\alpha,I}
such that
\mathrm{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
admits an
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes R
basis
\{e,f\}
over
U
with
e
a basis of
\omega_{\mathcal{A}}
. Let
\{\bar{e},\bar{f}\}
be their image over
\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}/p
. The unit root subspace is generated by a vector
\bar{v}=\bar{C}\bar{e}+HW\bar{f}
for some
\bar{C}\in(\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes R)/p
. Let
\varphi\colon R/p\to R/p
be the Frobenius. With respect to the basis
\{\bar{e},\bar{f}\}
of
{\mathrm{H}}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}}
and
\{\bar{e}^{(p)}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\varphi^{*}(\bar{e}),\bar{f}^{(p)}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\varphi^{*}(\bar{f})\}
of
{\mathrm{H}}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{(p)}}
, the matrix of Verschiebung
V\colon{\mathrm{H}}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}}\to{\mathrm{H}}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{(p)}}
can be written as

V=\begin{pmatrix}HW&\bar{B}\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}.

Since Verschiebung kills the unit root subspace,
V(\bar{C}\bar{e}+HW\bar{f})=(\bar{C}HW+\bar{B}HW)\bar{e}=0
. This shows that
\bar{B}=-\bar{C}
. Let
\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}_{n,r}\subset\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}
(resp.
\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}_{n,r-1}\subset\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r-1,I}
) be the inverse image of
U
in
\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}
(resp.
\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r-1,I}
). Then as discussed in §
3
,
\mathrm{H}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{A}}
over
\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}
is generated by the pullback of
e
and a lift
v=Ce+\widetilde{HW}f
of
\bar{v}
. For notational simplicity we will write these sections as
\{e,v\}
still. Similarly,
\mathrm{H}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}
is generated by pulling back via
\tilde{F}\colon\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}_{n,r}\to\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}_{n,r-1}
the pullbacks of
e
and
v
to
\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}_{n,r-1}
. We will write these as
\{\tilde{F}^{*}e,\tilde{F}^{*}v\}
. Then with respect to
\{e,v\}
and
\{\tilde{F}^{*}e,\tilde{F}^{*}v\}
the matrix of
(\lambda^{\prime})^{*}
can be described as

\displaystyle(\lambda^{\vee})^{*}

\displaystyle={\begin{pmatrix}1&-\tilde{F}^{*}(C\widetilde{HW}^{-1})\\
0&\tilde{F}^{*}(\widetilde{HW}^{-1})\end{pmatrix}}\cdot\begin{pmatrix}\widetilde{HW}&B\\
0&p\widetilde{HW}^{-1}\end{pmatrix}\cdot\begin{pmatrix}1&C\\
0&\widetilde{HW}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\widetilde{HW}&C\widetilde{HW}{}+B\widetilde{HW}-p\tilde{F}^{*}(C\widetilde{HW}^{-1})\\
0&p\tilde{F}^{*}(\widetilde{HW}^{-1})\end{pmatrix}.

Here
B
is a lift of
\bar{B}
modulo
p\mathrm{Hdg}^{-1}
. Therefore with respect to the basis
\{\xi e,\xi v\}
and
\{\tilde{F}^{*}(\xi e),\tilde{F}^{*}(\xi v)\}
, the matrix of
(\lambda^{\prime})^{*}\colon\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}\to\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A^{\prime}}}
is written as

(\lambda^{\prime})^{*}=\begin{pmatrix}1&C+B-p\tilde{F}^{*}(C\widetilde{HW}^{-1})\widetilde{HW}^{-1}\\
0&p\widetilde{HW}^{-1}\tilde{F}^{*}(\widetilde{HW}^{-1})\end{pmatrix}.

Since
\det{\widetilde{HW}}=\mathrm{Hdg}
, this proves the second claim of the lemma.
∎

Corollary 4.13
.

The kernel of the marked splitting
\mathcal{Q}
equals the kernel of Verschiebung modulo
p\mathrm{Hdg}^{-(p+1)}
.

Proof.

Follows from the proof of Proposition
4.12
.
∎

Definition 4.14
.

For a prime
\mathfrak{P}|p
, define the map
\tilde{F}^{*}_{\mathfrak{P}}\colon\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{r-1,\alpha,I}}\to\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}}
(and similarly for
\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r-1,I})
that coincides with
\tilde{F}^{*}
on the
\mathfrak{P}
-component and identity on the rest.

Remark 4.15
.

For each
\mathfrak{P}|p
, one can consider the partial Hasse invariant
\mathrm{Hdg}_{\mathfrak{P}}=\prod_{\sigma|\mathfrak{P}}\widetilde{HW}(\sigma)
. Then for a multi-index
\mathbf{r}=(r_{1},\dots,r_{h})
, one can consider the formal model
\mathfrak{X}_{\mathbf{r},\alpha,I}
whose generic fibre is defined by the relations
|p|\leq\prod_{i=1}^{h}|\mathrm{Hdg}_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}|^{p^{r_{i}}}
, constructed in the same fashion as
\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
using formal admissible blow-ups. In particular, for
r=\max\{r_{1},\dots,r_{h}\}
, and
s=\min\{r_{1},\dots,r_{h}\}
, we have
\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}\subset\mathfrak{X}_{\mathbf{r},\alpha,I}\subset\mathfrak{X}_{s,\alpha,I}
. The map on the generic fibre of the overconvergent moduli schemes defined by sending
A\mapsto A/H_{1}\cap A[\mathfrak{P}_{i}]
, induces a map
\tilde{F}_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}\colon\mathfrak{X}_{{r},\alpha,I}^{\mathfrak{c}}\to\mathfrak{X}_{\mathbf{r}^{\prime},\alpha,I}^{\mathfrak{cP}_{i}}
, where
\mathbf{r}^{\prime}
is the multi-index with
r-1
in the
i
-th place and
r
elsewhere. It is easy to see that the pullback of the modular sheaf over
\mathfrak{X}_{\mathbf{r}^{\prime},\alpha,I}^{\mathfrak{cP}_{i}}
along
\tilde{F}_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}
is isomorphic to the scalar extension of the modular sheaf over
\mathfrak{X}_{r-1,\alpha,I}
along
\tilde{F}_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}^{*}
as defined in Definition
4.14
.

Let
\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}\to\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}
be an
\alpha
-adically complete, admissible, normal
\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}
-algebra. In particular, it corresponds to an abelian scheme
A^{\prime}/R
with a generic trivialization
\mathcal{O}_{L}/p^{n}\mathcal{O}_{L}\xrightarrow{\sim}H_{n}^{\vee}(A^{\prime})
. Suppose that
\lambda_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}\colon A^{\prime}\to A
is an isogeny, such that
\ker\lambda_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}
is generically étale locally isomorphic to
\mathcal{O}_{L}/\mathfrak{P}_{i}
, and such that
\ker\lambda_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}\cap H_{1}(A^{\prime})=0
. We note that
A/H_{1}[\mathfrak{P}_{i}]\simeq A^{\prime}\otimes\mathfrak{P}_{i}^{-1}
. Therefore,
\mathrm{Hdg}(A^{\prime})=\mathrm{Hdg}(A)\mathrm{Hdg}_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}(A)^{p-1}
.

Proposition 4.16
.

There exists an
r
large enough such that the map
\lambda_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}^{*}\colon\mathrm{H}_{A}\to\mathrm{H}_{A^{\prime}}
restricts to a well-defined map
\lambda_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}^{*}\colon\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{A}\to\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{A^{\prime}}
that sends marked sections to marked sections. It also maps the kernel
\mathcal{Q}\subset\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{A}/p\mathrm{Hdg}(A)^{-(p+1)}
to the kernel
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\subset\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{A^{\prime}}/p\mathrm{Hdg}(A)^{-(p+1)}
.

Proof.

We first note that
\lambda^{*}_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}\colon\Omega_{A}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Omega_{A^{\prime}}
is an isomorphism, since
\lambda_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}
induces an isomorphism
H_{n}^{\vee}(A)\xrightarrow{\sim}H_{n}^{\vee}(A^{\prime})
. Then as in Proposition
4.12
, we have a diagram as follows.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{A^{\prime}}/\Omega_{{A}^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{F}^{*}_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}(\underline{\xi}({{A}})\widetilde{HW}({{A}}))\cdot\omega^{\vee}_{{A}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{A}/\Omega_{{A}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{H}_{{A}^{\prime}}/\Omega_{{A}^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{H}_{{A}^{\prime}}/\omega_{{A}^{\prime}}\simeq\omega^{\vee}_{{A}^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\xi}({{A}})\widetilde{HW}({{A}})\cdot\omega^{\vee}_{{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda^{*}_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{P}_{i}\widetilde{HW}_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}^{-1}\xi\widetilde{HW}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{F}^{*}_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}(\xi\widetilde{HW})}

The exact same reasoning as in Proposition
4.12
proves the first claim. By Corollary
4.13
,
\mathcal{Q}
is the kernel of Verschiebung modulo
p\mathrm{Hdg}^{-(p+1)}
. The second claim then follows from the observations that
\lambda_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}
is a lift of the partial Verschiebung at
\mathfrak{P}_{i}
of
A\otimes\mathfrak{P}_{i}
, and Verschiebung commutes with the partial Verschiebung.
∎

Definition 4.17
.

Let
\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},\Omega_{\mathcal{A}},s
be the modified de Rham sheaf, the modified modular sheaf and the marked section respectively. Let
\mathcal{Q}\subset\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}/p\mathrm{Hdg}^{{-p^{2}}}
be the kernel of the splitting
\psi\colon\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}/p\mathrm{Hdg}^{{-p^{2}}}\to\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}/p\mathrm{Hdg}^{{-p^{2}}}
. We call this marked splitting the modified unit root splitting and
\mathcal{Q}
the modified unit root subspace.

We remark that by Proposition
4.12
the modified unit root subspace is functorial for
\lambda^{\prime}\colon\mathcal{A}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{A}
.

Recall the notation
\beta_{n}=p^{n}\mathrm{Hdg}^{\frac{-p^{n}}{p-1}}
. Let
\eta\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=p\mathrm{Hdg}^{{-p^{2}}}
.

4.2.
Vector bundles with marked sections and marked splitting

In this section we recall the general formalism of vector bundles with marked sections following
[
AI21
, §2]
. We then define a subfunctor of a vector bundle with a marked section, which we call “vector bundle with marked sections and a marked splitting” and show representability of this functor.

Let
S
be a formal scheme with an ideal of definition
\mathscr{I}
which is invertible. Let
i\colon S/\mathscr{I}\xhookrightarrow{}S
be the closed subscheme defined by the ideal
\mathscr{I}
. Let
\mathbf{FSch}^{\mathscr{I}}_{S}
be the category of formal schemes
f\colon T\to S
such that
f^{-1}\mathscr{I}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{T}
is an invertible ideal in
T
.

Definition 4.18
.

A formal vector bundle of rank
n
is a formal vector group scheme
X\to S
which is isomorphic to
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{n}
locally over
S
.

Definition 4.19
.

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a locally free sheaf of rank
n
on
S
. The formal vector bundle
\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{E})
of rank
n
is defined as the functor on
\mathbf{FSch}^{\mathscr{I}}_{S}

\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{E})(t\colon T\to S)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathcal{E}^{\vee}(T)=\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{T}}(t^{*}\mathcal{E},\mathcal{O}_{T}).

Lemma 4.20
.

\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{E})
is representable by the formal scheme
\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{\widehat{\Sym^{\bullet}\mathcal{E}}}\to S
, where the completion is with respect to the
\mathscr{I}
-adic topology. This formal scheme is a formal vector bundle of rank
n
. Moreover, the contravariant functor
\mathbb{V}
defines an equivalence between locally free sheaves on
S
of constant rank and formal vector bundles of finite rank over
S
, and the equivalence preserves the notion of rank.

Proof.

[
AI21
, Lemma 2.2]
.
∎

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a locally free sheaf of rank
n
on
S
such that
\bar{\mathcal{E}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=i^{*}\mathcal{E}
has sections
s_{1},\dots,s_{m}\in\Gamma(S/\mathscr{I},\bar{\mathcal{E}})
for
m\leq n
, satisfying the following two properties:

(1)

The subsheaf
\bar{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}\subset\bar{\mathcal{E}}
generated by
s_{1},\dots,s_{m}
is locally free,

(2)

\bar{\mathcal{E}}/\bar{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
is locally free.

Definition 4.21
.

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a locally free sheaf with marked sections
s_{1},\dots,s_{m}
as above. The formal vector bundle with marked sections
\mathbb{V}_{0}(\mathcal{E},s_{1},\dots,s_{m})
is defined as the functor on
\mathbf{FSch}^{\mathscr{I}}_{S}

\mathbb{V}_{0}(\mathcal{E},s_{1},\dots,s_{m})(t\colon T\to S)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\{f\in\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{E})(T)\,|\,(f\textrm{ mod }t^{*}\mathscr{I})(t^{*}s_{i})=1\,\forall i\}

Lemma 4.22
.

\mathbb{V}_{0}(\mathcal{E},s_{1},\dots,s_{m})
is represented by an open formal subscheme of an admissible formal blow-up of
\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{E})
.

Proof.

This is
[
AI21
, Lemma 2.4]
. We recall the construction.
s_{1},\dots,s_{m}
define an ideal
\mathscr{J}\subset{\Sym^{\bullet}\mathcal{E}}/\mathscr{I}
given by
\mathscr{J}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(s_{1}-1,\dots,s_{m}-1)
. Let
\tilde{\mathscr{J}}
be the inverse image of
\mathscr{J}
in
\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{\Sym^{\bullet}\mathcal{E}}}
. Let
\mathbb{B}
be the blow-up of
\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{E})
along
\tilde{\mathscr{J}}
. Then take the
\mathscr{I}
-adic completion of the open in
\mathbb{B}
where the inverse image ideal of
\tilde{\mathscr{J}}
coincides with the inverse image ideal of
\mathscr{I}
. Then as shown in loc. cit. this formal scheme represents
\mathbb{V}_{0}(\mathcal{E},s_{1},\dots,s_{m})
.

In local coordinates
\mathbb{V}_{0}(\mathcal{E},s_{1},\dots,s_{m})
can be described as follows. Let
\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}\subset S
be an open which trivializes
\mathcal{E}
and
\mathscr{I}
, and let
X_{i}
be a lift of
s_{i}
for all
i
and extend it to a basis of
\mathcal{E}
. Then
\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{E})_{|\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.63885pt{R}}=\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997ptR\langle X_{1},\dots,X_{n}\rangle
. Then
\mathbb{V}_{0}(\mathcal{E},s_{1},\dots,s_{m})_{|\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.63885pt{R}}
is given by

\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}\langle\{X_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}\rangle\langle\{\frac{X_{i}-1}{\alpha}\}_{i=1}^{m}\rangle\simeq\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}\langle\{Z_{i}\}_{i=1}^{m},X_{m+1},\dots,X_{n}\rangle

where
\alpha
is a generator for
\mathscr{I}
, and the projection
\mathbb{V}_{0}(\mathcal{E},s_{1},\dots,s_{m})\to\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{E})
corresponds to the ring map that sends
X_{i}\mapsto 1+\alpha Z_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq m
and
X_{i}\mapsto X_{i}
otherwise.
∎

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a locally free sheaf of rank
n
with marked sections
s_{1},\dots,s_{m}
as above. Suppose the short exact sequence of locally free sheaves on
S/\mathscr{I}

0\to\bar{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}\to\bar{\mathcal{E}}\to\bar{\mathcal{E}}/\bar{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}\to 0

admits a splitting
\psi\colon\bar{\mathcal{E}}\to\bar{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
. Here
\bar{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
is the subsheaf generated by the
s_{i}
’s as above. Let
\mathcal{Q}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\ker{\psi}
.

Definition 4.23
.

Given a locally free sheaf
\mathcal{E}
on
S
of rank
n
, marked sections
s_{1},\dots,s_{m}
, and a subsheaf
\mathcal{Q}\subset\bar{\mathcal{E}}
corresponding to a splitting as above, we define the vector bundle with marked sections and marked splitting as the functor on
\mathbf{FSch}^{\mathscr{I}}_{S}

\mathbb{V}_{0}(\mathcal{E},s_{1},\dots,s_{m},\mathcal{Q})(t\colon T\to S)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\{f\in\mathbb{V}_{0}(\mathcal{E},s_{1},\dots,s_{m})(T)\,|\,(f\textrm{ mod }t^{*}\mathscr{I})(t^{*}\mathcal{Q})=0\}

Lemma 4.24
.

\mathbb{V}_{0}(\mathcal{E},s_{1},\dots,s_{m},\mathcal{Q})
is represented by an open formal subscheme of an admissible formal blow-up of
\mathbb{V}_{0}(\mathcal{E},s_{1},\dots,s_{m})
.

Proof.

The subsheaf
\mathcal{Q}\subset\bar{\mathcal{E}}
defines an ideal in
\Sym^{\bullet}\bar{\mathcal{E}}
, viz.
\mathcal{Q}\Sym^{\bullet}\bar{\mathcal{E}}
. Let
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}}
be the inverse image of this ideal in
\widehat{\Sym^{\bullet}\mathcal{E}}
. If
f\colon\mathbb{V}_{0}(\mathcal{E},s_{1},\dots,s_{m})\to\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{E})
is the projection, let
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=f^{-1}\tilde{\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{V}_{0}(\mathcal{E},s_{1},\dots,s_{m})}
be the inverse image ideal in
\mathbb{V}_{0}(\mathcal{E},s_{1},\dots,s_{m})
. Consider blow-up of
\mathbb{V}_{0}(\mathcal{E},s_{1},\dots,s_{m})
along
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}
. Let
X
be the
\mathscr{I}
-adic completion of the open in this blow-up where the inverse image ideal of
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}
coincides with the inverse image of
\mathscr{I}
. We claim that
X
represents
\mathbb{V}_{0}(\mathcal{E},s_{1},\dots,s_{m},\mathcal{Q})
.

To prove this, suppose
\tilde{t}\colon T\to X
be a lift of
t\colon T\to S
. Then certainly
\tilde{t}
defines a point of
\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{E})
and hence corresponds to an element
f\in\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{T}}(t^{*}\mathcal{E},\mathcal{O}_{T})
. Moreover this satisfies
(f\textrm{ mod }t^{*}\mathscr{I})(t^{*}s_{i})=1
. Since over
X
,
\mathscr{I}
coincides with
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}
,
(f\textrm{ mod }t^{*}\mathscr{I})
kills
t^{*}\mathcal{Q}
. Conversely, to prove that for any element
f\in\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{T}}(t^{*}\mathcal{E},\mathcal{O}_{T})
seen as a point of
\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{E})
, that sends
s_{i}\mapsto 1
and kills
\mathcal{Q}
modulo
t^{*}\mathscr{I}
, there exists a unique lift
\tilde{t}\colon T\to X
, it will be enough to prove this when
T=\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R^{\prime}}
and
t\colon T\to S
factors through an open
\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}\subset S
where
\bar{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}},\bar{\mathcal{E}}
and
\bar{\mathcal{E}}/\bar{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
are locally free. In that case picking lifts
X_{i}
of
s_{i}
as in the proof of Lemma
4.22
and lifts
Y_{m+1},\dots,Y_{n}
of a basis
y_{m+1},\dots,y_{n}
of
\mathcal{Q}
, we can write

\displaystyle X_{|\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.63885pt{R}}

\displaystyle\simeq\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}\langle\{X_{i}\}_{i=1}^{m}\{Y_{j}\}_{j=m+1}^{n}\rangle\langle\{\frac{X_{i}-1}{\alpha}\}_{i=1}^{m}\{\frac{Y_{j}}{\alpha}\}_{j=m+1}^{n}\rangle

\displaystyle\simeq R\langle\{Z_{i}\}_{i=1}^{m},\{W_{j}\}_{j=m+1}^{n}\rangle

where the projection
X\to\mathbb{V}_{0}(\mathcal{E},s_{1},\dots,s_{m})
corresponds to
Z_{i}\mapsto Z_{i}
and
Y_{j}\mapsto\alpha W_{j}
. Now
t^{*}\mathcal{E}=\oplus_{i=1}^{m}R^{\prime}(t^{*}X_{i})\bigoplus\oplus_{j=m+1}^{n}R^{\prime}(t^{*}Y_{j})
. An
R^{\prime}
-linear map
f\colon t^{*}\mathcal{E}\to R^{\prime}
that satisfies
f(X_{i})=1\textrm{ mod }(\alpha)
and
f(Y_{i})=0\textrm{ mod }(\alpha)
can be written uniquely as

f=\sum_{i=1}^{m}(1+\alpha r_{i})(t^{*}X_{i})^{\vee}+\sum_{j=m+1}^{n}\alpha q_{j}(t^{*}Y_{j})^{\vee}

for
r_{i},q_{j}\in R^{\prime}
. Then we can define a map
R^{\prime}\langle\{Z_{i}\}\{W_{j}\}\rangle
that sends
Z_{i}\mapsto r_{i}
and
W_{j}\mapsto q_{j}
for all
i,j
. This determines a point
\tilde{t}\in X(T)
.
∎

Lemma 4.25
.

The functor
\mathbb{V}_{0}(\mathcal{E},s_{1},\dots,s_{m},\mathcal{Q})
is functorial in tuples
(\mathcal{E},s_{1},\dots,s_{m},\mathcal{Q})
, i.e. given a map of locally free sheaves of equal rank
\rho\colon\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
, where
\mathcal{E}
(resp.
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
) is equipped with marked sections
s_{1},\dots,s_{m}
(resp.
s^{\prime}_{1},\dots,s^{\prime}_{m}
) and a subsheaf
\mathcal{Q}
(resp.
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
), corresponding to a marked splitting, if
\bar{\rho}(s_{i})=s^{\prime}_{i}
and
\bar{\rho}(\mathcal{Q})\subset\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
, then there are natural transformations
\mathbb{V}_{0}(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},s^{\prime}_{1},\dots,s^{\prime}_{m},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})\to\mathbb{V}_{0}(\mathcal{E},s_{1},\dots,s_{m},\mathcal{Q})
and
\mathbb{V}_{0}(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},s^{\prime}_{1},\dots,s^{\prime}_{m})\to\mathbb{V}_{0}(\mathcal{E},s_{1},\dots,s_{m})
such that the following diagram commutes.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{V}_{0}(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},s^{\prime}_{1},\dots,s^{\prime}_{m},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{V}_{0}(\mathcal{E},s_{1},\dots,s_{m},\mathcal{Q})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{V}_{0}(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},s^{\prime}_{1},\dots,s^{\prime}_{m})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{V}_{0}(\mathcal{E},s_{1},\dots,s_{m})}}

Proof.

Follows easily from the definition.
∎

4.3.
Formal
\mathcal{O}_{L}
-vector bundles with marked sections and marked splitting

In this section we define the relevant vector bundles with marked sections and marked splitting enriched with an action of
\mathcal{O}_{L}
. Although we focus on the sheaves relevant for our purpose, i.e.
\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}
and
\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}
, the theory can be developed more generally.

Recall
s=\dlog(P^{\textrm{univ}}_{n})\in\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}/\beta_{n}\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}
is the image of the universal generator of
H^{\vee}_{n}(\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I})
under the
\dlog
map. Let
s_{\sigma}
be its
\sigma
-component under the splitting
\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}=\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma}\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma)
. Following the VBMS formalism explained in §
4.2
we define the following formal
\mathcal{O}_{L}
-vector bundles with marked sections.

Definition 4.26
.

Define
\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}})
as the functor that associates to any
\alpha
-admissible formal scheme
\mathfrak{Z}\xrightarrow{\gamma}\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}
the following set:

\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}})(\mathfrak{Z}\xrightarrow{\gamma}\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Z}}}(\gamma^{*}\Omega_{\mathcal{A}},\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Z}}).

Similarly, define
\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}})
as the functor

\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}})(\mathfrak{Z}\xrightarrow{\gamma}\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Z}}}(\gamma^{*}\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Z}}).

Definition 4.27
.

Define
\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}},s)
as the functor that associates to any
\alpha
-admissible formal scheme
\mathfrak{Z}\xrightarrow{\gamma}\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}
, the following set:

\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}},s)(\mathfrak{Z}\xrightarrow{\gamma}\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\left\{h\in\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Z}}}\big(\gamma^{*}\Omega_{\mathcal{A}},\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Z}}\big)\,|\,(h\textrm{ mod }\gamma^{*}\beta_{n})(\gamma^{*}s)=1\right\}.

Similarly, define
\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s)
as the functor

\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s)(\mathfrak{Z}\xrightarrow{\gamma}\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\left\{h\in\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Z}}}\big(\gamma^{*}\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Z}}\big)\,|\,(h\textrm{ mod }\gamma^{*}\beta_{n})(\gamma^{*}s)=1\right\}.

Proposition 4.28
.

(1)

We have natural isomorphisms of functors

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}})\simeq\mathbb{V}(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}})=\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma}\mathbb{V}\big(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma)\big),\quad\quad\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}})\simeq\mathbb{V}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}})=\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma}\mathbb{V}\big(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma)\big).

where
\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{E})
for any locally finite free sheaf
\mathcal{E}
is defined as in Definition
4.19
.

(2)

We have natural isomorphisms of functors

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}},s)\simeq\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma}\mathbb{V}_{0}\big(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma),s_{\sigma}\big),\quad\quad\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s)\simeq\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma}\mathbb{V}_{0}\big(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma),s_{\sigma}\big).

where
\mathbb{V}_{0}(\mathcal{E},s)\subset\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{E})
for any locally finite free sheaf
\mathcal{E}
with a marked section
s
is defined as in Definition
4.21
.

Proof.

We note that since
K
is Galois, the natural map
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{K}\to\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\Sigma}
is an isomorphism. The claims of the proposition then follow immediately from the definitions.
∎

Taking into account the modified unit root subspace
\mathcal{Q}\subset\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}/p\mathrm{Hdg}^{-p^{2}}
, we define a geometric
\mathcal{O}_{L}
-vector bundle with marked section and marked splitting as follows.

Definition 4.29
.

(1)

For any
\sigma\in\Sigma
, define
\mathbb{V}_{0}\big(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma),s_{\sigma},\mathcal{Q}(\sigma)\big)
as the functor that associates to any
\alpha
-admissible formal scheme
\mathfrak{Z}\xrightarrow{\gamma}\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}
the following set:

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}_{0}\big(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma),s_{\sigma},\mathcal{Q}(\sigma)\big)(\mathfrak{Z})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\left\{h\in\mathbb{V}_{0}\big(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma),s_{\sigma}\big)(\mathfrak{Z})\,|\,(h\textrm{ mod }\gamma^{*}\eta)(\gamma^{*}\mathcal{Q}(\sigma))=0\right\}.

(2)

Define
\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s,\mathcal{Q})
as the functor that associates to any
\alpha
-admissible formal scheme
\mathfrak{Z}\xrightarrow{\gamma}\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}
the following set:

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s,\mathcal{Q})(\mathfrak{Z})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\left\{h\in\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s)(\mathfrak{Z})\,|\,(h\textrm{ mod }\gamma^{*}\eta)(\gamma^{*}\mathcal{Q})=0\right\}.

Proposition 4.30
.

We have a natural isomorphism of functors

\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s,\mathcal{Q})\simeq\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma}\mathbb{V}_{0}\big(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma),s_{\sigma},\mathcal{Q}(\sigma)\big).

Proof.

Clear from the definitions.
∎

Proposition 4.31
.

The functors
\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}},s),\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s)
and
\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s,\mathcal{Q})
are representable.

Proof.

Follows immediately from the discussion in §
4.2
.
∎

4.3.1.
Formal group action on formal
\mathcal{O}_{L}
-vector bundles

The vector bundles
\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}},s),\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s)
and
\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s,\mathcal{Q})
carry an action of the formal group
\mathfrak{T}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=1+\beta_{n}\Res_{\mathcal{O}_{L}/\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{G}_{a}
over
\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}
. This action realizes
\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}},s)
as a
\mathfrak{T}
-torsor over
\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}
. Moreover, there is a natural action of
\mathbb{T}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})={(\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})}^{\times}
on the aforementioned formal vector bundles over
\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
which we will describe too. Together we get an action of
\mathfrak{T}^{\textrm{ext}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}(1+\beta_{n}\Res_{\mathcal{O}_{L}/\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{G}_{a})
on these vector bundles over
\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
.

(1)

\mathfrak{T}^{\textrm{ext}}
-action on
\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}},s)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}
Let
(\rho,h)\in\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}},s)(R)
be a
{R}
-valued point of
\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}},s)
. Here
\rho\colon\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}\to\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}
is a morphism of formal schemes and
h\in\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes R}(\rho^{*}\Omega_{\mathcal{A}},\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes R)
. Then
\lambda\in\mathfrak{T}(R)
acts as
\lambda\ast(\rho,h)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(\rho,\lambda h)
. Let
\lambda\in\mathbb{T}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. Denote its class in
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}
by
\bar{\lambda}
. Then
\lambda
induces an isomorphism
{[\lambda]}\colon\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}
that induces the map

\displaystyle H^{\vee}_{n}(R)

\displaystyle\to H^{\vee}_{n}(R)

\displaystyle P

\displaystyle\mapsto\bar{\lambda}^{-1}P

on the
R
-valued points of
\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}
. There is a natural isomorphism
\gamma_{\lambda}\colon[\lambda]^{*}\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}
such that
(\gamma_{\lambda}\textrm{ mod }\beta_{n})([\lambda]^{*}s)=\bar{\lambda}^{-1}s
. Then we define the
\mathbb{T}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-action as
\lambda\ast(\rho,h)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=([\lambda]\circ\rho,\lambda h\circ\gamma_{\lambda})
.

(2)

\mathfrak{T}^{\textrm{ext}}
-action on
\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}
Let
(\rho,h)\in\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s)(R)
, with
\rho\colon\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}\to\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}
a morphism of formal schemes and
h\in\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes R}(\rho^{*}\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes R)
. Then
\lambda\in\mathfrak{T}(R)
acts as
\lambda\ast(\rho,h)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(\rho,\lambda h)
. If
\lambda\in\mathbb{T}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, then as before we have an isomorphism
[\lambda]\colon\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}
. This gives a natural isomorphism
\gamma_{\lambda}\colon[\lambda]^{*}\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}
such that
(\gamma_{\lambda}\textrm{ mod }\beta_{n})([\lambda]^{*}s)=\bar{\lambda}^{-1}s
. Then we define the
\mathbb{T}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-action as
\lambda\ast(\rho,h)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=([\lambda]\circ\rho,\lambda h\circ\gamma_{\lambda})
.

(3)

\mathfrak{T}^{\textrm{ext}}
-action on
\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s,\mathcal{Q})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}
This is defined by restricting the action defined on
\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s)
.

Lemma 4.32
.

The formal group
\mathfrak{T}
decomposes as
\mathfrak{T}=\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma}(1+\beta_{n}\mathbb{G}_{a})
over
\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}
. The action of
\mathfrak{T}
on
\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}},s)
and
\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s)
is compatible with the splitting of
\mathfrak{T}
and the vector bundles. That is to say, if
\lambda=(\lambda_{\sigma})\in\prod_{\sigma}(1+\beta_{n}\mathbb{G}_{a})(R)
, and
(\rho,h)\in\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}},s)(R)
, with
h=(h_{\sigma})\in\prod_{\sigma}\Hom_{R}(\rho^{*}\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma),R)
, then
\lambda\ast(\rho,h)=\prod_{\sigma}(\rho,\lambda_{\sigma}h_{\sigma})
. Similarly for
\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s)
.

Proof.

This is clear.
∎

\mathfrak{T}
-action in terms of local coordinates.

Based on Lemma
4.32
, the action of
\mathfrak{T}
on
\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}},s)
and on
\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s,\mathcal{Q})
can be described on local coordinates in the following manner.

Let
\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}\xhookrightarrow{}\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}
be an open subscheme such that
\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}
and
\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}
are trivialized as
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}}
-modules, and such that
\mathcal{Q}
is trivial too over
\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}/\eta
. Let
X\in\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}(R)
be a lift of
s
and
Y\in\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}(R)
be a lift of a local
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}}
generator
t
of
\mathcal{Q}
. Let
X_{\sigma},Y_{\sigma}
be their
\sigma
-components. The formal schemes
\mathbb{V}_{0}(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma),s_{\sigma})
and
\mathbb{V}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma),s_{\sigma},\mathcal{Q}(\sigma))
are realized as admissible blow-ups of
\mathbb{V}(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma))
and
\mathbb{V}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma))
respectively. Then as elaborated in §
4.2
the blow-ups are described by the following diagrams.

(5)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{V}(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{V}_{0}(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma),s_{\sigma})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}\langle X_{\sigma}\rangle}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}\langle Z_{\sigma}\rangle}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{\sigma}\mapsto 1+\beta_{n}Z_{\sigma}}

(6)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{V}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{V}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma),s_{\sigma})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{V}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s_{\sigma},\mathcal{Q}(\sigma))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}\langle X_{\sigma},Y_{\sigma}\rangle}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}\langle Z_{\sigma},Y_{\sigma}\rangle}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}\langle Z_{\sigma},W_{\sigma}\rangle}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{\sigma}\mapsto 1+\beta_{n}Z_{\sigma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{\sigma}\mapsto\alpha W_{\sigma}}

Let
\lambda\in\mathfrak{T}(R)
and let
\lambda=(\lambda_{\sigma})_{\sigma}
be its decomposition into coordinates. The action of
\lambda_{\sigma}
on
Z_{\sigma}
is such that
\lambda_{\sigma}\ast(1+\beta_{n}Z_{\sigma})=\lambda_{\sigma}(1+\beta_{n}Z_{\sigma})
. In other words,

\lambda_{\sigma}\ast Z_{\sigma}=\frac{\lambda_{\sigma}-1}{\beta_{n}}+\lambda_{\sigma}Z_{\sigma}.

Similarly,
\lambda_{\sigma}
acts on
W_{\sigma}
via

\lambda_{\sigma}\ast W_{\sigma}=\lambda_{\sigma}W_{\sigma}.

4.4.
p
-adic interpolation of
\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}
and
\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}

Let
n,r,\alpha,I
be as fixed in the beginning of the section. Denote by
\rho^{\prime}\colon\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s,\mathcal{Q})\xrightarrow{}\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}
and
\nu^{\prime}\colon\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}},s)\xrightarrow{}\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}
the projections.

Definition 4.33
.

(1)

For
k=k^{0}_{\alpha,I}\colon(\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}\to\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}
the universal character, define

\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{k,\alpha,I}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\nu^{\prime}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}},s)}[k].

The sections of this sheaf by definition are the functions
f\in\nu^{\prime}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}},s)}
that transform via
\lambda\ast f=k(\lambda)f
under the
\mathfrak{T}
-action.

(2)

For
k=k^{0}_{\alpha,I}
define

\mathbb{W}^{\prime}_{k,\alpha,I}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\rho^{\prime}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp},s,\mathcal{Q})}[k].

Let
k_{\sigma}\colon 1+\beta_{n}\mathbb{G}_{a}\to\mathbb{G}_{m}
be the restriction of
k^{0}_{\alpha,I}
to the
\sigma
-component of
\mathfrak{T}=\prod_{\sigma}(1+\beta_{n}\mathbb{G}_{a})
.

Proposition 4.34
.

Let
\nu^{\prime}_{\sigma}\colon\mathbb{V}_{0}(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma),s_{\sigma})\to\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}
be the projection for each
\sigma
. Then

\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{k,\alpha,I}=\hat{\otimes}_{\sigma}{(\nu^{\prime}_{\sigma})}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{V}_{0}(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma),s_{\sigma})}[k_{\sigma}].

In particular,
\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{k,\alpha,I}
is a line bundle on
\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}
.

Proof.

Denote
\hat{\otimes}_{\sigma}{(\nu^{\prime}_{\sigma})}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{V}_{0}(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma),s_{\sigma})}[k_{\sigma}]
by
\tilde{w}
. Then indeed
\tilde{\omega}\subset\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{k,\alpha,I}
as follows from Lemma
4.32
.

Take a Zariski open
\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}\subset\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}
that trivializes
\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}
. Then (
5
) shows that
{(\nu^{\prime})}^{-1}(\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R})\simeq\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R\langle\{Z_{\sigma}\}_{\sigma\in\Sigma}\rangle}
.

The description of the
\mathfrak{T}
-action on
\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}},s)
in terms of local coordinates imply by
[
AI21
, Lemma 3.9]

\tilde{\omega}(\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R})=R\cdot\prod_{\sigma}k_{\sigma}(1+\beta_{n}Z_{\sigma}).

Denote
\prod_{\sigma}k_{\sigma}(1+\beta_{n}Z_{\sigma})
by
k(1+\beta_{n}Z)
.

If
f\in\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{k,\alpha,I}(\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R})
, then
f/k(1+\beta_{n}Z)\in{R\langle\{Z_{\sigma}\}_{\sigma}\rangle}^{\mathfrak{T}(R)}
. Then the problem reduces to showing that the
\mathfrak{T}
invariant functions are simply
R
.

For the one variable case, this follows from an application of the Weierstrass preparation theorem. If
1+\beta_{n}R
acts on
R\langle Z\rangle
via
t\ast Z=\frac{t-1}{\beta_{n}}+tZ
, then take
f\in{R\langle Z\rangle}
invariant under the action of
1+\beta_{n}R
. Suppose
f=\sum a_{n}Z^{n}
. Then for any
a\in R
,
\sum a_{n}Z^{n}=(1+\beta_{n}a)\ast(\sum a_{n}Z^{n})=\sum a_{n}(a+(1+\beta_{n}a)Z)^{n}
. Letting
Z=0
, we see that
a_{0}=\sum a_{n}a^{n}
for any
a\in R
, which shows that
a_{n}=0
for all
n>0
.

For the general case the result follows by induction on the number of variables. Choose a bijection
\Sigma\simeq\{1,\dots,g\}
. Suppose
f=\sum a_{n}Z_{g}^{n}\in{R\langle Z_{1},\dots,Z_{g}\rangle}^{\mathfrak{T}(R)}
, with
a_{n}\in R\langle Z_{1},\dots,Z_{g-1}\rangle
for all
n
. Then for any element
\lambda=(\lambda_{i})\in\mathfrak{T}(R)
, such that
\lambda_{g}=1
,
f=\lambda\ast f=\sum(\lambda\ast a_{n})Z_{g}^{n}
. This shows that
\lambda\ast a_{n}=a_{n}
for all
n
, and then by induction
a_{n}\in R
. Finally
a_{n}=0
for all
n>0
by the same argument as above.
∎

Remark 4.35
.

The isomorphism classes of
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}}
line bundles can be naturally identified with elements of
H^{1}(\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I},\Res_{\mathcal{O}_{L}/\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{G}_{m})
. The subgroup
H^{1}(\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I},1+\beta_{n}\Res_{\mathcal{O}_{L}/\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{G}_{a})
classifies precisely
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}}
line bundles
\mathscr{L}
with a marked section
s\in\mathscr{L}/\beta_{n}\mathscr{L}
. Thus the isomorphism class of
(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}},\dlog(P_{n}^{\textrm{univ}}))
defines an element of
H^{1}(\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I},\mathfrak{T})
. Then
\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{k,\alpha,I}
defined as above is nothing but its image under the map induced by extension of structural group
H^{1}(\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I},\mathfrak{T})\xrightarrow{k}H^{1}(\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I},\mathbb{G}_{m})
.

Next we give a local description of
\mathbb{W}^{\prime}_{k,\alpha,I}
.

Proposition 4.36
.

Let
\rho^{\prime}_{\sigma}\colon\mathbb{V}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma),s_{\sigma},\mathcal{Q}(\sigma))\to\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}
be the projection for each
\sigma
. Then

\mathbb{W}^{\prime}_{k,\alpha,I}=\hat{\otimes}_{\sigma}(\rho^{\prime}_{\sigma})_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{V}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma),s_{\sigma},\mathcal{Q}(\sigma))}[k_{\sigma}].

Proof.

Let
\tilde{\mathbb{W}}=\hat{\otimes}_{\sigma}(\rho^{\prime}_{\sigma})_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{V}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma),s_{\sigma},\mathcal{Q}(\sigma))}[k_{\sigma}]
. Then clearly
\tilde{\mathbb{W}}\subset\mathbb{W}^{\prime}_{k,\alpha,I}
.

Take a Zariski open
\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}\subset\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}
that trivializes
\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}
compatibly with a trivialization of
\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}
and of
\mathcal{Q}
modulo
\eta
. Choosing coordinates as in the local description (
6
), we see that
(\rho^{\prime})^{-1}(\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R})\simeq\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}\langle\{Z_{\sigma},W_{\sigma}\}_{\sigma\in\Sigma}\rangle
.

The description of
\mathfrak{T}
-action on
\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s,\mathcal{Q})
in local coordinates, together with
[
AI21
, Lemma 3.13]
shows that

\tilde{\mathbb{W}}(\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R})=R\langle\{\frac{W_{\sigma}}{1+\beta_{n}Z_{\sigma}}\}_{\sigma\in\Sigma}\rangle\cdot k(1+\beta_{n}Z)

where we recall from the previous Proposition that
k(1+\beta_{n}Z)=\prod_{\sigma}k_{\sigma}(1+\beta_{n}Z_{\sigma})
. Since
k(1+\beta_{n}Z)
is a unit, in order to prove the reverse inclusion, it will be sufficient to show that
R\langle\{Z_{\sigma},W_{\sigma}\}_{\sigma}\rangle^{\mathfrak{T}(R)}=R\langle\{\frac{W_{\sigma}}{1+\beta_{n}Z_{\sigma}}\}_{\sigma\in\Sigma}\rangle
. We prove this by induction on the cardinality of
\Sigma
.

For the one variable case, this has been proved in loc. cit. For the general case, choose a bijection
\Sigma\simeq\{1,\dots,g\}
. Let
V_{g}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{W_{g}}{1+\beta_{n}Z_{g}}
. The inclusion
R\langle\{Z_{i},W_{i}\}_{i=1}^{g-1}\rangle\langle Z_{g},V_{g}\rangle\to R\langle\{Z_{i},W_{i}\}_{i=1}^{g}\rangle
is an isomorphism of topological rings. Let
f\in R\langle\{Z_{i},W_{i}\}_{i=1}^{g}\rangle^{\mathfrak{T}(R)}
. Write
f=\sum_{n\geq 0}A_{n}(Z_{g})V_{g}^{n}
for
A_{n}(Z_{g})\in R\langle\{Z_{i},W_{i}\}_{i=1}^{g-1}\rangle\langle Z_{g}\rangle
. For any
\lambda\in\mathfrak{T}(R)
with
\lambda_{i}=1
for all
i\neq g
, we have
A_{n}(Z_{g})=A_{n}(\lambda_{g}\ast Z_{g})
for all
n
. Thus for
\lambda_{g}=1+\beta_{n}a
for
a\in R
, we have
A_{n}(Z_{g})=A_{n}(a+\lambda_{g}Z_{g})
. Putting
Z_{g}=0
, we have
A_{n}(0)=A_{n}(a)
for any
a\in R
. The Weierstrass preparation theorem then implies that
A_{n}(Z_{g})=A_{n}(0)\in R\langle\{Z_{i},W_{i}\}_{i=1}^{g-1}\rangle
. Thus
f=\sum A_{n}V_{g}^{n}
with
A_{n}\in R\langle\{Z_{i},W_{i}\}_{i=1}^{g-1}\rangle
. The induction hypothesis then implies that
A_{n}\in R\langle\{\frac{W_{i}}{1+\beta_{n}Z_{i}}\}_{i=1}^{g-1}\rangle
. This proves the claim.
∎

Corollary 4.37
.

Let
\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}\subset\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}
be a Zariski open subset where
\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}
and
\mathcal{Q}
are trivialized. Then with the notation of (
6
),

\mathbb{W}^{\prime}_{k,\alpha,I}(\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R})\simeq R\left\langle\left\{V_{\sigma}\right\}_{\sigma\in\Sigma}\right\rangle\cdot k(1+\beta_{n}Z),\quad V_{\sigma}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{W_{\sigma}}{1+\beta_{n}Z_{\sigma}}.

Let
\nu\colon\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}},s)\xrightarrow{\nu^{\prime}}\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}\xrightarrow{h_{n}}\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
and
\rho\colon\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s,\mathcal{Q})\xrightarrow{\rho^{\prime}}\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}\xrightarrow{h_{n}}\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
be the projections.

Definition 4.38
.

(1)

For
k=k^{0}_{\alpha,I}
define
\mathfrak{w}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\left(\nu_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}},s)}\right)[k]
. This by definition is the sheaf of sections
f\in\nu_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}},s)}
that transform via
k
for the action of
\mathfrak{T}^{\textrm{ext}}
. This is the interpolation sheaf of Hilbert modular forms for the universal weight
k
.

(2)

For
k=k^{0}_{\alpha,I}
define
\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\left(\rho_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s,\mathcal{Q})}\right)[k]
. This by definition is the sheaf of sections
f\in\rho_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s,\mathcal{Q})}
that transform via
k
for the action of
\mathfrak{T}^{\textrm{ext}}
. This is the interpolation sheaf of de Rham classes for the universal weight
k
.

Lemma 4.39
.

\mathfrak{w}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}=(h_{n})_{*}\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{k,\alpha,I}[k]
and
\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}=(h_{n})_{*}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}_{k,\alpha,I}[k]
for the residual action of
(\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}

Proof.

This is clear.
∎

Remark 4.40
.

Note that the universal weight
k=k^{0}_{\alpha,I}
kills the torsion group
\Delta\subset(\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}
. We will later take care of the torsion part of the character and define the interpolation sheaves
\mathfrak{w}_{k,\alpha,I}
and
\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}
for the univeral weight
k_{\alpha,I}\colon(\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}\to\Lambda_{\alpha,I}^{\times}
, as promised in the beginning of the section by tensoring
\mathfrak{w}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}
and
\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}
respectively with an appropriate coherent sheaf on
\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}\times_{\mathfrak{W}^{0}_{\alpha,I}}\mathfrak{W}_{\alpha,I}
. In particular,
\mathfrak{w}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}
and
\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}
will be the restriction of
\mathfrak{w}_{k,\alpha,I}
and
\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}
to the connected component of the trivial character.

Filtration on
\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k}
.

An important result of this work is the following.

Theorem
.

The sheaf
\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}
comes equipped with a natural Hodge filtration induced by the Hodge filtration on
\mathrm{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
such that the Gauss–Manin connection
\nabla
on
\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}
satisfies Griffiths’ transversality with respect to the Hodge filtration.

We define the Hodge filtration on
\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}
later in Lemma
5.2
and prove Griffiths’ transversality for
\nabla
in Theorem
5.5
. The way we show that
\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}
is equipped with a Hodge filtration is by producing a filtration locally on coordinates, and then proving that it glues. But before we prove these results, we will introduce a finer filtration on
\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}
that will eventually help us to prove that the Hodge filtration is well-defined.

Choose a bijection
\Sigma\simeq\{1,\dots,g\}
. Consider the lexicographic order on
\mathbb{N}^{g}
:

(a_{1},\dots,a_{g})>(b_{1},\dots,b_{g})
if and only if

(1)
\sum a_{i}>\sum b_{i}
, or

(2) if
\sum a_{i}=\sum b_{i}
, then for the first index where
a_{i}\neq b_{i}
,
a_{i}>b_{i}
.

Since this defines a well ordering on
\mathbb{N}^{g}
we get an order preserving bijection
\Xi\colon\mathbb{N}\simeq\mathbb{N}^{g}
. This allows us to define a natural filtration on
\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k}
.

Lemma 4.41
.

Let
f_{0}\colon{\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s,\mathcal{Q})}\to{\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}},s)}
be the projection. There is an increasing filtration
\{\mathrm{Fil}_{i}\}_{i\geq 0}
on
{f_{0}}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s,\mathcal{Q})}
with
\mathrm{Fil}_{0}({f_{0}}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s,\mathcal{Q})})=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}},s)}
. On local coordinates as in (
6
),

\mathrm{Fil}_{i}\left({f_{0}}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s,\mathcal{Q})}(\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R})\right)=\sum_{j\leq i}R\langle\{Z_{1},\dots,Z_{g}\}\rangle\cdot W^{\Xi(j)}.

Proof.

We need to show that the local description glues. For
\mathrm{Fil}_{0}
this is obvious by definition. For two different choice of
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes R
-basis of
{\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}}_{|\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.63885pt{R}}
, say
X,Y
and
X^{\prime},Y^{\prime}
, with
X,X^{\prime}
being lifts of the marked section and
Y,Y^{\prime}
being lifts of some generator of
\mathcal{Q}
, we get two different local coordinate description of
\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s,\mathcal{Q})_{|\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.63885pt{R}}
. Since all the filtered pieces contain
\mathrm{Fil}_{0}
, we may assume
X=X^{\prime}
. Then the components of
Y
and
Y^{\prime}
are related by
Y^{\prime}_{i}=u_{i}Y_{i}+a_{i}\eta X_{i}
for all
i
, with
u_{i},a_{i}\in R
. This implies that the isomorphism
R\langle\{Z_{i},W^{\prime}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{g}\rangle\xrightarrow{\sim}R\langle\{Z_{i},W_{i}\}_{i=1}^{g}\rangle
is given by sending
W^{\prime}_{i}\mapsto u_{i}W_{i}+a_{i}(1+\beta_{n}Z_{i})
. Clearly, this isomorphism respects the filtration given by the lexicographic ordering.
∎

Recall
\rho^{\prime}\colon\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s,\mathcal{Q})\to\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}
was the projection.

Lemma 4.42
.

The filtration on
\rho^{\prime}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s,\mathcal{Q})}
induced by taking direct image of the filtration defined in Lemma
4.41
is stable for the action of
\mathfrak{T}
. Therefore, we can define a filtration on
\mathbb{W}^{\prime}_{k,\alpha,I}
as
\mathrm{Fil}_{i}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}_{k,\alpha,I}=\mathrm{Fil}_{i}\left(\rho^{\prime}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s,\mathcal{Q})}\right)[k]
with the property that
\mathrm{Fil}_{0}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}_{k,\alpha,I}=\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{k,\alpha,I}
and
\mathrm{Gr}_{i}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}_{k,\alpha,I}\simeq\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{k,\alpha,I}\otimes{\eta}^{-\ell(i)}(HW\cdot\omega_{\mathcal{A}}^{-2})^{\Xi(i)}
, where for
\Xi(i)=(a_{1},\dots,a_{g})
we let
\ell(i)=\sum a_{k}
, and
HW^{\Xi(i)}=\prod HW(k)^{a_{k}}
.

Proof.

This is clear from the description of the action on local coordinates.
∎

Here we collect a few results that will allow us to prove that
\mathfrak{w}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}
is a line bundle and the filtration on
\mathbb{W}^{\prime}_{k,\alpha,I}
descends to a filtration on
\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}
which then can be realized as the completion of the colimit of its filtered pieces, which are locally free sheaves of finite rank.

Lemma 4.43
.

Let
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{1,r,I}}^{\circ\circ}
be the ideal of topologically nilpotent elements in
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{1,r,I}}
. With
I,r
as fixed in the beginning of the section, for any
2\leq l\leq a+r
,

k(1+p^{l-1}(\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}))-1\subset\mathrm{Hdg}^{\frac{p^{l}-p}{p-1}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{1,r,I}}^{\circ\circ}.

Proof.

[
AIP16a
