, Lemma 4.4]
.
∎

Lemma 4.44
.

The natural
\mathfrak{T}^{\textrm{ext}}
-equivariant map
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}}\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}},s)}
induces an isomorphism

\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}}/q\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{k}/q\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{k}.

Proof.

[
AIP16a
, Lemme 4.5]
.
∎

Lemma 4.45
.

Let
h\colon\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}\to\mathfrak{IG}_{n-1,r,I}
be the projection for any
n
.
h
is finite and the trace map
\mathrm{Tr}_{h}\colon h_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}}\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n-1,r,I}}
induced by the trace on the adic generic fibre satisfies

\mathrm{Hdg}^{p^{n-1}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n-1,r,I}}\subset\mathrm{Tr}_{h}(h_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}})

for any
2\leq n\leq a+r
.

Proof.

[
AIP16a
, Proposition 3.4]
.
∎

Lemma 4.46
.

Let
\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}\subset\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
be an open where
\mathrm{Hdg}
is trivialized. Letting
c_{0}=1
, for every
1\leq n\leq a+r
, there exists
c_{n}\in\mathrm{Hdg}^{-\frac{p^{n}-p}{p-1}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}}(\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R})
satisfying
\mathrm{Tr}_{h}(c_{n})=c_{n-1}
.

Proof.

Immediate from Lemma
4.45
.
∎

Recall
\rho\colon\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s,\mathcal{Q})\to\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
was the projection and
\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}=\rho_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s,\mathcal{Q})}[k]
.

Theorem 4.47
.

The action of
\mathfrak{T}^{\textrm{ext}}
on
\rho_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s,\mathcal{Q})}
preserves the filtration
\{\mathrm{Fil}_{i}\}_{i}
induced by taking direct image of the filtration defined in Lemma
4.41
. Let
\mathrm{Fil}_{i}\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}=\mathrm{Fil}_{i}\left(\rho_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s,\mathcal{Q})}\right)[k]
.

(1)

\mathrm{Fil}_{i}\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}
is a finite locally free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}}
-module.

(2)

\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}
is the
\alpha
-adic completion of
\varinjlim_{i}\mathrm{Fil}_{i}\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}
.

(3)

\mathrm{Fil}_{0}\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}=\mathfrak{w}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}
and
\mathrm{Gr}_{i}\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}\simeq\mathfrak{w}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}\otimes\eta^{-\ell(i)}(HW\cdot\omega_{\mathcal{A}}^{-2})^{\Xi(i)}
.

Proof.

We already know the similar results for
\mathbb{W}^{\prime}_{k,\alpha,I}
. Also it is clear that
\mathrm{Fil}_{0}
is preserved by the
\mathfrak{T}^{\textrm{ext}}
-action and
\mathrm{Fil}_{0}\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}=\mathfrak{w}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}
. By Lemma
4.39
,
\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}=(h_{n})_{*}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}_{k,\alpha,I}[k]
for the residual action of
(\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}
. The idea is to pick generators of
\mathrm{Gr}_{i}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}_{k,\alpha,I}
and modify them to produce generators of
\mathrm{Gr}_{i}\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}
. To that intent, recall we have a
\mathfrak{T}^{\textrm{ext}}
equivariant isomorphism
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}}/q\simeq\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{k,\alpha,I}/q
by Lemma
4.44
. Let
\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}\subset\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
be a Zariski open that trivializes
\omega_{\mathcal{A}}
and let
\omega
be a
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}}
generator. Let
V
be the pullback of
\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}
to
\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}
. This gives a
\mathfrak{T}^{\textrm{ext}}
equivariant isomorphism

\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}}(V)/q\otimes\eta^{-\ell(i)}(HW\cdot\omega_{\mathcal{A}}^{-2})^{\Xi(i)}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Gr}_{i}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}_{k}(V)/q=\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}_{k}(V)/q\otimes\eta^{-\ell(i)}(HW\cdot\omega_{\mathcal{A}}^{-2})^{\Xi(i)}.

Let
\bar{s}_{i}
be the image of the class of
\eta^{-\ell(i)}(HW\cdot\omega_{\mathcal{A}}^{-2})^{\Xi(i)}
. In particular
t\ast\bar{s}_{i}=\bar{s}_{i}
for all
t\in(\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}
, since
\eta^{-\ell(i)}(HW\cdot\omega_{\mathcal{A}}^{-2})^{\Xi(i)}
is defined over
\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
. Pick a lift
s_{i}
of
\bar{s}_{i}
to
\mathrm{Fil}_{i}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}_{k,\alpha,I}
.

Choose lifts
\tilde{\tau}
of
\tau\in(\mathcal{O}_{L}/p^{n}\mathcal{O}_{L})^{\times}
in
(\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}
. With
c_{n}
as in Lemma
4.46
, define

\tilde{s}_{i}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\sum_{\tau\in(\mathcal{O}_{L}/p^{n}\mathcal{O}_{L})^{\times}}k(\tilde{\tau})^{-1}\tau(c_{n}s_{i})\in\mathrm{Hdg}^{-\frac{p^{n}-p}{p-1}}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}_{k,\alpha,I}(V).

We claim that
\tilde{s}_{i}\in\mathrm{Fil}_{i}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}_{k,\alpha,I}(V)
and its image generates
\mathrm{Gr}_{i}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}_{k,\alpha,I}
. Moreover since
(\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}
acts on
\tilde{s}_{i}
via
k
, it descends to
\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k}
. To prove the claim we note that

\displaystyle\tilde{s}_{i}-s_{i}

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{\tau\in(\mathcal{O}_{L}/p^{n}\mathcal{O}_{L})^{\times}}k(\tilde{\tau})^{-1}\tau(c_{n}s_{i})\right)-s_{i}

\displaystyle\in\sum_{\tau\in(\mathcal{O}_{L}/p^{n}\mathcal{O}_{L})^{\times}}k(\tilde{\tau})^{-1}(\tau(c_{n}s_{i})-\tau(c_{n})s_{i})+R^{\circ\circ}\mathrm{Fil}_{i}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}_{k,\alpha,I}

\displaystyle\subset\sum_{\tau\in(\mathcal{O}_{L}/p^{n}\mathcal{O}_{L})^{\times}}k(\tilde{\tau})^{-1}\tau(c_{n})(\tau(s_{i})-s_{i})+R^{\circ\circ}\mathrm{Fil}_{i}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}_{k,\alpha,I}

\displaystyle\subset R^{\circ\circ}\mathrm{Fil}_{i}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}_{k,\alpha,I}+\mathrm{Fil}_{i-1}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}_{k,\alpha,I}.

Here we used the fact that
\sum k(\tilde{\tau})^{-1}\tau(c_{n})\in 1+R^{\circ\circ}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}}
whose proof we refer to
[
AIP18
, Lemme 5.4]
.
The fact that
\tilde{s}_{i}
generates
\mathrm{Gr}_{i}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}_{k,\alpha,I}
follows by noting that it does so modulo
R^{\circ\circ}
.

So we have produced a local basis of
\mathrm{Fil}_{i}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}_{k,\alpha,I}
for each
i
that descends to
\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}
. This proves that the filtration on
\rho_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s,\mathcal{Q})}
is preserved by the
\mathfrak{T}^{\textrm{ext}}
-action and so the filtration on
\mathbb{W}^{\prime}_{k,\alpha,I}
descend to a filtration on
\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}
. The rest of the claims in the theorem follow immediately.
∎

Let
\chi\colon\Delta\subset(\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}\xrightarrow{k^{\textrm{un}}}\Lambda_{\alpha,I}^{\times}
be the finite part of the universal character. For
p\neq 2
,
\Delta=(\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes Z_{p})^{\times}/1+p(\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes Z_{p})
and for
p=2
,
\Delta
is a quotient of
(\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes Z_{p})^{\times}/1+4(\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. Using this we view
\chi
as a character of
(\mathcal{O}_{L}/q\mathcal{O}_{L})^{\times}
. Let
p\colon\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{r,\alpha,I}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}\times_{\mathfrak{W}^{0}_{\alpha,I}}\mathfrak{W}_{\alpha,I}\to\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
be the projection induced by the finite flat base change
\mathfrak{W}_{\alpha,I}\to\mathfrak{W}^{0}_{\alpha,I}
.

Definition 4.48
.

For
i=1
if
p\neq 2
and
i=2
if
p=2
, define a coherent sheaf
\mathfrak{w}^{\chi}_{k,\alpha,I}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\left(p^{*}(f_{i})_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{i,r,I}}\right)[\chi^{-1}]
for the action of
(\mathcal{O}_{L}/q\mathcal{O}_{L})^{\times}
on
f_{i}\colon\mathfrak{IG}_{i,r,I}\to\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
.

Definition 4.49
.

(1)

Define the sheaf of overconvergent Hilbert modular forms of weight
k=k^{\textrm{un}}
to be
\mathfrak{w}_{k,\alpha,I}=p^{*}\mathfrak{w}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}\otimes\mathfrak{w}^{\chi}_{k,\alpha,I}
.

(2)

Define the sheaf of overconvergent de Rham classes to be
\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}=p^{*}\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}\otimes\mathfrak{w}^{\chi}_{k,\alpha,I}
.

Proposition 4.50
.

The sheaf
\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}
is equipped with a filtration by coherent
\mathcal{O}_{\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{r,\alpha,I}}
modules
\mathrm{Fil}_{i}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}
and moreover
\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}
is the
\alpha
-adic completion of
\varinjlim_{i}\mathrm{Fil}_{i}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}
. We have
\mathrm{Fil}_{0}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}=\mathfrak{w}_{k,\alpha,I}
and
\mathrm{Gr}_{i}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}\simeq\mathfrak{w}_{k,\alpha,I}\otimes\eta^{-\ell(i)}(HW\cdot\omega_{\mathcal{A}}^{-2})^{\Xi(i)}
.

Proof.

Define
\mathrm{Fil}_{i}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=p^{*}\mathrm{Fil}_{i}\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}\otimes\mathfrak{w}^{\chi}_{k,\alpha,I}
. The rest of the claims follow immediately from Theorem
4.47
.
∎

We remarked in the introduction to the section that the construction of
\mathfrak{w}_{k,\alpha,I}
appears in the previous work of Andreatta–Iovita–Pilloni
[
AIP16a
]
. Here we compare our construction to theirs and show why we get isomorphic sheaves.

In
[
AIP16a
]
the authors consider a torsor
\mathfrak{F}_{n,r,I}
over
\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}
for the group
\mathfrak{T}
. This torsor is defined on points
\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}\xrightarrow{\gamma}\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}
for any normal admissible
\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}
-algebra
R
as follows.

\mathfrak{F}_{n,r,I}(R)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\{\omega\in\omega_{\mathcal{A}}\,|\,\omega=\gamma^{*}(s)\in\gamma^{*}\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}/\beta_{n}\}.

The action of
\mathfrak{T}
on
\mathfrak{F}_{n,r,I}
is the obvious one, i.e.
\lambda\in\mathfrak{T}(R)
acts via
\lambda\ast\omega=\lambda\omega
. Moreover there is an action of
\mathfrak{T}^{\textrm{ext}}
on
\mathfrak{F}_{n,r,I}
over
\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
. This is given by first noting that any point of
\mathfrak{F}_{n,r,I}(R)
can be seen as a pair
(P,\omega)
where
P\in H^{\vee}_{n}(R)
and
\omega\in\omega_{\mathcal{A}}(R),\omega=\dlog(P)\textrm{ mod }\beta_{n}
. Then
\lambda\in(\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}
acts via
\lambda\ast(P,\omega)=(\bar{\lambda}P,\lambda\omega)
, where
\bar{\lambda}
is the class of
\lambda
in
(\mathcal{O}_{L}/p^{n}\mathcal{O}_{L})^{\times}
. Then they define the sheaf of Hilbert modular forms for universal weight
k=k^{0}_{\alpha,I}
as follows. Let
\nu^{\prime}\colon\mathfrak{F}_{n,r,I}\to\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
be the projection.

Definition 4.51
.

The sheaf of Hilbert modular forms for weight
k=k^{0}_{\alpha,I}
is defined as

\mathfrak{w}^{0,\textrm{old}}_{k,\alpha,I}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\nu^{\prime}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{F}_{n,r,I}}[k^{-1}]

for the action of
\mathfrak{T}^{\textrm{ext}}
.

We now show why the sheaf
\mathfrak{w}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}
is naturally isomorphic to
\mathfrak{w}^{0,\textrm{old}}_{k,\alpha,I}
.

Proposition 4.52
.

There is an isomorphism of formal schemes over
\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
,
a\colon\mathfrak{F}_{n,r,I}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}},s)
. This isomorphism interacts with the
\mathfrak{T}^{\textrm{ext}}
action in the following manner: for any point
x\in\mathfrak{F}_{n,r,I}(R)
,
\lambda\ast a(x)=a(\lambda^{-1}\ast x)
.

Proof.

Define
a
by sending a point
(P,\omega)\mapsto(P,\omega^{\vee})
. Then it is easy to check the rest of the claims.
∎

Proposition 4.53
.

There is a natural isomorphism
\mathfrak{w}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}\simeq\mathfrak{w}^{0,\textrm{old}}_{k,\alpha,I}
.

Proof.

Let
\nu\colon\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}},s)\to\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
be the projection. Then the isomorphism induced by
a
,

\nu_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}},s)}\simeq\nu^{\prime}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{F}_{n,r,I}}

induces an isomorphism
\mathfrak{w}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}\simeq\mathfrak{w}^{0,\textrm{old}}_{k,\alpha,I}
due to Proposition
4.52
.
∎

Here we recall an important result about the surjectivity of the specialization map for cusp forms.

Let
\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}^{*}\to\mathfrak{W}^{0}_{\alpha,I}
be the blow-up spaces constructed exactly as
\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
but now starting from the minimal compactification
M^{*}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})
. These are formal models for overconvergent neighbourhoods of
M^{*}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})
. Let
\mathfrak{M}^{*}_{r,\alpha,I}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}^{*}\times_{\mathfrak{W}^{0}_{\alpha,I}}\mathfrak{W}_{\alpha,I}
. There is a natural map
f\colon\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{r,\alpha,I}\to\mathfrak{M}^{*}_{r,\alpha,I}
induced by the projection
\bar{M}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})\to M^{*}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})
. Let
\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I},\mathcal{M}^{*}_{r,\alpha,I}
be their adic generic fibre. Recall that
D
was the boundary divisor of
\bar{M}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})
. By an abuse of notation we denote by
D
its inverse image in
\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{r,\alpha,I}
.

Theorem 4.54
.

We have
R^{i}f_{*}\mathfrak{w}_{k,\alpha,I}(-D)=0
for all
i>0
. Let
g\colon\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I}\to\mathcal{W}_{\alpha,I}
be the projection to the weight space. Then for any weight
\kappa\in\mathcal{W}_{\alpha,I}
,

\kappa^{*}g_{*}\mathfrak{w}_{k,\alpha,I}(-D)[1/\alpha]=H^{0}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I},\kappa^{*}\mathfrak{w}_{k,\alpha,I}(-D)[1/\alpha])

is the space of
r
-overconvergent Hilbert cuspforms of tame level
\mu_{N}
,
\mathfrak{c}
-polarization and weight
\kappa
.

Proof.

The first part follows from
[
AIP16
, Corollary 3.20]
. For the second part we remark that the projection
g
factors through
\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I}\xrightarrow{f}\mathcal{M}^{*}_{r,\alpha,I}\to\mathcal{W}_{\alpha,I}
, and
\mathcal{M}^{*}_{r,\alpha,I}
is affinoid. Then the claim follows from the first part.
∎

4.5.
Overconvergent arithmetic Hilbert modular forms

Recall that we defined the notion of arithmetic Hilbert modular forms in §
2.2.1
. These were Hilbert modular forms associated to the group
G=\Res_{L/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbf{GL}_{2,L}
. With
\Gamma=\mathcal{O}_{L}^{\times,+}/U_{N}^{2}
, we saw that the quotient
\bar{M}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})\to\bar{M}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})/\Gamma
is finite étale. Given a classical weight
(v,w)\in\mathfrak{W}^{G}
the sheaf of arithmetic Hilbert modular forms of tame level
\mu_{N}
,
\mathfrak{c}
-polarization and weight
(v,w)
with coefficients in
R
was defined to be
\underline{\omega}^{(v,w)}_{R}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(p_{*}\omega^{k}_{\mathcal{A},R})^{\Gamma}
. The definition of overconvergent arithmetic Hilbert modular forms is given in a similar manner. We follow
[
AIP16a
]
.

Let
\bar{\mathfrak{M}}^{G}_{r,\alpha,I}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{r,\alpha,I}\times_{\mathfrak{W}}\mathfrak{W}^{G})/\Gamma
. By Proposition
2.4
the quotient map
p\colon\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{r,\alpha,I}\times_{\mathfrak{W}}\mathfrak{W}^{G}\to\bar{\mathfrak{M}}^{G}_{r,\alpha,I}
is finite étale. Consider the pullback of
\mathfrak{w}_{k,\alpha,I}
along
f\colon\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{r,\alpha,I}\times_{\mathfrak{W}}\mathfrak{W}^{G}\to\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{r,\alpha,I}
. The action of
\Gamma
on
\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{r,\alpha,I}\times_{\mathfrak{W}}\mathfrak{W}^{G}
can be lifted to an action on
f^{*}\mathfrak{w}_{k,\alpha,I}
as follows. Let
(v_{\alpha,I},w_{\alpha,I})\colon(\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\to(\Lambda_{\alpha,I}\hat{\otimes}_{\Lambda}\Lambda^{G})^{\times}=\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}(\Lambda^{G}_{\alpha,I})^{\times}
be the universal character. Viewing a section
g
of
f^{*}\mathfrak{w}_{k,\alpha,I}
by Koecher’s principle as a rule that associates to any tuple
(A,\iota,\lambda,\psi,\omega)
a value
g(A,\iota,\lambda,\psi,\omega)\in\Lambda^{G}_{\alpha,I}
, the action of
\epsilon
is given by

(\epsilon\cdot g)(A,\iota,\lambda,\psi,\omega)=v_{\alpha,I}(\epsilon^{-1})g(A,\iota,\epsilon\lambda,\psi,\omega).

Definition 4.55
.

The sheaf of
r
-overconvergent arithmetic Hilbert modular forms of tame level
\mu_{N}
,
\mathfrak{c}
-polarization and weight
k^{G}_{\alpha,I}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(v_{\alpha,I},w_{\alpha,I})
is defined to be

\mathfrak{w}^{G,\mathfrak{c}}_{k,\alpha,I}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(p_{*}f^{*}\mathfrak{w}_{k,\alpha,I})^{\Gamma}.

Similarly one defines the sheaf of
r
-overconvergent arithmetic Hilbert cuspforms of tame level
\mu_{N}
,
\mathfrak{c}
-polarization and weight
k^{G}_{\alpha,I}
to be

\mathfrak{w}^{G,\mathfrak{c}}_{k,\alpha,I}(-D)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(p_{*}f^{*}\mathfrak{w}_{k,\alpha,I}(-D))^{\Gamma}.

We have a surjectivity result about the specialization map of arithmetic Hilbert cuspforms analogous to Theorem
4.54
.

Let
\bar{\mathcal{M}}^{G}_{r,\alpha,I}
be the adic generic fibre of
\bar{\mathfrak{M}}^{G}_{r,\alpha,I}
. Let
g\colon\bar{\mathcal{M}}^{G}_{r,\alpha,I}\to\mathcal{W}^{G}_{\alpha,I}
be the projection to the weight space, where
\mathcal{W}^{G}_{\alpha,I}=\Spa(\Lambda^{G}_{\alpha,I}[1/\alpha],\Lambda^{G}_{\alpha,I})
.

Theorem 4.56
.

For any weight
k^{G}\in\mathcal{W}^{G}_{\alpha,I}
,
(k^{G})^{*}\mathfrak{w}^{G,\mathfrak{c}}_{k,\alpha,I}(-D)[1/\alpha]
is the space of
r
-overconvergent arithmetic Hilbert cuspforms of tame level
\mu_{N}
,
\mathfrak{c}
-polarization and weight
k^{G}
.

Proof.

We need to show that
R^{i}g_{*}\mathfrak{w}^{G,\mathfrak{c}}_{k,\alpha,I}(-D)[1/\alpha]=0
for all
i>0
. This follows from Theorem
4.54
by noting that over the generic fibre applying the invariant functor
(\cdot)^{\Gamma}
to a Čech resolution of
p_{*}f^{*}\mathfrak{w}_{k,\alpha,I}(-D)
is exact since
(\cdot)^{\Gamma}
is obtained by the application of the projector
e=\frac{1}{\#\Gamma}\sum_{\epsilon\in\Gamma}\epsilon
.
∎

Let
g_{\mathfrak{c}}
denote the projection to the weight space
\mathfrak{W}^{G}_{\alpha,I}
from the formal model
\bar{\mathfrak{M}}^{G}_{r,\alpha,I}
corresponding to the moduli of abelian schemes with
\mathfrak{c}
-polarization. For
x\in L^{\times,+}
coprime to
p
, consider the isomorphism
L_{(x\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{c})}\colon{g_{x\mathfrak{c}}}_{*}\mathfrak{w}^{G,x\mathfrak{c}}_{k,\alpha,I}\xrightarrow{}{g_{\mathfrak{c}}}_{*}\mathfrak{w}^{G,\mathfrak{c}}_{k,\alpha,I}
given by

L_{(x\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{c})}(f)(A,\iota,\lambda,\psi,\omega)=v_{\alpha,I}(x)f(A,\iota,x\lambda,\psi,\omega).

The isomorphism depends only on the principal ideal
(x)
and preserves cuspidality.

Definition 4.57
.

Define the sheaf of
r
-overconvergent arithmetic Hilbert modular forms of tame level
\mu_{N}
and weight
k^{G}_{\alpha,I}
to be

\mathfrak{w}^{G}_{k,\alpha,I}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\left(\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{c}\in\textrm{Frac}(L)^{(p)}}{g_{\mathfrak{c}}}_{*}\mathfrak{w}^{G,\mathfrak{c}}_{k,\alpha,I}\right)/\left(L_{(x\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{c})}(f)-f\right)_{x\in\textrm{Princ}(L)^{+,(p)}}.

One defines similarly the subsheaf of
r
-overconvergent arithmetic Hilbert cuspforms, which we denote by
\mathfrak{w}^{G}_{k,\alpha,I}(-D)
. (Note the
D
in the notation does not have anything to do any boundary divisor, but we choose this notation to stay consistent with our previous notation when the polarization module was fixed and we were working over a fixed toroidal compactification.)

We can analogously define arithmetic overconvergent de Rham sheaves. With notation as before, let
\gamma_{\epsilon}\colon\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{r,\alpha,I}\times_{\mathfrak{W}}\mathfrak{W}^{G}\to\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{r,\alpha,I}\times_{\mathfrak{W}}\mathfrak{W}^{G}
be given by sending
(A,\iota,\lambda,\psi)\mapsto(A,\iota,\epsilon\lambda,\psi)
for any
\epsilon\in\Gamma
. The identity
[1]\colon A\to A
induces an isomorphism
[1]^{*}\colon f^{*}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}\to{\gamma_{\epsilon}}_{*}f^{*}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}
. Define the action of
\epsilon
on
p_{*}f^{*}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}
to be
\epsilon\cdot s=v_{\alpha,I}(\epsilon)[1]^{*}s
.

Definition 4.58
.

The sheaf of
r
-overconvergent arithmetic de Rham classes of tame level
\mu_{N}
,
\mathfrak{c}
-polarization and weight
k^{G}_{\alpha,I}=(v_{\alpha,I},w_{\alpha,I})
is defined to be

\mathbb{W}^{G,\mathfrak{c}}_{k,\alpha,I}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(p_{*}f^{*}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I})^{\Gamma}.

Denote by
\mathbb{W}^{G,\mathfrak{c}}_{k,\alpha,I}(-D)
the subsheaf of cuspidal classes.

For
x\in L^{\times,+}
coprime to
p
, let
\gamma_{x}\colon\bar{\mathfrak{M}}^{G,\mathfrak{c}}_{r,\alpha,I}\to\bar{\mathfrak{M}}^{G,x\mathfrak{c}}_{r,\alpha,I}
be given by sending
(A,\iota,\lambda\mathcal{O}_{L}^{\times,+},\psi)\mapsto(A,\iota,x\lambda\mathcal{O}_{L}^{\times,+},\psi)
. The identity
[1]\colon A\to A
induces an isomorphism
[1]^{*}\colon\mathbb{W}^{G,x\mathfrak{c}}_{k,\alpha,I}\to{\gamma_{x}}_{*}\mathbb{W}^{G,\mathfrak{c}}_{k,\alpha,I}
. Define the map
L_{(x\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{c})}\colon{g_{x\mathfrak{c}}}_{*}\mathbb{W}^{G,x\mathfrak{c}}_{k,\alpha,I}\to{g_{\mathfrak{c}}}_{*}\mathbb{W}^{G,\mathfrak{c}}_{k,\alpha,I}
by
L_{(x\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{c}}(s)=v_{\alpha,I}(x^{-1})[1]^{*}s
.

Definition 4.59
.

Define the sheaf of
r
-overconvergent arithmetic de Rham classes of tame level
\mu_{N}
and weight
k^{G}_{\alpha,I}=(v_{\alpha,I},w_{\alpha,I})
to be

\mathbb{W}^{G}_{k,\alpha,I}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\left(\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{c}\in\textrm{Frac}(L)^{(p)}}{g_{\mathfrak{c}}}_{*}\mathbb{W}^{G,\mathfrak{c}}_{k,\alpha,I}\right)/\left(L_{(x\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{c})}(s)-s\right)_{x\in\textrm{Princ}(L)^{+,(p)}}.

Denote by
\mathbb{W}^{G}_{k,\alpha,I}(-D)
the subsheaf of cuspidal classes.

5.
p
-adic iteration of the Gauss–Manin connection

In this section we will define iteration of the Gauss–Manin connection for analytic weights. For simplicity of notation we will ignore the log poles at the cusps. The first main result of this section is the following.

Theorem
.

There is a filtration
\{\mathrm{Fil}_{i}\}_{i\geq 0}
on
\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}
such that the graded pieces over the generic fibre are
\mathrm{Gr}_{n}\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}[1/\alpha]\simeq\mathfrak{w}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}\otimes\Sym^{n}\omega_{\mathcal{A}}^{-\otimes 2}[1/\alpha]
. There is a connection

\nabla_{k}\colon\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}[1/\alpha]\to\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}\hat{\otimes}\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}/\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}}[1/\alpha]

induced by the Gauss–Manin connection on
\mathrm{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
, which satisfies Griffiths’ transversality with respect to the filtration
\mathrm{Fil}_{i}
above. Moreover, it also induces a connection

\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}[1/\alpha]\to\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}\hat{\otimes}\Omega^{1}_{\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{r,\alpha,I}/\Lambda_{\alpha,I}}[1/\alpha]

that satisfies Griffiths’ transversality with respect to the filtration defined by tensoring
\mathrm{Fil}_{i}
with
\mathfrak{w}^{\chi}_{k,\alpha,I}
.

5.1.
The Gauss–Manin connection on
\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}

Let
\mathcal{IG}^{\prime}_{n,r,I}\to\mathcal{IG}_{n,r,I}
be the analytic adic space classifying trivializations
(\mathcal{O}_{L}/p^{n}\mathcal{O}_{L})^{2}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{A}[p^{n}]^{\vee}
compatible with the trivializations
\mathcal{O}_{L}/p^{n}\mathcal{O}_{L}\xrightarrow{\sim}H_{n}^{\vee}
. Let
\mathfrak{IG}^{\prime}_{n,r,I}\to\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}
be the normalization.

Proposition 5.1
.

The Gauss–Manin connection on
\mathrm{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
over
\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}^{\prime}
restricts to a connection

\nabla\colon\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}\to\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}\hat{\otimes}\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}^{\prime}/\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}}

such that
(\nabla\textrm{ mod }\beta_{n})(s)=0
and
(\nabla\textrm{ mod }\eta)(\mathcal{Q})\subset\mathcal{Q}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}^{\prime}/\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}}/(\eta)
.

Proof.

We have a commutative diagram as follows coming from the functoriality of the
\dlog
map.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{O}_{L}/p^{n}\mathcal{O}_{L})^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{\mu_{p^{n}}^{2}}\otimes\mathfrak{d}^{-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}[p^{n}]^{\vee}(\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{\mathcal{A}}/p^{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dlog}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dlog}

The bottom arrow
\dlog_{\mathcal{A}[p^{n}]^{\vee}}
composed with the projection on to
\omega_{\mathcal{A}}/\beta_{n}\simeq\omega_{H_{n}}/\beta_{n}
factors through
\mathcal{A}[p^{n}]^{\vee}\to H_{n}^{\vee}\xrightarrow{\dlog}\omega_{H_{n}}
. The connection on
\mathrm{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
modulo
p^{n}
is the connection
\bar{\nabla}
on the invariant differentials of the universal vector extension of
\mathcal{A}[p^{n}]^{\vee}
. Since
\mu_{p^{n}}
is isotrivial, the functoriality of the Gauss–Manin connection and the commutativity of the diagram shows that
\bar{\nabla}(\dlog(P^{\textrm{univ}}_{n}))=0
. This shows that
\nabla(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}})=0\textrm{ mod }\beta_{n}\mathrm{H}_{\mathcal{A}}\hat{\otimes}\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}^{\prime}/\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}}
. In particular
\nabla(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}})\subset\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}\hat{\otimes}\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}^{\prime}/\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}}
.

On the other hand since
\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}=\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}+\underline{\xi}\widetilde{HW}\cdot\mathrm{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
we are left to show that
\nabla
maps
\underline{\xi}\widetilde{HW}\cdot\mathrm{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
to
\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}
. Since the Gauss–Manin connection is functorial, it commutes with the splitting of
\mathrm{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
into the
g
different components. Recalling the notation from Corollary
4.8
we need to show that

\nabla\left(\underline{\xi}(i_{j})\widetilde{HW}(i_{j})\cdot\mathrm{H}_{\mathcal{A}}(i_{j})\right)\subset\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}(i_{j})\hat{\otimes}\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}^{\prime}/\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}}.

To show this note that by Lemma
4.10
,
\underline{\xi}(i_{j})\widetilde{HW}(i_{j})=\tilde{F}^{*}(\underline{\xi}(i_{j-1}))
is a
p
-th power at all height 1 localizations. Thus
\nabla
maps it to
\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}+p\mathrm{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
. Since
p\in\underline{\xi}(i_{j})\widetilde{HW}(i_{j})
for all
i_{j}
we conclude. This proves the first two claims of the proposition. Now we show that
(\nabla\textrm{ mod }\eta)(\mathcal{Q})\subset\mathcal{Q}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}^{\prime}/\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}}/(\eta)
.

Let
\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R_{0}}\subset\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
be an open such that
\mathrm{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
is trivialized over
\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}
as
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes R_{0}
-modules. Let
\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}\subset\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}
and
\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}^{\prime}\subset\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}^{\prime}
be its inverse image in
\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}
and
\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}^{\prime}
respectively. Assume also that
\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}
is trivialized over
\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}
. Pick
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes R_{0}
-basis
\omega,\zeta
of
\mathrm{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
such that
e\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\xi\omega
is a lift of
s
for some local generator
\xi
of
\underline{\xi}
and such that
f\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=Ce+(\xi HW)\zeta
is a lift of a generator of
\mathcal{Q}
for some local generator
HW
of
\widetilde{HW}
and some
C\in\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes R
as in the proof of Proposition
4.12
. Assume also that the image of
\zeta
in
\omega_{\mathcal{A}}^{\vee}
is the dual of
\omega
.

Let
\omega_{\sigma},\zeta_{\sigma}
be the
\sigma
-components of
\omega
and
\zeta
. Let
\Theta_{\sigma}=KS(\omega_{\sigma},\zeta_{\sigma})
be the Kodaira–Spencer class in
\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}/\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}}
corresponding to the image of
\omega_{\sigma}^{\otimes 2}
under the Kodaira–Spencer isomorphism

KS\colon\omega_{\mathcal{A}}^{\otimes 2}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}/\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}}.

The
\Theta_{\sigma}
thus form a basis for
\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}/\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}}
. Suppose that

(7)

\displaystyle\nabla(\omega_{\sigma})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\tau}\omega_{\sigma}\otimes\alpha^{\sigma}_{\tau}\Theta_{\tau}+\zeta_{\sigma}\otimes\Theta_{\sigma}

(8)

\displaystyle\nabla(\zeta_{\sigma})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\tau}\omega_{\sigma}\otimes\beta^{\sigma}_{\tau}\Theta_{\tau}+\sum_{\tau}\zeta_{\sigma}\otimes\gamma^{\sigma}_{\tau}\Theta_{\tau}

for
\alpha^{\sigma}_{\tau},\beta^{\sigma}_{\tau},\gamma^{\sigma}_{\tau}\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}}
.
Therefore we have

(9)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\nabla(e_{\sigma})&=\nabla(\xi({\sigma})\cdot\omega_{\sigma})\\
&=\xi(\sigma)\sum_{\tau}\omega_{\sigma}\otimes\alpha^{\sigma}_{\tau}\Theta_{\tau}+\xi(\sigma)\cdot\zeta_{\sigma}\otimes\Theta_{\sigma}+\xi(\sigma)\cdot\omega_{\sigma}\otimes\dlog\xi(\sigma)\\
&=\omega_{\sigma}\otimes\left(\xi(\sigma)\sum_{\tau}\alpha^{\sigma}_{\tau}\Theta_{\tau}+\mathrm{d}\xi(\sigma)\right)+\zeta_{\sigma}\otimes\xi(\sigma)\Theta_{\sigma}\\
&=e_{\sigma}\otimes\left(\sum_{\tau}\alpha^{\sigma}_{\tau}\Theta_{\tau}+\dlog\xi(\sigma)-\frac{C_{\sigma}\Theta_{\sigma}}{HW(\sigma)}\right)+f_{\sigma}\otimes\frac{\Theta_{\sigma}}{HW(\sigma)}\end{split}

(10)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\nabla(f_{\sigma})&=\nabla(C_{\sigma}e_{\sigma}+\xi(\sigma)HW(\sigma)\cdot\zeta_{\sigma})\\
&=\nabla(C_{\sigma}e_{\sigma})+\xi(\sigma)HW(\sigma)\sum_{\tau}\omega_{\sigma}\otimes\beta^{\sigma}_{\tau}\Theta^{\tau}+\xi(\sigma)HW(\sigma)\sum_{\tau}\zeta_{\sigma}\otimes\gamma^{\sigma}_{\tau}\Theta_{\tau}\\
&+\xi(\sigma)HW(\sigma)\cdot\zeta_{\sigma}\otimes\dlog(\xi(\sigma)HW(\sigma))\\
&=\nabla(C_{\sigma}e_{\sigma})+e_{\sigma}\otimes\left(\sum_{\tau}HW(\sigma)\beta^{\sigma}_{\tau}\Theta_{\tau}-C_{\sigma}\sum_{\tau}\gamma^{\sigma}_{\tau}\Theta_{\tau}-\dlog(\xi(\sigma)HW(\sigma))\right)\\
&+f_{\sigma}\otimes\left(\sum_{\tau}\gamma^{\sigma}_{\tau}\Theta_{\tau}+\dlog(\xi(\sigma)HW(\sigma))\right)\end{split}

Now the proof of the previous part of the proposition shows that over
\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}^{\prime}
,
\nabla(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}})\subset\beta_{n}\mathrm{H}_{\mathcal{A}}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}^{\prime}/\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}}
. Then the third equality of (
9
) implies that
\Theta_{\sigma}\in\beta_{n}\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}^{\prime}/\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}}
for all
\sigma
. In other words the image of
\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}/\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}}\to\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}^{\prime}/\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}}
is contained in
\beta_{n}\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}^{\prime}/\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}}
. Moreover the same is true of
\mathrm{d}\xi(\sigma)
. This implies together with the explicit formula of (
10
) that
\nabla(f_{\sigma})\equiv 0\textrm{ mod }(\eta)
, proving the final part of the proposition.
∎

5.2.
The Gauss–Manin connection on
\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}

Recall that the universal character
k=k^{0}_{\alpha,I}
is analytic on
1+p^{n-1}(\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. In particular there are
u_{\sigma}\in p^{1-n}\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}
such that
k_{\sigma}(t)=\exp(u_{\sigma}\log(t))
for all
t\in 1+\beta_{n}\mathbb{G}_{a}
. In this section we will define the connection on
\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}
. We would like to have Griffith’s transversality for some filtration on
\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}
. But the filtration given in Theorem
4.47
, i.e. the filtration given by lexicographic ordering for a choice of numbering of the set
\Sigma
, doesn’t satisfy this. Thus as promised before, here we define the Hodge filtration on
\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}
.

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
\rho^{\prime}\colon\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s,\mathcal{Q})\to\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}
and
h_{n}\colon\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}\to\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
be the projections. Then the filtration on
\rho^{\prime}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s,\mathcal{Q})}
defined on local coordinates
\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}\subset\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}
by

\mathrm{Fil}_{i}\left(\rho^{\prime}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s,\mathcal{Q})}(\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R})\right)=\mathrm{Fil}_{i}R\langle\{Z_{\sigma},W_{\sigma}\}_{\sigma\in\Sigma}\rangle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\bigoplus_{j=0}^{i}R\langle\{Z_{\sigma}\}_{\sigma\in\Sigma}\rangle\otimes_{R}\Sym^{j}{R[W_{\sigma}]_{\sigma\in\Sigma}}

is well-defined. Moreover,
(h_{n})_{*}\mathrm{Fil}_{i}
is stable under the action of
\mathfrak{T}^{\textrm{ext}}
for all
i
. In particular it induces a filtration on
\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}
, by defining
\mathrm{Fil}_{i}\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathrm{Fil}_{i}\left(\rho_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s,\mathcal{Q})}\right)[k]
, where
\rho=h_{n}\circ\rho^{\prime}
. This is defined to be the Hodge filtration. The graded pieces over the generic fibre are
\mathrm{Gr}_{i}\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}[1/\alpha]\simeq\mathfrak{w}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}\otimes\Sym^{i}\omega_{\mathcal{A}}^{-\otimes 2}[1/\alpha]\simeq\mathfrak{w}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}\otimes\Sym^{i}(\oplus_{\sigma}\omega_{\mathcal{A}}(-2\sigma))[1/\alpha]
.

Proof.

We note that
\oplus_{j=0}^{i}\Sym^{j}R[W_{\sigma}]_{\sigma\in\Sigma}
contains all polynomials in
\{W_{\sigma}\}_{\sigma\in\Sigma}
of degree
\leq i
. In particular, choosing an ordering
\Xi\colon\Sigma\simeq\{1,\dots,g\}
, there is a greatest element in
\Sym^{i}R[W_{\sigma}]_{\sigma\in\Sigma}
corresponding to the multi-index
(i,0,\dots,0)
. Let
i_{0}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\Xi^{-1}(i,0,\dots,0)
. Denoting by
\mathrm{Fil}_{i}^{\prime}
the lexicographic ordering of Theorem
4.47
, we then have
\mathrm{Fil}_{i}=\mathrm{Fil}_{i_{0}}^{\prime}
. The lemma follows.
∎

Convention:
Henceforth, unless otherwise stated
\{\mathrm{Fil}_{i}\}_{i\geq 0}
will denote the Hodge filtration of Lemma
5.2
, and
not
the filtration induced by a lexicographic ordering on
\Sigma
. Also we will denote by
\mathrm{Fil}_{i}
the filtration induced on
\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}
by tensoring the above filtration with
\mathfrak{w}^{\chi}_{k,\alpha,I}
.

Let
\mathcal{P}^{(1)}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}^{\prime}/\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}}
be the first infinitesimal neighborhood of the closed subscheme of
\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}^{\prime}\times_{\mathfrak{W}^{0}_{\alpha,I}}\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}^{\prime}
defined by the diagonal embedding
\Delta\colon\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}^{\prime}\xhookrightarrow{}\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}^{\prime}\times_{\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.63885pt{\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}}}\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}^{\prime}
. Let
p_{1},p_{2}
be the first and second projections
\mathcal{P}^{(1)}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}^{\prime}/\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}}\to\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}^{\prime}
. Then using Grothendieck’s formalism of connections
[
BO15
, §2]
, we get an
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{P}^{(1)}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}^{\prime}/\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}}}
-linear isomorphism
\epsilon^{\sharp}\colon p^{*}_{2}\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}\xrightarrow{\sim}p_{1}^{*}\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}
associated to the connection
\nabla\colon\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}\to\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}^{\prime}/\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}}
. This
\epsilon^{\sharp}
is characterized by the properties that
\Delta^{*}\epsilon^{\sharp}=\textrm{id}
,
\nabla(x)=\epsilon^{\sharp}(1\otimes x)-x\otimes 1
and it satisfies a suitable cocycle condition with respect to the three possible pullbacks of
\epsilon^{\sharp}
to
\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}^{\prime}\times_{\mathfrak{W}^{0}_{\alpha,I}}\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}^{\prime}\times_{\mathfrak{W}^{0}_{\alpha,I}}\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}^{\prime}
.

Let
\tilde{\rho}\colon\vartheta^{*}\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s,\mathcal{Q})\to\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}^{\prime}
be the pullback of
\rho^{\prime}\colon\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s,\mathcal{Q})\to\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}
to
\vartheta\colon\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}^{\prime}\to\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}
. Sometimes we will drop the notation
\vartheta
for simplicity.

Lemma 5.3
.

The connection
\nabla\colon\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}\to\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}\hat{\otimes}\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}^{\prime}/\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}}
induces an isomorphism associated to a connection (in the sense of Grothendieck) on
\tilde{\rho}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s,\mathcal{Q})}
.

\epsilon_{0}^{\sharp}\colon p_{2}^{*}\tilde{\rho}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s,\mathcal{Q})}\xrightarrow{\sim}p_{1}^{*}\tilde{\rho}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s,\mathcal{Q})}

Proof.

The isomorphism
\epsilon^{\sharp}\colon p_{2}^{*}\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}\xrightarrow{\sim}p_{1}^{*}\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}
splits by
\mathcal{O}_{L}
-linearity into
\epsilon^{\sharp}_{\sigma}\colon p_{2}^{*}\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma)\xrightarrow{\sim}p_{1}^{*}\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma)
for all
\sigma\in\Sigma
. Let
\tilde{\rho}_{\sigma}\colon\mathbb{V}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma),s_{\sigma},\mathcal{Q}(\sigma))\to\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}^{\prime}
be the
\sigma
-component of
\tilde{\rho}
. Each
\epsilon^{\sharp}_{\sigma}
induces a connection
\epsilon^{\sharp}_{\sigma,0}\colon p_{2}^{*}{(\tilde{\rho}_{\sigma})}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{V}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma),s_{\sigma},\mathcal{Q}(\sigma))}\xrightarrow{\sim}p_{1}^{*}{(\tilde{\rho}_{\sigma})}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{V}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma),s_{\sigma},\mathcal{Q}(\sigma))}
by
[
AI21
, §2.4]
. Then
\epsilon^{\sharp}_{0}
is defined by the tensor product
\otimes\epsilon^{\sharp}_{\sigma,0}
.
∎

Lemma 5.4
.

The action of
(\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}
on
\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s,\mathcal{Q})
over
\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
as defined in §
4.3.1
can be lifted to an action on
\vartheta^{*}\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s,\mathcal{Q})
over
\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
such that the induced action commutes with
\epsilon^{\sharp}_{0}
.

Proof.

The map
\mathcal{IG}^{\prime}_{n,r,I}\to\mathcal{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
is a torsor for the group

\begin{pmatrix}(\mathcal{O}_{L}/p^{n}\mathcal{O}_{L})^{\times}&\mu_{p^{n}}\otimes\mathfrak{d}^{-1}\\
0&(\mathcal{O}_{L}/p^{n}\mathcal{O}_{L})^{\times}\end{pmatrix}.

Then
(\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}
acts on
\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}^{\prime}
through the quotient

(\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}\to\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&(\mathcal{O}_{L}/p^{n}\mathcal{O}_{L})^{\times}\end{pmatrix}

and this action lifts the action on
\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}
. For
\lambda\in(\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}
we get an isomorphism
[\lambda]\colon\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}^{\prime}\to\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}^{\prime}
over
\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
, that induces an isomorphism
\gamma_{\lambda}\colon\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}\to\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}
sending the marked section
s\mapsto\bar{\lambda}^{-1}s
and the marked subspace
\mathcal{Q}
to itself. Since the connection on
\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}
is induced by the Gauss–Manin connection on
\mathrm{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
, by functoriality of the Gauss–Manin connection,
\nabla
commutes with
\gamma_{\lambda}
. The last claim follows by noticing that the action on
\vartheta^{*}\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s,\mathcal{Q})
is induced by the isomorphism
\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}\xrightarrow{\gamma_{\lambda}}\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}\xrightarrow{\times\lambda}\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}
which obviously commutes with
\nabla
.
∎

Theorem 5.5
.

There is an integrable connection on
\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}
,

\nabla_{k}\colon\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}\to\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}\hat{\otimes}\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}/\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}}[1/\alpha]

for which the filtration on
\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}
defined in Lemma
5.2
satisfies Griffith’s transversality.

Proof.

We use the notation of the proof of Proposition
5.1
. Recall
\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}^{\prime}\subset\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}^{\prime}
was an open that was the inverse image of an open
\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}_{0}\subset\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
that trivializes
\mathrm{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
, and such that over
\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}^{\prime}
,
\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}
is trivial with
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes R^{\prime}
-basis
\{e,f\}
adapted to the marked section
s
and modified unit root subspace
\mathcal{Q}
. Let
I(\Delta)=\ker{(R^{\prime}\hat{\otimes}_{\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}}R^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\textrm{mult}}R^{\prime})}
, and let
R^{(1)}=R^{\prime}\hat{\otimes}_{\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}}R^{\prime}/I(\Delta)^{2}
.

Then in terms of the basis
\{e,f\}
,
\epsilon^{\sharp}
is given by a matrix

A=\begin{pmatrix}a&&b\\
c&&d\end{pmatrix}\in\mathrm{GL}_{2}\left(\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes R^{(1)}\right).

Decomposing into components, we get matrices

A_{\sigma}=\begin{pmatrix}a_{\sigma}&&b_{\sigma}\\
c_{\sigma}&&d_{\sigma}\end{pmatrix}\in\mathrm{GL}_{2}\left(R^{(1)}\right)

with respect to the basis
e_{\sigma},f_{\sigma}
of
\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma)
for each
\sigma
.

Since
\Delta^{*}(\epsilon^{\sharp})=\textrm{id}
, we have that
a_{\sigma}=1+a^{0}_{\sigma}
and
d_{\sigma}=1+d^{0}_{\sigma}
with
a^{0}_{\sigma},d^{0}_{\sigma},b_{\sigma},c_{\sigma}\in I(\Delta)
for all
\sigma
. Moreover, the squares of
a^{0}_{\sigma},d^{0}_{\sigma},b_{\sigma},c_{\sigma}
are all
0
in
R^{(1)}
.

Comparing the expression of
\nabla
in terms of
a,b,c,d
on the one hand and that in (
9
) and (
10
) on the other, we see that
c_{\sigma}HW(\sigma)
is the Kodaira–Spencer class
\Theta_{\sigma}
.

By Lemma
5.3
there is an isomorphism
\epsilon^{\sharp}_{0}\colon p_{2}^{*}\tilde{\rho}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s,\mathcal{Q})}\xrightarrow{\sim}p_{1}^{*}\tilde{\rho}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s,\mathcal{Q})}
induced by
\epsilon^{\sharp}\colon p_{2}^{*}\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}\simeq p_{1}^{*}\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}
. We show that
\epsilon^{\sharp}_{0}
restricts to a connection on
\mathbb{W}^{\prime}_{k,\alpha,I}
. We show this on local coordinates. So recalling the local description of
\mathbb{W}^{\prime}_{k,\alpha,I}
from Corollary
4.37
, we have
{\mathbb{W}^{\prime}_{k,\alpha,I}}_{|\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.63885pt{R^{\prime}}}=R^{\prime}\langle\{V_{\sigma}\}\rangle k(1+\beta_{n}Z)
, where we recall
V_{\sigma}=\frac{W_{\sigma}}{1+\beta_{n}Z_{\sigma}}
and
k(1+\beta_{n}Z)
was the notation for
\prod_{\sigma}k_{\sigma}(1+\beta_{n}Z_{\sigma})
. Thus
\epsilon^{\sharp}_{0}
is described by its action on
V_{\sigma}
and
1+\beta_{n}Z_{\sigma}
. We have

\epsilon^{\sharp}_{0}(V_{\sigma})=\eta^{-1}(b_{\sigma}+\eta d_{\sigma}V_{\sigma})(a_{\sigma}+\eta c_{\sigma}V_{\sigma})^{-1}\quad\epsilon^{\sharp}_{0}(1+\beta_{n}Z_{\sigma})=(a_{\sigma}+\eta c_{\sigma}V_{\sigma})(1+\beta_{n}Z_{\sigma}).

From this one can deduce the following formula for
\nabla_{k}(x)=\epsilon^{\sharp}_{0}(1\otimes x)-x\otimes 1
.

(11)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\nabla_{k}\left(\prod_{\sigma}V^{i_{\sigma}}_{\sigma}\cdot k(1+\beta_{n}Z)\right)=&\prod_{\sigma}V_{\sigma}^{i_{\sigma}}\Bigg(\sum_{\sigma}i_{\sigma}V_{\sigma}^{-1}\otimes b_{\sigma}\eta^{-1}+\sum_{\sigma}(u_{\sigma}-i_{\sigma})\otimes a^{0}_{\sigma}\\
&+\sum_{\sigma}i_{\sigma}\otimes d^{0}_{\sigma}+\sum_{\sigma}(u_{\sigma}-i_{\sigma})V_{\sigma}\otimes\eta c_{\sigma}\Bigg)\big(k(1+\beta_{n}Z)\otimes 1\big)\end{split}

This connection descends to
\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}
after inverting
\alpha
, by the formula above. Then we descend
\nabla_{k}
to
\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
by taking
k
-invariants for the
(\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}
action using Lemma
5.4
and noting
\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}\to\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
is generically étale.
∎

Corollary 5.6
.

The connection
\nabla_{k}\colon\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}\to\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}\hat{\otimes}\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}/\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}}[1/\alpha]
induces a connection which we still denote by
\nabla_{k}

\nabla_{k}\colon\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}\to\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}\hat{\otimes}\Omega^{1}_{\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{r,\alpha,I}/\Lambda_{\alpha,I}}[1/\alpha]

that satisfies Griffiths transverality with respect to the filtration on
\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}
defined by tensoring
\mathrm{Fil}_{i}
of Lemma
5.2
with
\mathfrak{w}^{\chi}_{k,\alpha,I}
.

Proof.

Recall
\mathfrak{w}^{\chi}_{k,\alpha,I}
was defined as
\left((f_{i})_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{i,r,I}}\otimes_{\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}}\Lambda_{\alpha,I}\right)[\chi^{-1}]
for
f_{i}\colon\mathfrak{IG}_{i,r,I}\to\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
the projection with
i=1
if
p\neq 2
and
i=2
if
p=2
. The universal derivation
(f_{i})_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{i,r,I}}\otimes_{\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}}\Lambda_{\alpha,I}\to(f_{i})_{*}\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{i,r,I}/\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}}\otimes\Lambda_{\alpha,I}
commutes with the action of
(\mathcal{O}_{L}/q\mathcal{O}_{L})^{\times}
and thus induces a connection
\mathfrak{w}^{\chi}_{k,\alpha,I}\to\mathfrak{w}^{\chi}_{k,\alpha,I}\hat{\otimes}\Omega^{1}_{\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{r,\alpha,I}}[1/\alpha]
by taking
\chi^{-1}
-invariants and upon inverting
\alpha
. This along with
\nabla_{k}
defined on
\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}
above induces the required connection on
\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}
.
∎

The Kodaira–Spencer isomorphism
\omega_{\mathcal{A}}^{\otimes 2}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}/\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}}
induces a decomposition of
\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}/\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}}
corresponding to the decomposition
\omega_{\mathcal{A}}^{\otimes 2}=\prod_{\sigma}\omega_{\mathcal{A}}^{2\sigma}
. Here the tensor product is taken as
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{{\mathfrak{X}_{\alpha,I}}}
-modules. This induces an isomorphism
\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}}[1/\alpha]\simeq\prod_{\sigma}\omega_{\mathcal{A}}^{2\sigma}[1/\alpha]
.

Definition 5.7
.

Define
\nabla_{k}(\sigma)\colon\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}\to\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k+2\sigma,\alpha,I}[1/\alpha]
as the map obtained by composing
\nabla_{k}
with the projection onto the
\sigma
component of
\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}/\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}}[1/\alpha]\simeq\prod_{\sigma}\omega_{\mathcal{A}}^{2\sigma}
, followed by the natural map to
\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k+2\sigma,\alpha,I}
.

\nabla_{k}(\sigma)\colon\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}\xrightarrow{\nabla_{k}}\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}\hat{\otimes}\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}/\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}}[1/\alpha]\xrightarrow{}\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}\hat{\otimes}\omega_{\mathcal{A}}^{2\sigma}[1/\alpha]\xrightarrow{}\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k+2\sigma,\alpha,I}[1/\alpha]

Similarly denote still by
\nabla_{k}(\sigma)\colon\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}\to\mathbb{W}_{k+2\sigma,\alpha,I}[1/\alpha]
the map obtained by twisting
\nabla_{k}(\sigma)
as above with the connection on
\mathfrak{w}^{\chi}_{k,\alpha,I}
followed by the projection onto the
\sigma
-component under the Kodaira–Spencer isomorphism.

Corollary 5.8
.

The
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}_{r,\alpha,I}}
-linear map induced by the connection
\nabla_{k}(\sigma)
on the graded piece

\nabla_{k}(\sigma)\colon\mathrm{Gr}_{n}\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}[1/\alpha]\to\mathrm{Gr}_{n+1}\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k+2\sigma,\alpha,I}[1/\alpha]

sends an element
\omega^{k-2\mathbf{i}}\in\mathfrak{w}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}\otimes\Sym^{n}\omega_{\mathcal{A}}^{-\otimes 2}[1/\alpha]
to
(u_{\sigma}-i_{\sigma})\omega^{k-2\mathbf{i}}
for
\mathbf{i}=(i_{\tau})_{\tau\in\Sigma}\in\mathbb{N}^{g}
.

Proof.

Follows from (
11
) above.
∎

5.3.
\nabla_{k}
on
q
-expansions

For simplicity of notation, in this section we drop
\alpha,I
from the notation
\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}
and simply write
\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k}
. Also since we want to iterate the connection, and the connection
\nabla_{k}(\sigma)
maps
\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k}[1/\alpha]
to
\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k+2\sigma}[1/\alpha]
, in our notation we forget the dependency of the connection on the weight
k
, and simply write
\nabla(\sigma)
. Later we will need to compose
\nabla(\sigma)
and
\nabla(\tau)
for
\sigma\neq\tau\in\Sigma
. Lemma
5.10
below will show us that the order of composition does not matter.

In this section we will study the effect of
\nabla_{k}
on
q
-expansions. We begin by reviewing the definition of Tate objects for Hilbert–Blumenthal abelian varieties following
[
Kat78
]
.

Fix fractional ideals
\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}
such that
\mathfrak{c}=\mathfrak{a}\mathfrak{b}^{-1}
. Let
S
be a set of
g
linearly independent
\mathbb{Q}
-linear forms
\ell_{i}\colon L\to\mathbb{Q}
, such that
\ell_{i}(x)>0
for all
x\gg 0
, where by
x\gg 0
we mean
x
is a totally positive element. We say an element is
S
-positive if
\ell_{i}(x)\geq 0
for all
i
. Let
\mathfrak{ab}_{S}=\{x\in\mathfrak{ab}\,|\,x\textrm{ is }S\textrm{-positive}\}
be the set of
S
-positive elements in
\mathfrak{ab}
.
\mathfrak{ab}_{S}
is a finitely generated monoid.

Definition 5.9
.

Define
\mathbb{Z}\llbracket\mathfrak{ab},S\rrbracket
to be the ring of all formal series
\sum_{\beta\in\mathfrak{ab}_{S}}a_{\beta}q^{\beta}
. Define
\mathbb{Z}(\!(\mathfrak{ab},S)\!)=\mathbb{Z}\llbracket\mathfrak{ab},S\rrbracket[1/q^{\beta}]
for some
\beta\gg 0
.

We remark that inverting
q^{\beta}
for some
\beta\gg 0
inverts
q^{\gamma}
for all
\gamma\gg 0
. So
\mathbb{Z}(\!(\mathfrak{ab},S)\!)
is well-defined. In particular,
\mathbb{Z}(\!(\mathfrak{ab},S)\!)
is the collection of all formal series
\sum_{\beta\in\mathfrak{ab}}a_{\beta}q^{\beta}
such that for some integer
n\gg 0
, we have
\ell_{i}(\beta)\geq-n
whenever
a_{\beta}\neq 0
.

Over the ring
\mathbb{Z}(\!(\mathfrak{ab},S)\!)
, we have the
g
-dimensional algebraic torus
\mathbb{G}_{m}\otimes\mathfrak{d}^{-1}\mathfrak{a}^{-1}
together with an
\mathcal{O}_{L}
-linear group homomorphism
\underline{q}\colon\mathfrak{b}\to\mathbb{G}_{m}\otimes\mathfrak{d}^{-1}\mathfrak{a}^{-1}
defined as follows. To give such a group homomorphism is the same as giving an
\mathcal{O}_{L}
-linear group homomorphism
\mathfrak{ab}\to\mathbb{G}_{m}\otimes\mathfrak{d}^{-1}
. This is equivalent to giving a group homomorphism
\mathfrak{ab}\to\mathbb{G}_{m}
which we define to be
\beta\mapsto q^{\beta}\in\mathbb{G}_{m}(\mathbb{Z}(\!(\mathfrak{ab},S)\!))
. The rigid analytic quotient
\mathbb{G}_{m}\otimes\mathfrak{d}^{-1}\mathfrak{a}^{-1}/\underline{q}(\mathfrak{b})
is algebraizable to a Hilbert–Blumenthal abelian variety denoted
\textrm{Tate}_{\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}}(q)
over
\mathbb{Z}(\!(\mathfrak{ab},S)\!)
which carries a canonical
\mathfrak{c}=\mathfrak{a}\mathfrak{b}^{-1}
polarization

\lambda_{\textrm{can}}\colon\textrm{Tate}_{\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}}(q)^{\vee}\xrightarrow{\sim}\textrm{Tate}_{\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}}(q)\otimes\mathfrak{a}\mathfrak{b}^{-1}\simeq\textrm{Tate}_{\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{a}}(q).

We quickly recall that there exists canonical isomorphisms as follows
[
Kat78
, (1.1.17), (1.1.18)]
.

(1)

\omega_{\textrm{Tate}_{\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}}(q)}\simeq\mathfrak{a}\otimes\mathbb{Z}(\!(\mathfrak{ab},S)\!)
;
\omega^{\vee}_{\textrm{Tate}_{\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}}(q)}\simeq\mathfrak{d}^{-1}\mathfrak{a}^{-1}\otimes\mathbb{Z}(\!(\mathfrak{ab},S)\!)
.

(2)

\Omega^{1}_{\mathbb{Z}(\!(\mathfrak{ab},S)\!)}\simeq\mathfrak{ab}\otimes\mathbb{Z}(\!(\mathfrak{ab},S)\!)
;
\Der\Big(\mathbb{Z}(\!(\mathfrak{ab},S)\!),\mathbb{Z}(\!(\mathfrak{ab},S)\!)\Big)\simeq\mathfrak{d^{-1}a^{-1}b^{-1}}\otimes\mathbb{Z}(\!(\mathfrak{ab},S)\!)
.

We now base change to
\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}
, so that
\textrm{Tate}_{\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}}(q)
is defined over
R\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}(\!(\mathfrak{ab},S)\!)
.

For simplicity assume
\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}
are coprime to
p
. Everything that follows holds true with appropriate modifications in the general case by choosing an isomorphism
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}\simeq\mathfrak{a}^{-1}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}
which amounts to choosing a
\Gamma_{00}(p^{\infty})
-structure on
\textrm{Tate}_{\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}}(q)

[
Kat78
, (1.1.15)]
. When
\mathfrak{a}
is coprime to
p
, we have the natural equality
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}=\mathfrak{a}^{-1}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}
inside
L\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}
which induces a canonical
\Gamma_{00}(p^{\infty})
-structure on
\textrm{Tate}_{\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}}(q)
.

For any
\sigma\in\Sigma
, let
e_{\sigma}\in\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes R
be the corresponding idempotent. Let
\omega_{\textrm{can}}(\sigma)
be the image of
e_{\sigma}\in\mathfrak{a}\otimes R=\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes R
under the canonical identification
\omega_{\textrm{Tate}_{\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}}(q)}\simeq\mathfrak{a}\otimes R
. Let
\Theta_{\sigma}=KS(\omega_{\textrm{can}}^{\otimes 2}(\sigma))
be the corresponding Kodaira–Spencer class. Then
\Theta_{\sigma}
is the image of
e_{\sigma}\in\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes R
under the identification
\Omega^{1}_{R/\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}}\simeq\mathfrak{ab}\otimes R=\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes R
. The homomorphism
e_{\sigma}^{\vee}\colon\Omega^{1}_{R/\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}}\to R
that is dual to
e_{\sigma}
, induces the derivation
\theta_{\sigma}\in\Der(R,R)
defined as

\theta_{\sigma}\Big(\sum a_{\beta}q^{\beta}\Big)=\sum\sigma(\beta)a_{\beta}q^{\beta}.

Having recalled generalities about the Tate objects, we go back to computing the effect of
\nabla_{k}
on
q
-expansions.

Let
\nabla(\omega_{\textrm{can}}(\sigma))=\zeta_{\textrm{can}}(\sigma)\otimes\Theta_{\sigma}
. Then
\nabla(\zeta_{\textrm{can}}(\sigma))=0
. Let
\omega_{\textrm{can}}
and
\zeta_{\textrm{can}}
be the
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes R
-basis of
\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\textrm{Tate}_{\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}}(q)}={H}^{1}_{\textrm{dR}}(\textrm{Tate}_{\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}}(q)/R)
, whose
\sigma
-components are
\omega_{\textrm{can}}(\sigma)
and
\zeta_{\textrm{can}}(\sigma)
respectively. With respect to this basis the matrix of
\nabla=(\nabla_{\sigma})_{\sigma}
is thus given as follows.

\nabla_{\sigma}=\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
\Theta_{\sigma}&0\end{pmatrix}

(Note
\nabla_{\sigma}\colon\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\textrm{Tate}_{\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}}(q)}(\sigma)\to\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\textrm{Tate}_{\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}}(q)}(\sigma)\hat{\otimes}\Omega^{1}_{R/\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}}
is just the
\sigma
component of
\nabla
. In particular, it should not be confused with
\nabla_{k}(\sigma)
of Definition
5.7
).

Let
\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k}(q)
be the pullback of
\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k}
to
\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}
along the structure morphism defining
\textrm{Tate}_{\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}}(q)
together with the canonical
\Gamma_{00}(p^{n})
-structure defined as above. Then we can write
\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k}(q)\simeq R\langle\{V_{\sigma}\}_{\sigma}\rangle\cdot k(1+p^{n}Z)
as in Corollary
4.37
. Then formula (
11
) gives us

(12)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\nabla_{k}(\sigma)\left(a\prod_{\tau}V_{\tau}^{i_{\tau}}\cdot k(1+p^{n}Z)\right)&=\theta_{\sigma}(a)\prod_{\tau}V_{\tau}^{i_{\tau}}\cdot(k+2\sigma)(1+p^{n}Z)\\
&+p(u_{\sigma}-i_{\sigma})V_{\sigma}\prod_{\tau}V_{\tau}^{i_{\tau}}\cdot(k+2\sigma)(1+p^{n}Z)\end{split}

for any
a\in R
.

Lemma 5.10
.

For any
\sigma,\tau\in\Sigma
, the maps
\nabla_{k}(\sigma)
and
\nabla_{k}(\tau)
commute, i.e.

\nabla_{k+2\sigma}(\tau)\circ\nabla_{k}(\sigma)=\nabla_{k+2\tau}(\sigma)\circ\nabla_{k}(\tau)

as maps
\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k}\to\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k+2\sigma+2\tau}[1/\alpha]
.

Proof.

It is enough to check this on the ordinary locus
\mathfrak{X}_{\alpha,I}^{\textrm{ord}}
, as the ordinary locus is dense in
\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
. On the ordinary locus the result follows by verifying on
q
-expansions using (
12
) and the
q
-expansion principle. (See also
[
Kat78
, (2.1.14)]
)
∎

Lemma 5.11
.

Let
g(q)\in R
and
N\geq 1
. Then we can write

\nabla(\sigma)^{N}\Big(g(q)\prod_{\tau}V_{\tau}^{i_{\tau}}\cdot k(1+p^{n}Z)\Big)=\sum_{j=0}^{N}p^{j}a_{N,k,i_{\sigma},j}\theta_{\sigma}^{N-j}(g(q))V_{\sigma}^{j}\prod_{\tau}V_{\tau}^{i_{\tau}}\cdot(k+2N\sigma)(1+p^{n}Z).

Here
a_{N,k,i_{\sigma},0}=1
and for
j\geq 1
, we have

a_{N,k,i_{\sigma},j}=\binom{N}{j}\prod_{i=1}^{j-1}(u_{\sigma}-i_{\sigma}+N-1-i).

Proof.

This is a similar computation as
[
AI21
, Lemma 3.38]
.
∎

Let
{\mathbb{W}}^{0}_{k}(\sigma)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\sum_{n}\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k+2n\sigma}
and let
\mathbb{W}^{0}=\rho_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s,\mathcal{Q})}
where
\rho\colon\mathbb{V}^{\mathcal{O}_{L}}_{0}(\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}},s,\mathcal{Q})\to\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
is the projection. Let
\mathbb{W}_{k}(\sigma)=\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k}(\sigma)\otimes\mathfrak{w}^{\chi}_{k,\alpha,I}
and let
\mathbb{W}=\mathbb{W}^{0}\otimes\mathfrak{F}
. Here
\mathfrak{F}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(f_{i})_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{i,r,I}}\otimes_{\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}}\Lambda_{\alpha,I}
, where
f_{i}\colon\mathfrak{IG}_{i,r,I}\to\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
is the projection with
i=1
for
p\neq 2
and
i=2
otherwise. For each prime
\mathfrak{P}|p
, we have defined the
U_{\mathfrak{P}}
operator in the next section (Definition
6.3
) which we will use now.

Corollary 5.12
.

Let
f_{\sigma}
be the inertia degree for the embedding
\sigma
, and suppose that
\sigma
induces the
\mathfrak{P}
-adic valuation on
\mathcal{O}_{L}
for a prime
\mathfrak{P}|p
. Let
k=\chi\cdot k^{0}
with
\chi=k|_{\Delta}
the torsion part of the character and
k^{0}=k\chi^{-1}
. Assume
k^{0}_{\sigma}(t)=\exp(u_{\sigma}\log{t})
for
t\in 1+\beta_{n}\mathbb{G}_{a}
and
u_{\sigma}\in\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}
. Let
\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{\alpha,I}^{\textrm{ord}}=\mathfrak{X}_{\alpha,I}^{\textrm{ord}}\times_{\mathfrak{W}^{0}_{\alpha,I}}\mathfrak{W}_{\alpha,I}
. For any
g\in H^{0}(\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{\alpha,I}^{\textrm{ord}},\mathbb{W}_{k})^{U_{\mathfrak{P}}=0}
,

(\nabla(\sigma)^{p^{f_{\sigma}}-1}-\textrm{{id}})(g)\in pH^{0}\big(\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{\alpha,I}^{\textrm{ord}},{\mathbb{W}}\big)\cap H^{0}\big(\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{\alpha,I}^{\textrm{ord}},\mathbb{W}_{k}(\sigma)\big).

Proof.

We recall that
\mathbb{W}_{k}=\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k^{0}}\otimes\mathfrak{w}^{\chi}_{k,\alpha,I}
and the connection
\nabla
on
\mathbb{W}_{k}
is defined by the composite of the connection on
\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k^{0}}
as defined in Theorem
5.5
and the connection on
\mathfrak{w}^{\chi}_{k,\alpha,I}
which is defined by the universal derivation on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{i,r,I}}
for
i
as above. Let
\Lambda_{\alpha,I}(\!(\mathfrak{ab},S)\!)=\Lambda_{\alpha,I}\otimes_{\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}}R
. The base change of
\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{\Lambda_{\alpha,I}}(\!(\mathfrak{ab},S)\!)
to
\mathfrak{IG}_{i,r,I}\to\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
is just copies of
\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{\Lambda_{\alpha,I}}(\!(\mathfrak{ab},S)\!)
indexed by
(\mathcal{O}_{L}/q\mathcal{O}_{L})^{\times}
. Hence the universal derivation on
{\Lambda_{\alpha,I}}(\!(\mathfrak{ab},S)\!)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{i,r,I}}
is determined by the universal derivation on
{\Lambda_{\alpha,I}}(\!(\mathfrak{ab},S)\!)
. The
q
-expansion of any section
g\in H^{0}(\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{r,\alpha,I},\mathbb{W})^{U_{\mathfrak{P}}=0}
at
\textrm{Tate}_{\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}}(q)
corresponds to a tuple
(g_{j})_{j\in(\mathcal{O}_{L}/q\mathcal{O}_{L})^{\times}}
with
g_{j}\in\Lambda_{\alpha,I}(\!(\mathfrak{ab},S)\!)\langle\{V_{\sigma}\}_{\sigma}\rangle\cdot k^{0}(1+p^{n}Z)
. Moreover, for each
j
,
U_{\mathfrak{P}}(g_{j})=0
. By the
q
-expansion principle it will be enough to prove the corollary for
g=g(q)\prod_{\tau}V^{i_{\tau}}_{\tau}\cdot k^{0}(1+p^{n}Z)
for
g(q)\in\Lambda_{\alpha,I}(\!(\mathfrak{ab},S)\!)
, such that
g(q)
is
\mathfrak{P}
-depleted. By Lemma
5.11
, it is enough to show
\theta_{\sigma}^{p^{f_{\sigma}}-1}(g(q))\cdot(k+2(p^{f_{\sigma}}-1)\sigma)(1+p^{n}Z)\equiv g(q)k(1+p^{n}Z)\textrm{ mod }p
, which is clear.
∎

5.4.
Iteration of
\nabla

In this section we will finally define the
p
-adic iteration of the Gauss–Manin connection. We begin with a preparatory lemma.

Lemma 5.13
.

For
I=[p^{a},p^{b}]
,

(1)

\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}=\mathcal{O}_{K}[[T_{1},\dots,T_{g}]]\langle\frac{p}{\alpha},\frac{T_{1}}{\alpha},\dots,\frac{T_{g}}{\alpha},u,v\rangle/({\alpha}^{p^{a}}v-p,uv-{\alpha}^{p^{b-a}})
if
b\neq\infty
.

(2)

\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}=\mathcal{O}_{K}[[T_{1},\dots,T_{g}]]\langle\frac{p}{\alpha},\frac{T_{1}}{\alpha},\dots,\frac{T_{g}}{\alpha},u\rangle/({\alpha}^{p^{a}}u-p)
if
b=\infty
.

Let
U\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{A}
be a Zariski open in
\mathfrak{X}
where
\omega_{\mathcal{A}}
is trivial. Then

U\times_{\mathfrak{X}}\mathfrak{X}_{r,I}=\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{A\hat{\otimes}\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}\langle w\rangle/(w\mathrm{Hdg}^{p^{r+1}}}-\alpha).

Proof.

See
[
AIP16a
, §3.4.1]
.
∎

Lemma 5.14
.

Let
C_{1}=\#(\mathcal{O}_{L}/p\mathcal{O}_{L})^{\times}
. Then the kernel of the restriction map
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{1,r,I}}/(\alpha^{j})\xrightarrow{\phi_{1}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}^{\textrm{\emph{ord}}}_{1,I}}/(\alpha^{j})

is killed by
\mathrm{Hdg}^{j(p^{r+1})+C}
. The kernel of the restriction map
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}}/(\alpha^{j})\xrightarrow{\phi_{n}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,I}^{\textrm{ord}}}/(\alpha^{j})
is killed by
\mathrm{Hdg}^{j(p^{r+1})+C_{n}}
where
C_{n}=C_{1}+\frac{p^{n}-p}{p-1}
.

Proof.

The formulas of Lemma
5.13
show that the kernel of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{r,I}}/(\alpha^{j})\xrightarrow{\phi_{0}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}^{\textrm{ord}}_{\alpha,I}}/(\alpha^{j})
is killed by
\mathrm{Hdg}^{j(p^{r+1})}
. The trace map
\mathrm{Tr}\colon\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{1,r,I}}\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{r,I}}
then gives a commutative diagram as follows.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ker{\phi_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{1,r,I}}/(\alpha^{j})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}^{\textrm{ord}}_{1,I}}/(\alpha^{j})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ker{\phi_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{r,I}}/(\alpha^{j})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}^{\textrm{ord}}_{\alpha,I}}/(\alpha^{j})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Tr}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Tr}}

Suppose
x\in\ker{\phi_{1}}
. Then
\mathrm{Tr}(x)\in\ker{\phi_{0}}
and hence
\mathrm{Tr}(\mathrm{Hdg}^{j(p^{r+1})}x)=0
. In other words, for any lift
\tilde{x}\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{1,r,I}}
of
x
,
\mathrm{Tr}(\mathrm{Hdg}^{j(p^{r+1})}\tilde{x})\in\alpha^{j}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{r,I}}
. Let
\mathfrak{D}^{-1}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\{y\in\textrm{Frac}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{1,r,I}})\,|\,\mathrm{Tr}(yz)\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{r,I}}\textrm{ for all }z\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{1,r,I}}\}
. Then
\mathrm{Hdg}^{j(p^{r+1})}\tilde{x}\in\alpha^{j}\mathfrak{D}^{-1}
as
\ker{\phi_{1}}
is an ideal. By using normality of the rings involved, the first claim then follows by localizing at height 1 primes and noting that
\mathfrak{D}^{-1}
is the usual inverse different in such tamely ramified extensions of DVR’s. For the second part, we note that
\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}
is the normalization of
\mathfrak{Y}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathfrak{IG}_{1,r,I}\times_{H^{\vee}_{1}}H_{n}^{\vee}
where
\mathfrak{IG}_{1,r,I}\to H_{1}^{\vee}
is the universal generator of
H_{1}^{\vee}
. The faithfully flat extension
H_{n}^{\vee}\to H_{1}^{\vee}
has different
\mathfrak{D}(H_{n}^{\vee}/H_{1}^{\vee})
that contains
\mathrm{Hdg}^{\frac{p^{n}-p}{p-1}}

[
AIP18
, Proposition 3.5]
. By flatness the kernel of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Y}}/(\alpha^{j})\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Y}^{\textrm{ord}}}/(\alpha^{j})
is killed by
\mathrm{Hdg}^{j(p^{r+1})+C_{1}}
. Since
\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}
is the normalization of
\mathfrak{Y}
, it is finite and in particular
\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}\subset\mathfrak{D}^{-1}(\mathfrak{Y}/\mathfrak{IG}_{1,r,I})
. Thus
\mathrm{Hdg}^{\frac{p^{n}-p}{p-1}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}}\subset\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Y}}
, which proves the second claim.
∎

Lemma 5.15
.

Let
g_{n}\colon\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}\to\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}
be the projection. The kernel and cokernel of
g_{n}^{*}\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}/\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}}\to\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}/\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}}
is killed by a power of
\mathrm{Hdg}
. Let
\vartheta\colon\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}^{\prime}\to\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}
be the projection. The kernel of
\vartheta^{*}\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}/\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}}\to\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}^{\prime}/\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}}
is killed by a power of
\mathrm{Hdg}
.

Proof.

This is similar to
[
AI21
, Lemma 3.3]
. For the second part one views
{\mathfrak{IG}^{\prime}}^{,\textrm{ord}}_{n,I}
as a torsor over
\mathfrak{X}_{\alpha,I}^{\textrm{ord}}
for the group

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}(\mathcal{O}_{L}/p^{n}\mathcal{O}_{L})^{\times}&\mu_{p^{n}}\otimes\mathfrak{d}^{-1}\\
0&(\mathcal{O}_{L}/p^{n}\mathcal{O}_{L})^{\times}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
, and argues as before using smoothness of
\mathfrak{IG}^{\textrm{ord}}_{n,I}
.
∎

Assumption 5.16
.

Let
k\colon\mathbb{T}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to{(\Lambda_{\alpha,I})}^{\times}
be a weight such that
k=\chi k^{0}
where
\chi=k|_{\Delta}
is the finite part of the character and
k^{0}=k\chi^{-1}
. Assume that for all
\sigma\in\Sigma
, there exists
u_{\sigma}\in\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}
, such that
k^{0}
factors as

\displaystyle k^{0}\colon(\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}\to(\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{K})^{\times}\simeq\prod_{\sigma}\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}\xrightarrow{(k^{0}_{\sigma})_{\sigma}}{(\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I})}^{\times}

with
k^{0}_{\sigma}(t)=\exp(u_{\sigma}\log{t})
for all
t\in\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
.

Let
s\colon\mathbb{T}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to{(\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I})}^{\times}
be a weight such that for all
\sigma\in\Sigma
, there exists
v_{\sigma}\in\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}
such that
s
factors as

s\colon(\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}\to(\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{K})^{\times}\simeq\prod_{\sigma}\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}\xrightarrow{(s_{\sigma})_{\sigma}}{(\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I})}^{\times}.

with
s_{\sigma}(t)=\exp(v_{\sigma}\log{t})
for all
t\in\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
.

In particular, we can take
\alpha=p
and
I=[0,1]
for
p\neq 2
, and
I=[0,1/2]
for
p=2
.

Note that the explicit description of the Gauss–Manin connection in (
11
) together with Lemma
5.15
implies that there exists an integer
D
such that
\nabla_{k}(\sigma)(\mathbb{W}_{k})\subset\frac{1}{p\mathrm{Hdg}^{D}}\mathbb{W}_{k+2\sigma}
for all
\sigma
. Let
\nabla(\sigma)\colon\mathbb{W}\to\frac{1}{p\mathrm{Hdg}^{D}}\mathbb{W}
be the map defined by
\nabla(\sigma)|_{\mathbb{W}_{k}}=\nabla_{k}(\sigma)
. In particular, for all
N\geq 1
,

(\nabla(\sigma)^{p^{f_{\sigma}}-1}-\textrm{id})^{N}(\mathbb{W}_{k})\subset\frac{1}{(p\mathrm{Hdg}^{D})^{(p^{f_{\sigma}}-1)N}}\mathbb{W}_{k}(\sigma).

Lemma 5.17
.

Let
\sigma\in\Sigma
induce the
\mathfrak{P}
-adic valuation on
L
. There exists an integer
\ell
depending on
r,n
and
p
, and an integer
C>0
, such that for any
g\in H^{0}(\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{r,p,[0,1]},\mathbb{W}_{k})^{U_{\mathfrak{P}}=0}
, and every positive integer
N
, we have

\displaystyle\Big(\nabla(\sigma)^{p^{f_{\sigma}}-1}-\textrm{\emph{id}}\Big)^{N}(g)\in\left(\frac{p}{\mathrm{Hdg}^{C}}\right)^{N}H^{0}(\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{\ell,p,[0,1]},\mathbb{W})\cap H^{0}(\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{\ell,p,[0,1]},\mathbb{W}_{k}(\sigma)).

Proof.

By Corollary
5.12
we see that

\Big(\nabla(\sigma)^{p^{f_{\sigma}}-1}-\textrm{id}\Big)^{N}(g)|_{\bar{\mathfrak{M}}^{\textrm{ord}}_{p,[0,1]}}\in p^{N}H^{0}(\bar{\mathfrak{M}}^{\textrm{ord}}_{p,[0,1]},\mathbb{W})\cap H^{0}(\bar{\mathfrak{M}}^{\textrm{ord}}_{p,[0,1]},\mathbb{W}_{k}(\sigma)).

Locally on
\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{r,p,[0,1]}
, we then have that

(p\mathrm{Hdg}^{D})^{(p^{f_{\sigma}}-1)N}\Big(\nabla(\sigma)^{p^{f_{\sigma}}-1}-\textrm{id}\Big)^{N}(g)\in\ker{\left(\mathbb{W}/(p^{p^{f_{\sigma}}N})\to\mathbb{W}^{\textrm{ord}}/(p^{p^{f_{\sigma}}N})\right)}.

Here
\mathbb{W}^{\textrm{ord}}=\mathbb{W}|_{\bar{\mathfrak{M}}^{\textrm{ord}}_{p,[0,1]}}
. By Corollary
4.37

\mathbb{W}^{\prime}/(p^{j})
is a polynomial algebra over
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}}/(p^{j})
for any
j
. Since
\mathbb{W}=\mathbb{W}^{0}\otimes\mathfrak{F}
, we first deal with
\mathbb{W}^{0}
. Here we see by Lemma
5.14

the kernel of
\mathbb{W}^{0}/(p^{p^{f_{\sigma}}N})\to\mathbb{W}^{0,\textrm{ord}}/(p^{p^{f_{\sigma}}N})
is killed by
\mathrm{Hdg}^{p^{f_{\sigma}}N(p^{r+1})+C_{n}}
. By the same lemma
\ker\left(\mathfrak{F}/(p^{p^{f_{\sigma}}N})\to\mathfrak{F}^{\textrm{ord}}/(p^{p^{f_{\sigma}}N})\right)
is killed by
\mathrm{Hdg}^{p^{f_{\sigma}}N(p^{r+1})+C_{2}}
. Therefore

p^{(p^{f_{\sigma}}-1)N}\mathrm{Hdg}^{N(2p^{f_{\sigma}}(p^{r+1})+D(p^{f_{\sigma}}-1)+C_{n}+C_{2}}\Big(\nabla(\sigma)^{p^{f_{\sigma}}-1}-\textrm{id}\Big)^{N}(g)\in p^{p^{f_{\sigma}}N}H^{0}(\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{r,\alpha,I},\mathbb{W}).

In particular, choosing
C\gg 0
, such that
CN\geq N(2p^{f_{\sigma}}(p^{r+1})+D(p^{f_{\sigma}}-1)+C_{n}+C_{2}
for all
N>0
, we see that

\mathrm{Hdg}^{CN}\Big(\nabla(\sigma)^{p^{f_{\sigma}}-1}-\textrm{id}\Big)^{N}(g)\in p^{N}H^{0}(\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{r,\alpha,I},\mathbb{W}).

Choosing
\ell\geq r
such that
p/\mathrm{Hdg}^{C}\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\ell,I}}
, we get that

\Big(\nabla(\sigma)^{p^{f_{\sigma}}-1}-\textrm{id}\Big)^{N}(g)\in\left(\frac{p}{\mathrm{Hdg}^{C}}\right)^{N}H^{0}(\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{\ell,\alpha,I},\mathbb{W}).

∎

Proposition 5.18
.

Let
k,s
be as in Assumption
5.16
, and suppose
\sigma\in\Sigma
induce the
\mathfrak{P}
-adic valuation.
Then for any prime
p\geq 3
, there exists an integer
\ell
depending on
r,n
and
p
such that for every
g\in H^{0}(\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{r,\alpha,I},\mathbb{W}_{k})^{U_{\mathfrak{P}}=0}
the sequences in
m

A(g,s_{\sigma})_{m}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\sum_{j=1}^{m}(-1)^{j-1}\frac{(\nabla(\sigma)^{p^{f_{\sigma}}-1}-\textrm{id})^{j}(g)}{j}

and if we write
H_{i,m}
for the set of tuples
(j_{1},\dots,j_{i})
of
i
positive integers with
j_{1}+\cdots+j_{i}\leq m
,

B(g,s_{\sigma})_{m}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\sum_{i=0}^{m}\frac{v_{\sigma}^{i}}{i!(p^{f_{\sigma}}-1)^{i}}\left(\sum_{(j_{1},\dots,j_{i})\in H_{i,m}}\Big(\prod_{a=1}^{i}\frac{(-1)^{j_{a}-1}}{j_{a}}\Big)(\nabla(\sigma)^{p^{f_{\sigma}}-1}-\textrm{id})^{j_{1}+\cdots+j_{i}}\right)(g)

converge in
H^{0}(\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{\ell,\alpha,I},\mathbb{W})
. Moreover, if we denote the limits

\log{\Big(\nabla(\sigma)^{p-1}\Big)}(g)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\lim_{m\to\infty}A(g,s_{\sigma})_{m}

and

\nabla(\sigma)^{s_{\sigma}}(g)=\exp\left(\frac{v_{\sigma}}{p^{f_{\sigma}}-1}\log{(\nabla(\sigma)^{p^{f_{\sigma}}-1})}\right)(g)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\lim_{m\to\infty}B(g,s_{\sigma})_{m}

then
\nabla(\sigma)^{s_{\sigma}}(g)\in H^{0}(\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{\ell,\alpha,I},\mathbb{W}_{k+2s_{\sigma}})
.
The same results hold for
p=2
if
v_{\sigma}\in 4\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}
.

Proof.

The convergence of
A(g,s_{\sigma})_{m}
is clear from Lemma
5.17
. We prove convergence for
B(g,s_{\sigma})_{m}
.

Let’s first deal with the case
p\geq 3
. Let

X\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{(\nabla(\sigma)^{p^{f_{\sigma}}-1}-\textrm{id})^{j_{1}+\cdots+j_{i}}(g)}{i!\prod j_{a}}.

Then by Lemma
5.17
,
X\in(p/\mathrm{Hdg}^{C})^{\sum j_{a}-v_{p}(i!)-\sum v_{p}(j_{a})}H^{0}(\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{\ell,\alpha,I},\mathbb{W})
. Now
v_{p}(i!)\leq\frac{i-1}{p-1}\leq\frac{i}{p-1}
. Hence
v_{p}(j_{a})\leq\frac{j_{a}-1}{p-1}
too. Using these inequalities,

\displaystyle\sum_{a=1}^{i}j_{a}-v_{p}(i!)-\sum_{a=1}^{i}v_{p}(j_{a})\geq\sum_{a=1}^{i}\left(j_{a}-\frac{1}{p-1}-v_{p}(j_{a})\right)\geq\sum_{a=1}^{i}j_{a}\left(1-\frac{1}{p-1}\right).

This proves convergence in this case. For the case
p=2
we note that the terms
\frac{(\nabla(\sigma)^{2^{f_{\sigma}}-1}-\textrm{id})^{j_{1}+\cdots+j_{i}}(g)}{\prod j_{a}}
do not have poles and the term
v_{\sigma}^{i}/i!
is divisible by
2^{i}
, which gives convergence in this case. Finally,
\nabla(\sigma)^{s_{\sigma}}(g)\in H^{0}(\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{\ell,\alpha,I},\mathbb{W}_{k+2s_{\sigma}})
as can be seen from its expansion as a power series and the fact that
t\ast\nabla(\sigma)(g)=(k+2\sigma)(t)\nabla(\sigma)(g)
.
∎

Thus given any
g\in H^{0}(\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{r,\alpha,I},\mathbb{W}_{k})^{\{U_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}=0\}_{i=1}^{h}}
, i.e.
g
lies in the kernel of
U_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}
for all
i
, there exists a large enough
\ell
depending on
r,n
and
p
such that one can consider
\prod_{\sigma}\nabla(\sigma)^{s_{\sigma}}(g)
as an element of
H^{0}(\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{\ell,\alpha,I},\mathbb{W}_{k+2s})
. Here
\prod_{\sigma}\nabla(\sigma)^{s_{\sigma}}
means the composition of the different
\nabla(\sigma)^{s_{\sigma}}
’s in any order. Note the order of composition does not matter since they mutually commute by Lemma
5.10
. Thus we fix such an
\ell
and define the following.

Definition 5.19
.

For
s\colon\mathbb{T}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to(\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I})^{\times}
as in Proposition
5.18
and
k
as in Assumption
5.16
, define
\nabla^{s}(g)
for
g\in H^{0}(\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{r,p,[0,1]},\mathbb{W}_{k})^{\{U_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}=0\}_{i=1}^{h}}
to be
\prod_{\sigma}\nabla(\sigma)^{s_{\sigma}}(g)\in H^{0}(\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{\ell,p,[0,1]},\mathbb{W}_{k+2s})
for some
\ell
for which the expression makes sense by Proposition
5.18
.

6.
Hecke operators and overconvergent projection

In this section we always assume that
\alpha=p
and
I=[0,1]
.

Recall that
p=\mathfrak{P}_{1}\cdots\mathfrak{P}_{h}
. In this section we first define the
U_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}
operators on
\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}
and the
V_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}
operators on
\mathfrak{w}_{k,\alpha,I}
. Next we will define the prime to
p
Hecke operators on
\mathfrak{w}_{k,\alpha,I}
.

6.1.
The
U_{\mathfrak{P}}
and
V_{\mathfrak{P}}
operators

For each
i=1,\dots,h
, fix
x_{i}\in L^{\times,+}
such that
\varpi_{i}/x_{i}\in(\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}
, and such that
\prod_{i=1}^{h}x_{i}=p
. Here
\varpi_{i}\in\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}=\prod_{j=1}^{h}\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{P}_{j}}
is the element with
p
in the
i
-th coordinate and
1
elsewhere.

Fix a suitable multi-index
\mathbf{r}=(r_{1},\dots,r_{h})
. Let
\mathbf{r}^{\prime}=(r_{1},\dots,r_{i}-1,\dots,r_{h})
. Let
\mathfrak{M}^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathbf{r},\alpha,I}\subset\bar{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathbf{r},\alpha,I}
be the inverse image of
M(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})\subset\bar{M}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})
under the projection
\bar{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathbf{r},\alpha,I}\to\bar{M}(\mu_{N},\mathfrak{c})
, and let
\mathcal{M}^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathbf{r},\alpha,I}
be its generic fibre. For each
i=1,\dots,h
, consider the subspace
\mathfrak{Y}_{i}\subset\mathfrak{M}^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathbf{r}^{\prime},\alpha,I}\times\mathfrak{M}^{x_{i}^{-1}\mathfrak{P}_{i}\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathbf{r},\alpha,I}
obtained as the normalization of the Hecke correspondence classifying pairs
(A^{\prime},\iota^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime},\psi^{\prime})
and
(A,\iota,\lambda,\psi)
, together with an isogeny
\pi_{i}\colon A^{\prime}\to A
, such that
\lambda=x_{i}^{-1}{\pi_{i}}_{*}\lambda^{\prime}
,
\ker\pi_{i}
is étale locally isomorphic to
\mathcal{O}_{L}/\mathfrak{P}_{i}
, and
\ker\pi_{i}
neither intersects the level
N
subgroup nor the canonical subgroup
H_{1}(A^{\prime})
. Choosing suitable smooth toroidal compactification, we can extend the Hecke correspondence to get maps
p_{1}\colon\bar{\mathfrak{Y}}_{i}\to\bar{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathbf{r}^{\prime},\alpha,I}
, and
p_{2}\colon\bar{\mathfrak{Y}_{i}}\to\bar{\mathfrak{M}}^{x_{i}^{-1}\mathfrak{P}_{i}\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathbf{r},\alpha,I}
. The map
p_{1}
is generically finite flat of degree
p^{f_{i}}
, and induces a trace map
\mathrm{Tr}_{p_{1}}
on the formal sheaves by normality. The Hecke correspondence lifts canonically to the partial Igusa tower. By Proposition
4.16
, the universal isogeny
\pi_{i}
induces a morphism of
\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{IG}_{n,\mathbf{r}^{\prime},I}}
-modules
\pi_{i}^{*}\colon p_{2}^{*}\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}\to p_{1}^{*}\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}
that preserves the Hodge filtration and the splitting, and induces an isomorphism
p_{2}^{*}\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}\xrightarrow{\sim}p_{1}^{*}\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}
. (We drop the polarization module from the notation when the context is clear)

Lemma 6.1
.

There is a morphism
\mathcal{U}_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}\colon{p_{1}}_{*}p_{2}^{*}\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}\to{p_{1}}_{*}p_{1}^{*}\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}
of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\mathbf{r}^{\prime},\alpha,I}}
-modules induced by the universal isogeny
\pi_{i}
which is an isomorphism on the modular sheaf
\mathfrak{w}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}
and which preserves the filtration and commutes with the Gauss–Manin connection. We also have a morphism
\mathcal{U}_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}\colon{p_{2}}_{*}p_{1}^{*}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}\to{p_{2}}_{*}p_{2}^{*}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}
of
\mathcal{O}_{\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{\mathbf{r}^{\prime},\alpha,I}}
-modules satisfying the same properties as above.

Proof.

The first claim follows from the definition of
\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}
and the morphism
\pi_{i}^{*}\colon p_{2}^{*}\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}\to p_{1}^{*}\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}
defined above. Considering the
\mathcal{U}
-correspondence on
\mathfrak{w}^{\chi}_{k,\alpha,I}
we get the required map for
\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}
.
∎

We can similarly consider the normalization
\mathfrak{Y}\subset\mathfrak{M}^{\mathfrak{c}}_{r,\alpha,I}\times\mathfrak{M}^{\mathfrak{c}}_{r+1,\alpha,I}
of the Hecke correspondence classifying pairs
(A,\iota,\lambda,\psi)
and
(A^{\prime},\iota^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime},\psi^{\prime})
, together with an isogeny
\pi\colon A^{\prime}\to A
, such that
\lambda=p^{-1}\pi_{*}\lambda^{\prime}
,
\ker{\pi}
is étale locally isomorphic to
\mathcal{O}_{L}/p\mathcal{O}_{L}
, and
\ker{\pi}
neither intersects the level
N
subgroup nor the canonical subgroup. Then similarly as above we have the following lemma.

Lemma 6.2
.

There is a morphism
\mathcal{U}\colon{p_{1}}_{*}p_{2}^{*}\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}\to{p_{1}}_{*}p_{1}^{*}\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}
of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{r,\alpha,I}}
-modules induced by the universal isogeny
\pi
which is an isomorphism on the modular sheaf
\mathfrak{w}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}
and which preserves the filtration and commutes with the Gauss–Manin connection. We also have a morphism
\mathcal{U}\colon{p_{2}}_{*}p_{1}^{*}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}\to{p_{2}}_{*}p_{2}^{*}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}
of
\mathcal{O}_{\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{r,\alpha,I}}
-modules satisfying the same properties as above. Moreover, the induced map on the
m
-graded pieces is
0
modulo
(p/\mathrm{Hdg}^{p+1})^{m}
.

Proof.

The proof of the first two claims is similar as above. For the last claim we observe that by Proposition
4.12
, the induced map
\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A^{\prime}}}/\Omega_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}\to\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{A}}/\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}
is multiplication by
p/HW(\sigma)^{p+1}
on the
\sigma
-component. The claim then follows from the local description of
\mathbb{W}^{0}_{k,\alpha,I}
.
∎

Definition 6.3
.

Define the
U_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}
operator as the composition
