U_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}\colon H^{0}(\bar{\mathfrak{M}}^{x_{i}^{-1}\mathfrak{P}_{i}\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathbf{r},\alpha,I},\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I})\xrightarrow{{p_{1}}_{*}\circ\mathcal{U}_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}\circ p_{2}^{*}}H^{0}(\bar{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathbf{r}^{\prime},\alpha,I},{p_{1}}_{*}p_{1}^{*}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I})\xrightarrow{\frac{1}{p^{f_{i}}}\mathrm{Tr}}H^{0}(\bar{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathbf{r}^{\prime},\alpha,I},\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I})[1/p].

Similarly, define the
U
operator as the composition

U\colon H^{0}(\bar{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathfrak{c}}_{r+1,\alpha,I},\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I})\xrightarrow{{p_{1}}_{*}\circ\mathcal{U}\circ p_{2}^{*}}H^{0}(\bar{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathfrak{c}}_{r,\alpha,I},{p_{1}}_{*}p_{1}^{*}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I})\xrightarrow{\frac{1}{p^{g}}\mathrm{Tr}}H^{0}(\bar{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathfrak{c}}_{r,\alpha,I},\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I})[1/p].

Remark 6.4
.

Since we chose the
x_{i}
’s such that
\prod_{i=1}^{h}x_{i}=p
, we have that
\prod_{i=1}^{h}U_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}=U
.

Corollary 6.5
.

For any
g\in H^{0}(\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{r+1,\alpha,I},\mathfrak{w}_{k,\alpha,I})
with
q
-expansion
g=\sum_{\mathfrak{ab}}a_{\beta}q^{\beta}
at a cusp
\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}(\!(\mathfrak{ab},S)\!)
,
U(g)=\sum_{\mathfrak{ab}}a_{p\beta}q^{\beta}
on
q
-expansions.

We now define the
V_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}
operator. Fix
\mathbf{r},\mathbf{r}^{\prime}
as before. Consider the map
p_{2}\colon\bar{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathbf{r},\alpha,I}\to\bar{\mathfrak{M}}^{x_{i}\mathfrak{P}_{i}^{-1}\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathbf{r}^{\prime},\alpha,I}
defined on the generic fibre by sending a tuple
(A,\lambda,\iota,\psi)
to
(A^{\prime},\iota^{\prime},x_{i}\lambda^{\prime},\psi^{\prime})
, where
A^{\prime}=A/H_{1}[\mathfrak{P}_{i}]\otimes\mathfrak{P}_{i}
, and
\iota^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime},\psi^{\prime}
are induced by the quasi-isogeny
A\xrightarrow{\pi}A/H_{1}[\mathfrak{P}_{i}]\xrightarrow{}A/H_{1}[\mathfrak{P}_{i}]\otimes\mathfrak{P}_{i}
. The map
p_{2}
lifts to the partial Igusa tower. The map
\pi^{\vee}\colon A/H_{1}[\mathfrak{P}_{i}]\to A\otimes\mathfrak{P}_{i}^{-1}
such that
\pi^{\vee}\circ\pi
is the quotient by the
\mathfrak{P}_{i}
-torsion, induces an isomorphism
(\pi^{\vee})^{*}\colon\Omega_{A}\otimes\mathfrak{P}_{i}^{-1}\to\Omega_{A/H_{1}[\mathfrak{P}_{i}]}
, which upon tensoring by
\mathfrak{P}_{i}
gives an isomorphism
(\pi^{\vee})^{*}\colon\Omega_{A}\to\Omega_{A^{\prime}}
. Let
p_{1}\colon\bar{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathbf{r},\alpha,I}\to\bar{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathbf{r},\alpha,I}
be the identity. Considering the isomorphism on the universal objects
(\pi^{\vee})^{*}\colon p_{1}^{*}\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}\xrightarrow{\sim}p_{2}^{*}\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}
, we get an isomorphism
\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}\colon p_{1}^{*}\mathfrak{w}_{k,\alpha,I}\simeq p_{2}^{*}\mathfrak{w}_{k,\alpha,I}
.

Definition 6.6
.

Define the
V_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}
operator as the map

V_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}\colon H^{0}(\bar{\mathfrak{M}}^{x_{i}\mathfrak{P}_{i}^{-1}\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathbf{r}^{\prime},\alpha,I},\mathfrak{w}_{k,\alpha,I})\xrightarrow{\mathcal{V}^{-1}_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}\circ p_{2}^{*}}H^{0}(\bar{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathbf{r},\alpha,I},\mathfrak{w}_{k,\alpha,I}).

We can similarly consider the map
p_{2}\colon\bar{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathfrak{c}}_{r+1,\alpha,I}\to\bar{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathfrak{c}}_{r,\alpha,I}
defined on the generic fibre by sending
(A,\iota,\lambda,\psi)
to
(A^{\prime},\iota^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime},\psi^{\prime})
with
A^{\prime}=A/H_{1}
and
\iota^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime},\psi^{\prime}
induced by the quotient
\pi\colon A\to A/H_{1}
. The map
p_{2}
lifts to the partial Igusa tower. Let
p_{1}\colon\bar{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathfrak{c}}_{r+1,\alpha,I}\to\bar{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathfrak{c}}_{r+1,\alpha,I}
be the identity. The isogeny
\pi^{\vee}\colon A/H_{1}\to A
such that
\pi^{\vee}\circ\pi=p
induces an isomorphism
(\pi^{\vee})^{*}\colon p_{1}^{*}\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}\xrightarrow{\sim}p_{2}^{*}\Omega_{\mathcal{A}}
which in turn induces an isomorphism
\mathcal{V}\colon p_{1}^{*}\mathfrak{w}_{k,\alpha,I}\simeq p_{2}^{*}\mathfrak{w}_{k,\alpha,I}
.

Definition 6.7
.

Define the
V
operator as the map

V\colon H^{0}(\bar{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathfrak{c}}_{r,\alpha,I},\mathfrak{w}_{k,\alpha,I})\xrightarrow{\mathcal{V}^{-1}\circ p_{2}^{*}}H^{0}(\bar{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathfrak{c}}_{r+1,\alpha,I},\mathfrak{w}_{k,\alpha,I}).

Remark 6.8
.

We have
\prod_{i}V_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}=V
.

Corollary 6.9
.

For any
g\in H^{0}(\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{r,\alpha,I},\mathfrak{w}_{k,\alpha,I})
with
q
-expansion
g=\sum_{\mathfrak{ab}}a_{\beta}q^{\beta}
at a cusp
\Lambda^{0}_{\alpha,I}(\!(\mathfrak{ab},S)\!)
,
V(g)=\sum_{\mathfrak{ab}}a_{\beta}q^{p\beta}
on
q
-expansions.

Proof.

Left to the reader.
∎

Corollary 6.10
.

(1)

U_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}\circ V_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}=\textrm{id}
on
H^{0}(\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{\mathbf{r},\alpha,I},\mathfrak{w}_{k,\alpha,I})
for any suitable multi-index
\mathbf{r}
.

(2)

For
g\in H^{0}(\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{\mathbf{r},\alpha,I},\mathfrak{w}_{k,\alpha,I})
, if we denote by
g^{[\mathfrak{P}_{i}]}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(\textrm{id}-V_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}\circ U_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}})(g)
the
\mathfrak{P}_{i}
-depletion of
g
, then
U_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}(g^{[\mathfrak{P}_{i}]})=0
. Moreover if
g=\sum_{\mathfrak{ab}}a_{\beta}q^{\beta}
, then
g^{[\mathfrak{P}_{i}]}=\sum_{\beta\notin\mathfrak{abP}_{i}}a_{\beta}q^{\beta}
.

Proof.

Left to the reader.
∎

Proposition 6.11
.

For every
\delta\in\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0}
, the
\Lambda_{\alpha,I}[1/\alpha]
-Banach module
H^{0}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I},\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I})
admits a slope
\delta
decomposition which restricts to a slope decomposition on
H^{0}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I},\mathrm{Fil}_{n}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I})
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Moreover, the inclusion
H^{0}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I},\mathrm{Fil}_{n}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I})^{\leq\delta}\subset H^{0}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I},\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I})^{\leq\delta}
is an isomorphism for
n
large enough (depending on
\delta
).

Proof.

The operator
U
is compact on the coherent sheaf
\mathrm{Fil}_{n}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}
, and so by the usual formalism of slope decomposition we have locally on the weight space a slope
\delta
decomposition

H^{0}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I},\mathrm{Fil}_{n}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I})=H^{0}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I},\mathrm{Fil}_{n}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I})^{\leq\delta}\oplus H^{0}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I},\mathrm{Fil}_{n}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I})^{>\delta}.

By Lemma
6.2
, the
U
operator on
H^{0}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I},\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}/\mathrm{Fil}_{n}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I})
is divisible by
p^{\delta+1}
for
n
large enough. It follows that
H^{0}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I},\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}/\mathrm{Fil}_{n}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I})
also admits a slope
\delta
decomposition and that
H^{0}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I},\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}/\mathrm{Fil}_{n}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I})^{\leq\delta}=0
.
∎

6.2.
Hecke operators prime to
p

We will not need to define the prime to
p
Hecke operators on the de Rham interpolation sheaf
\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}
, it’ll be sufficient to define them for the modular sheaf
\mathfrak{w}_{k,\alpha,I}
. We refer the reader to
[
AIP16a
, §8.5]
for the construction and definition of
T_{\ell}
for any ideal
\ell\nmid pN
. We remark that the construction in loc. cit. works integrally as well.

Let
f_{\mathfrak{c}}\colon\bar{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathfrak{c}}_{r,\alpha,I}\to\mathfrak{W}_{p}
be the structure map.

Proposition 6.12
.

For every ideal
\ell\nmid pN
, there exists an operator
T_{\ell}\colon{f_{\ell\mathfrak{c}}}_{*}\mathfrak{w}^{\ell\mathfrak{c}}_{k,\alpha,I}\to{f_{\mathfrak{c}}}_{*}\mathfrak{w}^{\mathfrak{c}}_{k,\alpha,I}
which is the Hecke operator at
\ell
.

Proof.

See
[
AIP16a
, §8.5]
.
∎

For a prime
\ell|N
, we define the
U_{\ell}
operator as follows. Consider the normalization
\mathfrak{Y}_{\ell}\subset\bar{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathfrak{c}}_{r,\alpha,I}\times\bar{\mathfrak{M}}^{\ell\mathfrak{c}}_{r,\alpha,I}
of the Hecke correspondence defined generically as classifying pairs
(A,\iota,\lambda,\psi)
and
(A^{\prime},\iota^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime},\psi^{\prime})
together with an isogeny
\pi_{\ell}\colon A\to A^{\prime}
such that
\ker\pi_{\ell}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{L}/\ell
étale locally, and
\ker\pi_{\ell}\cap\psi(\mu_{N}\otimes\mathfrak{d}^{-1})=0
. Then a similar discussion as before yields an operator
U_{\ell}\colon{f_{\ell\mathfrak{c}}}_{*}\mathfrak{w}^{\ell\mathfrak{c}}_{k,\alpha,I}\to{f_{\mathfrak{c}}}_{*}\mathfrak{w}^{\mathfrak{c}}_{k,\alpha,I}
.

Proposition 6.13
.

For every prime
\ell|N
, there exists an operator
U_{\ell}\colon{f_{\ell\mathfrak{c}}}_{*}\mathfrak{w}^{\ell\mathfrak{c}}_{k,\alpha,I}\to{f_{\mathfrak{c}}}_{*}\mathfrak{w}^{\mathfrak{c}}_{k,\alpha,I}
.

For
\ell
an ideal prime to
pN
, we define the central action. Define a map
S_{\ell}\colon\mathfrak{M}^{\mathfrak{c}}_{r,\alpha,I}\to\mathfrak{M}^{\mathfrak{c}\ell^{2}}_{r,\alpha,I}
as the normalization of the map induced on generic fibres by sending
A\mapsto A\otimes\ell^{-1}
together with the induced real multiplication, polarization and level structure. As before it is easy to see that there is a morphism
\pi_{\ell}^{*}\colon S_{\ell}^{*}\mathfrak{w}^{\mathfrak{c}\ell^{2}}_{k,\alpha,I}\to\mathfrak{w}^{\mathfrak{c}}_{k,\alpha,I}
.

Definition 6.14
.

The Hecke operator
S_{\ell}
is defined as

S_{\ell}\colon{f_{\mathfrak{c}\ell}}_{*}\mathfrak{w}^{\mathfrak{c}\ell^{2}}_{k,\alpha,I}\xrightarrow{S^{*}_{\ell}}{f_{\mathfrak{c}}}_{*}S^{*}_{\ell}\mathfrak{w}^{\mathfrak{c}\ell^{2}}_{k,\alpha,I}\xrightarrow{\pi_{\ell}^{*}}{f_{\mathfrak{c}}}_{*}\mathfrak{w}^{\mathfrak{c}}_{k,\alpha,I}.

6.3.
Hecke operators on arithmetic Hilbert modular forms

The Hecke operators defined above on the sheaf of geometric Hilbert modular forms induce Hecke operators on the sheaf of arithmetic Hilbert modular forms, which agree with the classically defined Hecke operators on automorphic forms using double coset operators. Let us first recall that we have the following diagram of analytic adic spaces.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I}^{G,\mathfrak{c}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I}^{\mathfrak{c}}\times_{\mathcal{W}}\mathcal{W}^{G}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I}^{\mathfrak{c}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}^{G}_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}_{p}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\mathfrak{c}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\mathfrak{c}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{\mathfrak{c}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

We recall that
\mathfrak{w}^{G,\mathfrak{c}}_{k,\alpha,I}=(p_{*}f^{*}\mathfrak{w}_{k,\alpha,I})^{\Gamma}[1/p]
, where
\Gamma=\mathcal{O}_{L}^{\times,+}/U_{N}^{2}
. The sheaf of overconvergent arithmetic Hilbert modular forms was defined as

\mathfrak{w}^{G}_{k,\alpha,I}[1/p]=\left(\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{c}\in\textrm{Frac}(L)^{(p)}}{g_{\mathfrak{c}}}_{*}\mathfrak{w}^{G,\mathfrak{c}}_{k,\alpha,I}[1/p]\right)/\left(L_{(x\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{c})}(f)-f\right)_{x\in\textrm{Princ}(L)^{+,(p)}}.

Proposition 6.15
.

(1)

For
\ell
an ideal in
\mathcal{O}_{L}
that is prime to
pN
, the operators
T_{\ell}
and
S_{\ell}
commute with the action of
\Gamma
and induce operators
T_{\ell}\colon{g_{\ell\mathfrak{c}}}_{*}\mathfrak{w}^{G,\ell\mathfrak{c}}_{k,\alpha,I}\to{g_{\mathfrak{c}}}_{*}\mathfrak{w}^{G,\mathfrak{c}}_{k,\alpha,I}
and
S_{\ell}\colon{g_{\mathfrak{c}\ell^{2}}}_{*}\mathfrak{w}^{G,\mathfrak{c}\ell^{2}}_{k,\alpha,I}\to{g_{\mathfrak{c}}}_{*}\mathfrak{w}^{G,\mathfrak{c}}_{k,\alpha,I}
respectively.

(2)

For
\ell
a prime ideal dividing
N
, the operator
U_{\ell}
commutes with the action of
\Gamma
, and induces an operator
U_{\ell}\colon{g_{\ell\mathfrak{c}}}_{*}\mathfrak{w}^{G,\ell\mathfrak{c}}_{k,\alpha,I}\to{g_{\mathfrak{c}}}_{*}\mathfrak{w}^{G,\mathfrak{c}}_{k,\alpha,I}
.

Moreover,
T_{\ell}
and
S_{\ell}
as defined above, commutes with
L(xc,c)
for any
x\in L^{\times,+}
, with
(x,p)=1
. Thus they induce endomorphisms
T_{\ell}
and
S_{\ell}
of
\mathfrak{w}^{G}_{k,\alpha,I}
which preserve the cuspforms.

Proof.

Left to the reader.
∎

We now explain how to descend the
U_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}
and
V_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}
operators to
\mathfrak{w}^{G}_{k,\alpha,I}
. The operator
U_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}
commutes with the
\Gamma
-action as well as the
\mathrm{Princ}(L)^{+,(p)}
-action, and descends to an operator
U_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}\colon{g_{x_{i}^{-1}\mathfrak{P}_{i}\mathfrak{c}}}_{*}\mathfrak{w}^{G,x_{i}^{-1}\mathfrak{P}_{i}\mathfrak{c}}_{k,\alpha,I}\to{g_{\mathfrak{c}}}_{*}\mathfrak{w}^{G,\mathfrak{c}}_{k,\alpha,I}
. However, this operator is dependent on the choice of
x_{i}
, and hence is not canonical. Similarly,
V_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}
descends to an operator
V_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}\colon{g_{x_{i}\mathfrak{P}_{i}^{-1}\mathfrak{c}}}_{*}\mathfrak{w}^{G,x_{i}\mathfrak{P}_{i}^{-1}\mathfrak{c}}_{k,\alpha,I}\to{g_{\mathfrak{c}}}_{*}\mathfrak{w}^{G,\mathfrak{c}}_{k,\alpha,I}
which also depends on the choice of
x_{i}
. We make these canonical in the following way. Let
(v_{\text{un}},w_{\text{un}})
be the universal weight on
\mathcal{W}^{G}_{p}
.

Definition 6.16
.

(1)

Define the operator
\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}\colon{g_{x_{i}^{-1}\mathfrak{P}_{i}\mathfrak{c}}}_{*}\mathfrak{w}^{G,x_{i}^{-1}\mathfrak{P}_{i}\mathfrak{c}}_{k,\alpha,I}\to{g_{\mathfrak{c}}}_{*}\mathfrak{w}^{G,\mathfrak{c}}_{k,\alpha,I}
as
\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(\varpi_{i}/x_{i})^{v_{\text{un}}}U_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}
.

(2)

Define the operator
\mathbf{V}_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}\colon{g_{x_{i}\mathfrak{P}_{i}^{-1}\mathfrak{c}}}_{*}\mathfrak{w}^{G,x_{i}\mathfrak{P}_{i}^{-1}\mathfrak{c}}_{k,\alpha,I}\to{g_{\mathfrak{c}}}_{*}\mathfrak{w}^{G,\mathfrak{c}}_{k,\alpha,I}
as
\mathbf{V}_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}=(x_{i}/\varpi_{i})^{v_{\text{un}}}V_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}
.

Proposition 6.17
.

The operators
\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}
and
\mathbf{V}_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}
are independent of the choice of
x_{i}
, and induce endomorphisms
\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}
and
\mathbf{V}_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}
of
\mathfrak{w}^{G}_{k,\alpha,I}
preserving cuspforms. Moreover,
\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}\circ\mathbf{V}_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}=\text{id}
, and
\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}=U
, and
\prod_{i=1}^{h}\mathbf{V}_{\mathfrak{P}_{i}}=V
.

Proof.

Left to the reader.
∎

6.4.
Overconvergent projection

In this section we will define the overconvergent projection in families upon studying the cohomology of the complex of
\mathcal{O}_{\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I}}
sheaves obtained by the connection
\nabla
which is described as follows. In particular in this section all sheaves are considered over the analytic adic spaces. Hence to simplify notation we will still write
\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}
but it is to be understood that this is a sheaf over
\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I}
. Consider the complex

(13)

\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}\xrightarrow{\nabla}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}\hat{\otimes}\Omega^{1}_{\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I}/\Lambda_{\alpha,I}}\cdots\xrightarrow{\nabla}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}\hat{\otimes}\Omega^{g}_{\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I}/\Lambda_{\alpha,I}}.

Denote by
\mathbb{W}^{\bullet}_{k,\alpha,I}
the complex obtained by tensoring the above complex by
\mathcal{O}_{\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I}}(-D)
(and the universal derivation on it) where we recall
D
is the boundary divisor. By Griffiths’ transversality we obtain a complex corresponding to the filtration on
\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}
as follows.

(14)

\mathrm{Fil}_{n}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}\xrightarrow{\nabla}\mathrm{Fil}_{n+1}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}\hat{\otimes}\Omega^{1}_{\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I}/\Lambda_{\alpha,I}}\cdots\xrightarrow{\nabla}\mathrm{Fil}_{n+g}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}\hat{\otimes}\Omega^{g}_{\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I}/\Lambda_{\alpha,I}}.

Denote by
\mathrm{Fil}_{n}^{\bullet}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}
the complex obtained by tensoring the above complex with
\mathcal{O}_{\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I}}(-D)
. By taking quotient of the first complex by the second we obtain a third complex
(\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}/\mathrm{Fil}_{n}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I})^{\bullet}
which sits in a short exact sequence of complexes on
\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I}
that gives a long exact sequence of hypercohomology groups.

(15)

\displaystyle\begin{split}0&\to H^{0}_{\textrm{dR}}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I},\mathrm{Fil}_{n}^{\bullet}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I})\to H^{0}_{\textrm{dR}}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I},\mathbb{W}^{\bullet}_{k,\alpha,I})\to H^{0}_{\textrm{dR}}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I},(\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}/\mathrm{Fil}_{n}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I})^{\bullet})\\
&\to H^{1}_{\textrm{dR}}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I},\mathrm{Fil}_{n}^{\bullet}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I})\to H^{1}_{\textrm{dR}}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I},\mathbb{W}^{\bullet}_{k,\alpha,I})\to\cdots\end{split}

Lemma 6.18
.

The cohomology of the de Rham complex of coherent sheaves
\mathrm{Fil}_{n}^{\bullet}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}
can be computed using global sections.

Proof.

We recall that if
f\colon\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I}\to\mathcal{M}^{*}_{r,\alpha,I}
is the projection to the minimal compactification, which is an affinoid adic space,
R^{i}f_{*}\mathfrak{w}_{k,\alpha,I}(-D)=0
. We note that in order to prove the lemma, it will be enough to prove that each sheaf in the complex
\mathrm{Fil}_{n}^{\bullet}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}
is acyclic for the direct image functor
f_{*}
. Because then an injective resolution of
\mathrm{Fil}_{n}^{\bullet}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}
will give an acyclic resolution of
f_{*}\mathrm{Fil}_{n}^{\bullet}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}
, and since
\Gamma(\mathcal{M}^{*}_{r,\alpha,I},\cdot)
is exact, the lemma will follow.

We first show that
\mathrm{Fil}_{n}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}(-D)
is acyclic for
f_{*}
. By Lemma
5.2
, the sheaf
\mathrm{Fil}_{n}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}(-D)
is equipped with a finite filtration such that the graded pieces are finite direct sums of sheaves of cuspforms. Thus the graded pieces of the filtration are acyclic for
f_{*}
by what we just recalled above. Then a simple spectral sequence argument proves that
\mathrm{Fil}_{n}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}(-D)
is
f_{*}
-acyclic. Moreover, using the Kodaira–Spencer isomorphism, this same proof shows that
\mathrm{Fil}_{n+i}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I}(-D)\hat{\otimes}{\Omega}^{i}_{\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I}/\Lambda_{\alpha,I}}
is also
f_{*}
-acyclic for all
0\leq i\leq g
.
∎

Lemma 6.19
.

Let
t_{L}=\sum\sigma
be the generator of the parallel weights. There exists an exact sequence

0\to H^{0}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I},\mathfrak{w}_{k+2t_{L}}(-D))\xrightarrow{i}H^{g}_{\textrm{dR}}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I},\mathrm{Fil}_{n}^{\bullet}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I})\to\coker{i}\to 0

where
i
is
U
-equivariant and
\coker{i}
is killed by
\prod_{\sigma}\prod_{i=0}^{n+g-1}(u_{\sigma}-i)
.

Proof.

We first note that

H^{g}_{\textrm{dR}}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I},\mathrm{Fil}_{n}^{\bullet}\mathbb{W}_{k})=\frac{H^{0}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I},\mathrm{Fil}_{n+g}\mathbb{W}_{k+2t_{L}}(-D))}{\nabla H^{0}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I},\mathrm{Fil}_{n+g-1}\mathbb{W}_{k}(-D)\hat{\otimes}\Omega^{g-1}_{\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I}/\Lambda_{\alpha,I}})}

The
U
-equivariant inclusion of
H^{0}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I},\mathfrak{w}_{k+2t_{L}}(-D))
inside
H^{0}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I},\mathrm{Fil}_{n+g}\mathbb{W}_{k+2t_{L}}(-D))
induces a map
H^{0}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I},\mathfrak{w}_{k+2t_{L}}(-D))\to H^{g}_{\textrm{dR}}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I},\mathrm{Fil}_{n}^{\bullet}\mathbb{W}_{k})
which is an inclusion as can be seen from the local description below. We are left to understand the cokernel of the inclusion, and in particular to show that the cokernel is killed by
\prod_{\sigma}\prod_{i=0}^{n+g-1}(u_{\sigma}-i)
.

The proof relies on the local description of the connection (
11
). Choosing a numbering
\sigma\colon\{1,\dots,g\}\simeq\Sigma
, we first write the sheaf
\mathrm{Fil}_{n+g-1}\mathbb{W}_{k}\hat{\otimes}\Omega^{g-1}_{\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I}/\Lambda_{\alpha,I}}
on local coordinates as

\mathrm{Fil}_{n+g-1}\mathbb{W}_{k}\hat{\otimes}\Omega^{g-1}_{\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I}/\Lambda_{\alpha,I}}=\bigoplus_{i}\mathfrak{w}_{k}^{\leq n+g-1}[V_{1},\dots,V_{g}]\mathrm{d}\hat{X_{i}}.

Here
\mathrm{d}\hat{X_{i}}
corresponds via Kodaira–Spencer to a generator of
\omega_{\mathcal{A}}^{2(t_{L}-\sigma_{i})}
and the superscript
\leq n+g-1
denotes we take the polynomials in
V_{j}
’s of degree at most
n+g-1
.

The map
\nabla\colon\mathrm{Fil}_{n+g-1}\mathbb{W}_{k}(-D)\hat{\otimes}\Omega^{g-1}_{\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I}/\Lambda_{\alpha,I}}\to\mathrm{Fil}_{n+g}\mathbb{W}_{k+2t_{L}}(-D)
can be described as the twist by
\mathcal{O}_{\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I}}(-D)
of a map

\bigoplus_{i}\mathfrak{w}_{k}^{\leq n+g-1}[V_{1},\dots,V_{g}]\mathrm{d}\hat{X_{i}}\xrightarrow{\nabla}\mathfrak{w}_{k+2t_{L}}^{\leq n+g}[V_{1},\dots,V_{g}].

that can be described using formula (
11
). In particular, the image of
\nabla
consists of polynomials in
V_{i}
of positive total degree and hence the map

\mathfrak{w}_{k+2t_{l}}(-D)\to\frac{\mathrm{Fil}_{n+g}\mathbb{W}_{k+2t_{L}}(-D)}{\nabla\mathrm{Fil}_{n+g-1}\mathbb{W}_{k}(-D)\hat{\otimes}\Omega^{g-1}_{\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I}/\Lambda_{\alpha,I}}}

is injective. Thus taking global sections we get
H^{0}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I},\mathfrak{w}_{k+2t_{L}}(-D))\xhookrightarrow{}H^{g}_{\textrm{dR}}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I},\mathrm{Fil}_{n}^{\bullet}\mathbb{W}_{k})
.

We prove the claim about the annihilator of
\coker{i}
by induction on
n
, the base case being
n=1-g
. For
n=1-g
we have
\mathrm{Fil}_{1}\mathbb{W}_{k+2t_{L}}/(\nabla\mathfrak{w}_{k}+\mathfrak{w}_{k+2t_{L}})\simeq\oplus_{i}\mathfrak{w}_{k+2t_{L}}V_{i}/u_{i}\mathfrak{w}_{k+2t_{L}}V_{i}
. This proves the base case. We have a diagram as follows with exact rows.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fil}_{n+g-2}\mathbb{W}_{k}\hat{\otimes}\Omega^{g-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fil}_{n+g-1}\mathbb{W}_{k}\hat{\otimes}\Omega^{g-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Gr}_{n+g-1}\mathbb{W}_{k}\hat{\otimes}\Omega^{g-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fil}_{n+g-1}\mathbb{W}_{k+2t_{L}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fil}_{n+g}\mathbb{W}_{k+2t_{L}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Gr}_{n+g}\mathbb{W}_{k+2t_{L}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla}

To complete the induction, we need to understand the connection on the graded pieces. Letting
k(1+\beta_{n}Z)
be a local generator of
\mathfrak{w}_{k}
, the map
\nabla
on the graded pieces can be described as follows.

\displaystyle\oplus\mathfrak{w}_{k}^{n+g-1}[V_{1},\dots,V_{g}]\mathrm{d}\hat{X}_{i}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\nabla}\mathfrak{w}_{k+2t_{L}}^{n+g}[V_{1},\dots,V_{g}]

\displaystyle k(1+\beta_{n}Z)\prod_{i}V_{i}^{n_{i}}\mathrm{d}\hat{X}_{j}

\displaystyle\mapsto(u_{j}-n_{j})(k+2t_{L})(1+\beta_{n}Z)V_{j}\prod_{i}V_{i}^{n_{i}}

This shows that the cokernel of
\nabla\colon\mathrm{Gr}_{n+g-1}\mathbb{W}_{k}\hat{\otimes}\Omega^{g-1}_{\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I}/\Lambda_{\alpha,I}}\xrightarrow{}\mathrm{Gr}_{n+g}\mathbb{W}_{k+2t_{L}}
is annihilated by
\prod_{\sigma}\prod_{i=0}^{n+g-1}(u_{\sigma}-i)
. The lemma then follows by applying Snake lemma to the diagram above and by the induction hypothesis.
∎

Lemma 6.20
.

For
\delta\in\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0}
, there exists
n\geq 0
such that the map

H^{i}_{\textrm{dR}}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I},\mathrm{Fil}_{n}^{\bullet}\mathbb{W}_{k,\alpha,I})^{\leq\delta}\to H^{i}_{\textrm{dR}}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I},\mathbb{W}^{\bullet}_{k,\alpha,I})^{\leq\delta}

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Arguing as in Proposition
6.11
, the sheaf
H^{i}_{\textrm{dR}}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I},(\mathbb{W}_{k}/\mathrm{Fil}_{n}\mathbb{W}_{k})^{\bullet})
admits slope decomposition locally on the weight space. Moreover, by the same proposition

H^{i}_{\textrm{dR}}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I},(\mathbb{W}_{k}/\mathrm{Fil}_{n}\mathbb{W}_{k})^{\bullet})^{\leq\delta}=0
for large enough
n
. The lemma then follows from the long exact sequence (
15
).
∎

Definition 6.21
.

For
\delta\geq 0
, let
n
be as in the above lemma. Let
\lambda=\prod_{\sigma}\prod_{i=0}^{n+g-1}(u_{\sigma}-i)
. For the finite slope
\delta\geq 0
, define the overconvergent projection in families to be the map induced by the isomorphisms as follows.

\displaystyle H^{\dagger}\colon H^{g}_{\textrm{dR}}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I},\mathbb{W}_{k}^{\bullet})^{\leq\delta}\otimes\Lambda_{\alpha,I}[\lambda^{-1}]

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{g}_{\textrm{dR}}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I},\mathrm{Fil}_{n}^{\bullet}\mathbb{W}_{k})^{\leq\delta}\otimes\Lambda_{\alpha,I}[\lambda^{-1}]

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{0}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I},\mathfrak{w}_{k+2t_{L}}(-D))^{\leq\delta}\otimes\Lambda_{\alpha,I}[\lambda^{-1}]

7.
Twisted triple product
p
-adic
L
-function

In this section we finally construct the twisted triple product
p
-adic
L
-function associated to finite slope families of Hilbert modular forms. We note that there has recently been a construction of these
p
-adic
L
-functions associated to Hida families due to I. Blanco-Chacón and M. Fornea
[
BF20
]
. We follow their article to describe the generalities of the setting.

Let
L/F
be a quadratic extension of totally real number fields, with
[F\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbb{Q}]=d
. Assume
p
is unramified in
L
. Let
\Sigma_{L}
and
\Sigma_{F}
be their respective set of embeddings into a common finite extension
K
of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
where both
L
and
F
are split. Let
G_{L}=\Res_{L/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbf{GL}_{2,L}
and
G_{F}=\Res_{F/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbf{GL}_{2,F}
. Let
(v,n)\in\mathbb{Z}[\Sigma_{L}]\times\mathbb{Z}
and
(w,m)\in\mathbb{Z}[\Sigma_{F}]\times\mathbb{Z}
be two classical weights.

Consider primitive eigenforms
g\in S^{G_{L}}({N_{g}},(v,n),\bar{\mathbb{Q}})
and
f\in S^{G_{F}}({N_{f}},(w,m),\bar{\mathbb{Q}})
generating irreducible cuspidal automorphic representations
\pi,\sigma
of
G_{L}(\mathbb{A})
and
G_{F}(\mathbb{A})
respectively. Here
S^{G_{L}}({N_{g}},(v,n),\bar{\mathbb{Q}})
denotes the space of arithmetic Hilbert cuspforms of level
N_{g}
and weight
(v,n)
with
\bar{\mathbb{Q}}
coefficients, and similarly for
S^{G_{F}}({N_{f}},(w,m),\bar{\mathbb{Q}})
. Denote by
\pi^{u},\sigma^{u}
their unitarizations and define a representation of
\mathbf{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{L\times F})
by
\Pi=\pi^{u}\otimes\sigma^{u}
. Let
\rho\colon\mathrm{Gal}(\bar{F}/F)\to S_{3}
be the homomorphism mapping the absolute Galois group of
F
to the symmetric group over 3 elements associated to the étale cubic algebra
L\times F/F
. Let
G_{L\times F}=\Res_{L\times F/F}\mathbf{GL}_{2,L\times F}
. The
L
-group
{}^{L}(G_{L\times F})
is given by the semi-direct product of
\hat{G}\rtimes\mathrm{Gal}(\bar{F}/F)
, where
\mathrm{Gal}(\bar{F}/F)
acts on
\hat{G}=\mathbf{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{C})^{\times 3}
through
\rho
. Assume the central character of
\Pi
is trivial when restricted to
\mathbb{A}_{F}^{\times}
.

Definition 7.1
.

The twisted triple product
L
-function associated with the unitary automorphic representation
\Pi
is given by the Euler product

L(s,\Pi,r)=\prod_{v}L_{v}(s,\Pi_{v},r)^{-1}

where
\Pi_{v}
is the local representation at the place
v
of
F
appearing in the restricted tensor product decomposition
\Pi=\otimes^{\prime}_{v}\Pi_{v}
, and representation
r
gives the action of the
L
-group of
G_{L\times F}
on
\mathbb{C}^{2}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{2}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{2}
which restricts to the natural
8
-dimensional representation of
\hat{G}
and for which
\mathrm{Gal}(\bar{F}/F)
acts via
\rho
permuting the vectors.

Let
D_{/F}
be a quarternion algebra. We denote by
\Pi^{D}
the irreducible unitary cuspidal automorphic representation of
D^{\times}(\mathbb{A}_{L\times F})
associated with
\Pi
by the Jacquet–Langlands correspondence when it exists. For a vector
\phi\in\Pi^{D}
one defines its period integral as

I^{D}(\phi)=\int_{[D^{\times}(\mathbb{A}_{F})]}\phi(x)\mathrm{d}x

where
[D^{\times}(\mathbb{A}_{F})]=\mathbb{A}_{F}^{\times}D^{\times}(F)\backslash D^{\times}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
. For simplicity of notation we write
I(\phi)
to denote the period integral when
D=M_{2}(F)
.

Theorem 7.2
.

Let
\eta\colon\mathbb{A}_{F}^{\times}\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
be the quadratic character attached to
L/F
by class field theory. Then the following are equivalent:

(1)

The central
L
-value
L(\frac{1}{2},\Pi,r)
does not vanish, and for every place
v
of
F
the local
\epsilon
-factor satisfies
\epsilon_{v}(\frac{1}{2},\Pi_{v},r)\eta_{v}(-1)=1
.

(2)

There exists a vector
\phi\in\Pi
, called a test vector, whose period integral
I(\phi)
does not vanish.

Proof.

This relies on Ichino’s formula
[
Ich08
, Theorem 1.1]
. For a complete proof see
[
BF20
, Theorem 3.2]
.
∎

Definition 7.3
.

Let
(v,n)\in\mathbb{Z}[\Sigma_{L}]\times\mathbb{Z}
and
(w,m)\in\mathbb{Z}[\Sigma_{F}]\times\mathbb{Z}
be two classical weights. We say the pair
(v,n)
and
(w,m)
are
F
-dominated if there exists
r\in\mathbb{N}[\Sigma_{L}]
such that
w=(v+r)_{|F}
and
mt_{F}={nt_{L}}_{|F}
(or equivalently,
m=2n
).

Remark 7.4
.

In their article
[
BF20
]
, the authors give sufficient criterion for the equivalent conditions of Theorem
7.2
to hold. If the weights
(v,n)
and
(w,m)
of
g
and
f
respectively are
F
-dominated, then the local
\epsilon
-factor at the archimedean places satisfy the hypothesis of the theorem. Moreover the same is true of the finite places if we assume that
\mathrm{N}_{L/F}(N_{g})\cdot d_{L/F}
and
N_{f}
are coprime, and that every finite prime dividing
N_{f}
splits in
L
.

Assumption 7.5
.

Assume that the equivalent conditions of Theorem
7.2
are satisfied. Also assume that the weights
(v,n),(w,m)
of
g,f
respectively are
F
-dominated.

Let
\mathfrak{A}
be an ideal in
\mathcal{O}_{F}
. Denote by
K_{11}(\mathfrak{A})
the following open compact group.

K_{11}(\mathfrak{A})=\left\{\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\mathbf{GL}_{2}(\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{F})\,|\,a\equiv d\equiv 1,c\equiv 0\text{ mod }\mathfrak{A}\right\}.

Let
\mathfrak{J}
be the element

{\begin{pmatrix}-1&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}}^{\Sigma_{F}}\in\mathbf{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})^{\Sigma_{F}}.

For any
h\in\sigma^{u}
, we define
h^{\mathfrak{J}}\in\sigma^{u}
to be the vector obtained by right translation
h^{\mathfrak{J}}(x)=h(x\mathfrak{J})
.

Let
\delta
be the Shimura–Maass differential operator
[
BF20
, 1956]
on nearly holomorphic cuspforms.

The natural inclusion
\mathbf{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{F})\to\mathbf{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{L})
defines by composition a
diagonal restriction
map

\zeta^{*}\colon S^{G_{L}}(K_{11}(\mathfrak{A}\mathcal{O}_{L}),(v,n),\mathbb{C})\to S^{G_{F}}(K_{11}(\mathfrak{A}),(v_{|F},2n),\mathbb{C}).

Definition 7.6
.

Let
\zeta\colon M(\mu_{\mathfrak{A}},\mathfrak{c})\to M(\mu_{\mathfrak{A}\mathcal{O}_{L}},\mathfrak{c}\mathcal{O}_{L}\mathfrak{d}^{-1}_{L/F})
be the map given on points by sending an abelian variety
A\mapsto A\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{F}}\mathcal{O}_{L}

[
BF20
, §4.1.2]
. Here
\mathfrak{d}_{L/F}
is the different ideal of the extension
L/F
.

Then as shown in loc. cit.

\zeta
induces the map on Shimura varieties given by the inclusion
\mathbf{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{F})\to\mathbf{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{L})
as above.

Lemma 7.7
.

For
A/\textrm{Spf}\hskip 1.99997pt{R}
parametrized by a point of
\mathfrak{IG}_{n,r,I}
, the trace map
\text{Tr}_{L/F}\colon\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{A}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{F}}\mathcal{O}_{L}\to\mathrm{H}^{\sharp}_{A}
preserves marked sections and the kernel of the marked splitting.

Proof.

The first statement follows from the functoriality of the
\dlog
map since
H^{\vee}_{n}(A\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{F}}\mathcal{O}_{L})=H_{n}^{\vee}(A)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{F}}\mathcal{O}_{L}
. The second statement follows from the fact that the kernel of the splitting is the kernel of Verschiebung, and the trace commutes with Verschiebung.
∎

Lemma 7.8
.

Let
r\in\mathbb{N}[\Sigma_{L}]
be such that
w=(v+r)_{|F}
. Then there is an
\mathcal{O}_{F}
ideal
\mathfrak{A}
supported on a subset of the prime factors of
N_{f}\cdot\mathrm{N}_{L/F}(N_{g})\cdot d_{L/F}
such that a test vector
\phi
as in the statement of Theorem
7.2
can be chosen to be of the form
\phi=(\delta^{r}(\breve{g}))^{u}\otimes(\breve{f}^{\mathfrak{J}})^{u}
for
\breve{g}\in S^{G_{L}}(K_{11}(\mathfrak{A}\mathcal{O}_{L}),(v,n),\bar{\mathbb{Q}})
and
\breve{f}\in S^{G_{F}}(K_{11}(\mathfrak{A}),(w,m),\bar{\mathbb{Q}})
. The cuspforms
\breve{g},\breve{f}
are eigenforms for all Hecke operators outside
N_{f}\cdot\mathrm{N}_{L/F}(N_{g})\cdot d_{L/F}
with the same eigenvalues of
g
and
f
respectively. Moreover, in this case the period integral can be expressed as

I(\phi)=\int_{[\mathbf{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{F})]}(\delta^{r}\breve{g})^{u}\otimes(\breve{f}^{\mathfrak{J}})^{u}\mathrm{d}x=\langle\zeta^{*}(\delta^{r}\breve{g}),\breve{f}^{*}\rangle

where
\breve{f}^{*}
is the cuspform in
S^{G_{F}}(K_{11}(\mathfrak{A}),(w,m),\bar{\mathbb{Q}})
whose adelic
q
-expansion coefficients are complex conjugates of those of
\breve{f}
, i.e.
\breve{f}^{*}=c\cdot\breve{f}|w_{N}
, where
w_{N}
is the Atkin-Lehner operator, and
c\neq 0
is a constant.

Proof.

[
BF20
, Lemma 3.4]
.
∎

Proposition 7.9
.

The value
L^{\text{alg}}(g,f)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{\langle\zeta^{*}(\delta^{r}\breve{g}),\breve{f}^{*}\rangle}{\langle f^{*},f^{*}\rangle}
is algebraic. We have

L\left(\frac{1}{2},\Pi,r\right)=(\ast)\cdot|L^{\text{alg}}(g,f)|^{2}

where
(\ast)
is a constant which can be ensured to be non-zero by suitably choosing the test vectors
\breve{g},\breve{f}
.

Proof.

The first statement is
[
BF20
, Proposition 3.5]
. The second statement follows from the proof of Theorem
7.2
using Ichino’s formula.
∎

Assumption 7.10
.

Let
g,f
be as above with weights
(v,n)
and
(w,m)
respectively that are
F
-dominated. Let
K
be a finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
containing all the Hecke eigenvalues of
g
and
f
, and such that
L
is split in
K
. Assume
f
has finite slope
\leq a
at a rational prime
p
not dividing
N_{g}N_{f}
, and unramified in
L
. Let
\breve{g},\breve{f}
be as in Lemma
7.8
, which by
[
Miy71
, Proposition 6]
are
K
-linear combinations of the oldforms coming from
g
and
f
respectively. Let
\omega_{g},\omega_{f}
be overconvergent families of weight
(v_{g},n_{g})
and
(w_{f},m_{f})
deforming
g
and
f
. Let
\breve{\omega}_{g},\breve{\omega}_{f}
be the overconvergent families of level
K_{11}(\mathfrak{A}\mathcal{O}_{L})
and
K_{11}(\mathfrak{A})
respectively, obtained from
\omega_{g}
and
\omega_{f}
using the same linear combinations of oldforms as with
\breve{g}
and
\breve{f}
. Therefore
\breve{\omega}_{g}
and
\breve{\omega}_{f}
interpolate
\breve{g}
and
\breve{f}
. Let
\Lambda_{g}
and
\Lambda_{f}
be the
p
-adically complete
K
-algebras such that
(v_{g},n_{g})\in\mathcal{W}^{G_{L}}(\Lambda_{g})
and
(w_{f},m_{f})\in\mathcal{W}^{G_{F}}(\Lambda_{f})
. Let
k_{g}=2v_{g}+n_{g}t_{L}
, and
k_{f}=2w_{f}+m_{f}t_{F}
. We assume that there exists a continuous character
r\colon(\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}\to\Lambda_{g}^{\times}
such that the following diagram is Cartesian.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Spm}(\Lambda_{g}\hat{\otimes}_{K}\Lambda_{f})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}^{G_{L}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Spm}(\Lambda_{f})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{W}^{G_{F}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(w_{f},m_{f})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(v_{g}+r,n_{g})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(v,n)\mapsto(v_{|F},2n)}

Assume that
r=r_{0}\cdot r_{\textrm{an}}
with
r_{0}\in\mathbb{N}[\Sigma_{L}]
and
r_{\textrm{an}}
is an analytic weight satisfying the same conditions as satisfied by the character
s
in Proposition
5.18
. Assume that
k_{g}
satisfies the same conditions as satisfied by
k
in Proposition
5.18
, i.e. the analytic part of
k_{g}
is given by
k^{\text{an}}_{g}(x)=\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma}\exp(u_{\sigma}\log(\sigma(x)))
, for all
x\in(\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}
and for
u_{\sigma}\in\Lambda_{g}
for all
\sigma
. For
\tau\in\Sigma_{F}
, let
v_{\tau}=u_{\sigma}+u_{\bar{\sigma}}
, where
\sigma,\bar{\sigma}
are the two extensions of
\tau
to
L
. Assume that the central character of
\zeta^{*}\omega_{g}\cdot\omega_{f}
is trivial.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be the set of primes lying above
p
in
L
. Denote by
\breve{\omega}_{g}^{[\mathcal{P}]}
the depletion of
\breve{\omega}_{g}
with respect to all the primes in
\mathcal{P}
. Under the above assumptions,
\nabla^{r}(\breve{\omega}_{g}^{[\mathcal{P}]})=\nabla^{r_{\textrm{an}}}(\nabla^{r_{0}}(\breve{\omega}_{g}^{[\mathcal{P}]}))
makes sense, and is a section of
\mathbb{W}^{G_{L}}_{k_{g}+2r,p,I}(-D)
in the sense of Definition
4.59
.

Let
\Lambda_{g,f}=\Lambda_{g}\hat{\otimes}_{\Lambda^{G_{F}}}\Lambda_{f}
. Consider the sheaves
\mathbb{W}_{k_{g}+2r,p,I}^{G_{L}}(-D)\otimes_{\Lambda_{g}}\Lambda_{g,f}
, and
\mathbb{W}_{k_{f},p,I}^{G_{F}}(-D)\otimes_{\Lambda_{f}}\Lambda_{g,f}
. By Lemma
7.7
and functoriality of VBMS, the trace map induces a map
\zeta^{*}\colon\mathbb{W}_{k_{g}+2r,p,I}^{G_{L}}(-D)\otimes_{\Lambda_{g}}\Lambda_{g,f}\to\mathbb{W}_{k_{f},p,I}^{G_{F}}(-D)\otimes_{\Lambda_{f}}\Lambda_{g,f}
, which is nothing but the diagonal restriction.

For each choice of representative
\mathfrak{c}_{i}
of
\mathrm{Cl}^{+}(\mathfrak{A})
, the corresponding component
\zeta^{*}\nabla^{r}(\breve{\omega}_{g}^{[\mathcal{P}]})_{i}
can be viewed as a class in
H^{d}_{\textrm{dR}}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{\ell,p,I}^{\mathfrak{c}_{i}}\otimes\Lambda_{g,f},\mathbb{W}_{k_{f}-2t_{F}})
for some suitable
\ell
determined by Proposition
5.18
, and
I\subset[0,1]
. Here we recall that
d=[F\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbb{Q}]
. Let
\zeta^{*}\nabla^{r}(\breve{\omega}_{g}^{[\mathcal{P}]})^{\leq a}_{i}
be its projection onto the slope “
\leq a
” part
H^{d}_{\textrm{dR}}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{\ell,p,I}^{\mathfrak{c}_{i}}\otimes\Lambda_{g,f},\mathbb{W}_{k_{f}-2t_{F}})^{\leq a}
of the de Rham cohomology group, possible by the spectral theory of the
U
operator
[
AIP18
, Appendice B]
. Then inverting
\lambda=\prod_{\tau\in\Sigma_{F}}\prod_{i=0}^{N}({v}_{\tau}-i)
for
N\gg 0
, it can be realized in the space
H^{0}(\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{\ell,p,I}^{\mathfrak{c}_{i}}\otimes\Lambda_{g,f}[\lambda^{-1}],\mathfrak{w}_{k_{f}})^{\leq a}
of slope
\leq a
using the overconvergent projection
H^{\dagger}
of Definition
6.21
. Since all of these operations commute with the action of
\Gamma
and
\text{Princ}(L)^{+,(p)}
, the sections
H^{\dagger}(\zeta^{*}\nabla^{r}(\breve{\omega}_{g}^{[\mathcal{P}]})^{\leq a}_{i})
together define an arithmetic overconvergent Hilbert modular form. Let us call this form
H^{\dagger,\leq a}(\zeta^{*}\nabla^{r}\breve{\omega}_{g}^{[\mathcal{P}]})
, which is a section of the
\mathrm{Spm}(\Lambda_{g,f}[\lambda^{-1}])
sheaf
\mathfrak{w}_{k_{f},p,I}^{G_{F}}(-D)^{\leq a}
in the sense of Definition
4.57
.

Let
\mathrm{Spm}(A)
be a connected affinoid of
\mathrm{Spm}(\Lambda_{g,f}[\lambda^{-1}])
. Consider the
A
-Banach module

M=H^{0}(\mathrm{Spm}(A),\mathfrak{w}_{k_{f},p,I}^{G_{F}}(-D)^{\leq a}).

Definition 7.11
.

Define the Hecke algebra
\mathcal{H}
acting on
M
to be the subring of
\End_{A}{M}
generated by the operators
T_{\ell},S_{\ell}
for
(\ell,p\mathfrak{A})=1
,
U_{\ell}
for
\ell|\mathfrak{A}
a prime, and
\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{P}}
for every prime
\mathfrak{P}|p
in
F
.

We note that for any element
f\in M
, the adelic
q
-expansion coefficient
c_{p}(y,f)
makes sense for any
y\in\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{F}F_{\infty,+}^{\times}
. This is because these coefficients only depend on the
q
-expansions of
f
at appropriate cusps as discussed in §
2.2.3
. Therefore, one can define a Hecke equivariant map

\displaystyle M

\displaystyle\to\mathcal{H}^{\vee}

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle\mapsto[T\mapsto c_{p}(1,f|T)].

The open affinoid
\mathrm{Spm}(\mathcal{H})
of the eigenvariety is finite and generically étale over the weight space
\mathrm{Spm}(A)
. Therefore the trace map
\mathrm{Tr}\colon\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{H}^{\vee}
is an isomorphism over the total ring of fractions
\mathbb{K}
of
A
.
Using this isomorphism we can define the Petersson inner product on
M
as follows.

Definition 7.12
.

Define the Petersson inner product as the pairing

\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle\colon M\times M\to\mathcal{H}^{\vee}\otimes_{A}\mathbb{K}\times\mathcal{H}^{\vee}\otimes_{A}\mathbb{K}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Tr}}\mathbb{K}.

Proposition 7.13
.

Let
x\in\mathrm{Spm}(A)
be a classical weight such that
M_{x}
consists entirely of classical forms of slope less than or equal to
a
, and the fibre
\mathcal{H}_{x}
if finite étale over
k(x)
. Then the Hecke equivariant map
M_{x}\to\mathcal{H}^{\vee}_{x}
is an isomorphism, and the inner product
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{x}
coincides upto a scalar with the classical Petersson inner product after base change
k(x)\to\mathbb{C}
.

Proof.

Under the assumption of étaleness, the fibre
\mathcal{H}_{x}
equals the Hecke algebra of
M_{x}
, and the first claim then follows from
[
Hid91
, Theorem 2.2]
. For the second claim, we notice that the trace pairing on
\mathcal{H}^{\vee}_{x}
is perfect. Hence
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{x}
is a Hecke equivariant perfect pairing on
M_{x}
. The result follows since the classical Petersson inner product is also a Hecke equivariant perfect pairing on
M_{x}\otimes_{k(x)}\mathbb{C}
.
∎

Definition 7.14
.

Let
\omega_{f}^{*}=\omega_{f}|w_{\mathfrak{A}}
where
w_{\mathfrak{A}}
is the Atkin–Lehner operator. The
p
-adic twisted triple product
L
-function associated to
g
and
f
satisfying the assumptions above is defined to be

\mathscr{L}^{g}_{p}(\breve{\omega}_{g},\breve{\omega}_{f})=\frac{\langle H^{\dagger,\leq a}(\zeta^{*}\nabla^{r}\breve{\omega}_{g}^{[\mathcal{P}]}),{\breve{\omega}_{f}^{*}}\rangle}{\langle{\omega_{f}^{*}},{\omega_{f}^{*}}\rangle}.

Corollary 7.15
.

For a pair of classical weights
\left((x,c),(y,2c)\right)\in\mathrm{Spm}(\Lambda_{g,f}[\lambda^{-1}])
factoring through
\mathrm{Spm}(A)
that are
F
-dominated, i.e. there exists
t\in\mathbb{N}[\Sigma_{L}]
, such that
(x+t)_{|F}=y
, letting
g_{x},\breve{g}_{x}
and
f_{y},\breve{f}_{y}
be the specializations of
{\omega}_{g},\breve{\omega}_{g}
and
\omega_{f},\breve{\omega}_{f}
at
(x,c)
and
(y,2c)
respectively, we have

\mathscr{L}_{p}^{g}(\breve{\omega}_{g},\breve{\omega}_{f})\left((x,c),(y,2c)\right)=\frac{\langle H^{\dagger,\leq a}(\zeta^{*}\nabla^{t}\breve{g}_{x}^{[\mathcal{P}]}),{\breve{f}_{y}^{*}}\rangle}{\langle{f_{y}^{*}},{f_{y}^{*}}\rangle}.

Proof.

Follows from construction since the projection to slope “
\leq a
” space, the overconvergent projection, and the diagonal restriction all commute with the specialization map.
∎

Next we compare our
p
-adic
L
-function with the definition given by Fornea and Blanco-Chacon
[
BF20
, Definition 3.8]
.

So let us now assume that
g,f
are ordinary, and the families
\omega_{g},\omega_{f}
are Hida families interpolating ordinary
p
-stabilizations
g^{(p)}
and
f^{(p)}
respectively. The families
\breve{\omega}_{g},\breve{\omega}_{f}
are
\Lambda_{g,f}
-adic families interpolating
\breve{g}^{(p)}
and
\breve{f}^{(p)}
as in loc. cit.

Proposition 7.16
.

Fix a pair of classical weights
((x,c),(y,2c))\in\mathrm{Spm}(A)
such that there exists
t\in\mathbb{Z}[\Sigma_{L}]
such that
y=(x+t)_{|F}
, and such that
(x,c)
and
(y,2c)
satisfy the condition of Proposition
7.13
. Then letting
g_{x},\breve{g}_{x}
and
f_{y},\breve{f}_{y}
be the specializations as above, we have

\mathscr{L}^{g}_{p}(\breve{\omega}_{g},\breve{\omega}_{f})((x,c),(y,2c))=\frac{\langle e_{n.o}\zeta^{*}(\theta^{t}\breve{g}^{[\mathcal{P}]}_{x}),\breve{f}_{y}^{*(p)}\rangle}{\langle f_{y}^{*(p)},f_{y}^{*(p)}\rangle}.

Here
e_{n.o}
is the ordinary projector
e_{n.o}=\lim_{n\to\infty}U^{n!}
. In particular, it coincides with Definition 3.8 in
[
BF20
]
at these points.

Proof.

We first note that since the Petersson product is Hecke equivariant, the special
L
-value
C=\mathscr{L}^{g}_{p}(\breve{\omega}_{g},\breve{\omega}_{f})((x,c),(y,2c))
only depends on the projection of
H^{\dagger,\leq a}(\zeta^{*}\nabla^{t}\breve{g}_{x}^{[\mathcal{P}]})
to the
\breve{f}_{y}^{*(p)}
isotypic component. In particular, since
\breve{f}_{y}^{*(p)}
is ordinary,

C=\frac{\langle e_{n.o}H^{\dagger,\leq 0}(\zeta^{*}\nabla^{t}\breve{g}_{x}^{[\mathcal{P}]}),{\breve{f}_{y}^{*(p)}}\rangle}{\langle{f_{y}^{*(p)}},{f_{y}^{*(p)}}\rangle}.

We note that since
\nabla^{t}\breve{g}_{x}^{[\mathcal{P}]}
is already realized at a finite step of the filtration on
\mathbb{W}_{k_{f}}
,
H^{\dagger}(\zeta^{*}\nabla^{t}\breve{g}_{x}^{[\mathcal{P}]})
makes sense, and
e_{n.o}H^{\dagger}(\zeta^{*}\nabla^{t}\breve{g}_{x}^{[\mathcal{P}]})=e_{n.o}H^{\dagger,\leq 0}(\zeta^{*}\nabla^{t}\breve{g}_{x}^{[\mathcal{P}]})
. Now by the proof of Lemma
6.19
,
\zeta^{*}\nabla^{t}\breve{g}_{x}^{[\mathcal{P}]}=H^{\dagger}(\zeta^{*}\nabla^{t}\breve{g}_{x}^{[\mathcal{P}]})+\nabla(h)
for some
h\in\mathrm{Fil}_{n+d-1}\mathbb{W}_{k_{f}}(-D)\hat{\otimes}\Omega^{g-1}_{\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{r,\alpha,I}}
. Let
\psi_{\textrm{Frob}}
be the unit root splitting over the ordinary locus. Then, since by Lemma
7.7
, the trace commutes with Verschiebung, implying the diagonal restriction commutes with unit root splitting, we have

\displaystyle H^{\dagger}(\zeta^{*}\nabla^{t}\breve{g}_{x}^{[\mathcal{P}]})=\psi_{\textrm{Frob}}(H^{\dagger}(\zeta^{*}\nabla^{t}\breve{g}_{x}^{[\mathcal{P}]}))

\displaystyle=\psi_{\textrm{Frob}}(\zeta^{*}\nabla^{t}\breve{g}_{x}^{[\mathcal{P}]})-\psi_{\textrm{Frob}}(\nabla h)

\displaystyle=\zeta^{*}\theta^{t}\breve{g}_{x}^{[\mathcal{P}]}-\psi_{\textrm{Frob}}(\nabla h).

Now
\psi_{\textrm{Frob}}\nabla=\sum_{\tau\in\Sigma_{F}}\theta_{\tau}
. Since
e_{n.o}\theta_{\tau}=0
for all
\tau\in\Sigma_{F}
, we have

e_{n.o}H^{\dagger,\leq 0}(\zeta^{*}\nabla^{t}\breve{g}_{x}^{[\mathcal{P}]})=\zeta^{*}\theta^{t}\breve{g}_{x}^{[\mathcal{P}]}.

This proves the claim.
∎

7.1.
Interpolation formula

We now prove an interpolation formula relating special values of this
p
-adic
L
-function to classical
L
-values.

Assume
(x,c)
and
(y,2c)
are a pair of
F
-dominated weights as before such that
y=(x+t)_{|F}
for some
t\in\mathbb{N}[\Sigma_{L}]
, and such that
(x,c)
and
(y,2c)
satisfy the condition of Proposition
7.13
. Assume further that the specializations
g_{x}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}={(\omega_{g})}_{x}
and
f_{y}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}={(\omega_{f})}_{y}
, which are classical by assumption, are eigenforms for all the Hecke operators
T_{\ell}
, where
\ell\nmid\mathfrak{A}
. Note in particular
\ell
can be
p
, and we assume
f_{y}
has slope
\leq a
. Moreover, assume that the Hecke polynomials for
T_{\mathfrak{p}}
for any prime
\mathfrak{p}|p
associated to both
g_{x}
and
f_{y}
are separable.

To relate the value
\frac{\langle H^{\dagger,\leq a}(\zeta^{*}\nabla^{t}\breve{g}_{x}^{[\mathcal{P}]}),\breve{f}_{y}^{*}\rangle}{\langle f_{y}^{*},f_{y}^{*}}
to the algebraic part of the classical
L
-value
\frac{\langle\zeta^{*}(\delta^{t}\breve{g}_{x}),\breve{f}_{y}^{*}\rangle}{\langle\breve{f}_{y}^{*},\breve{f}_{y}^{*}\rangle}
, we will split the computation into two cases, based on whether a prime
\mathfrak{p}|p
in
F
splits in
L
or stays inert. We will use this distinction to simplify the computation of Euler factors while passing from the
[\mathcal{P}]
-depletion of
\breve{g}
to
\breve{g}
.

We begin with some preparatory lemmas that we will repeatedly use in the computation.

Lemma 7.17
.

Let
\mathfrak{p}|p
be a prime in
F
.

(1)

\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}\nabla^{t}=p^{t_{\mathfrak{p}}}\nabla^{t}\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}
, where
t_{\mathfrak{p}}=\sum_{\sigma|\mathfrak{p}}t_{\sigma}
.

(2)

\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}H^{\dagger}=H^{\dagger}\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}
over any filtered piece of
\mathbb{W}_{k}
.

Proof.

(1)

We note that upto multiplication by a constant normalizing factor
p^{x_{\mathfrak{p}}}
which we can ignore,
\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}V_{\tau}=pV_{\tau}
if
\tau|\mathfrak{p}
, and
V_{\tau}
otherwise. By
[
Hid91
, §7G]
, on adelic
q
-expansions
c_{p}(y,f|\theta^{r})=y_{p}^{r}c_{p}(y,f)
. The claim then follows from the formula
c_{p}(y,f|\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{p}})=c_{p}(y\varpi_{\mathfrak{p}},f)
and equation (
12
), where
\varpi_{\mathfrak{p}}
is the idele with the uniformizer
p
in the
\mathfrak{p}
place and
1
elsewhere.

(2)

This again follows from equation (
12
), noting that
H^{\dagger}\nabla=0
. In fact one can work out a formula like
[
AI21
, Proposition 3.37]
for the overconvergent projection, and check the identity.

∎

Inert case
: Let
\mathfrak{p}|p
be a prime in
F
that stays inert in
L
. Let
\alpha_{g},\beta_{g}
be the two roots of the Hecke polynomial of
T_{\mathfrak{p}}
associated to
g_{x}
, which by assumption are not equal. Let
\breve{g}^{(\mathfrak{p})}_{\bullet}
be the
\mathfrak{p}
-stabilization of
\breve{g}_{x}
with eigenvalue
\bullet
for
\bullet=\alpha_{g},\beta_{g}
. Remark that
\breve{g}^{[\mathfrak{p}]}_{x}=\breve{g}^{(\mathfrak{p})[\mathfrak{p}]}_{\bullet}
for each of the
\mathfrak{p}
-stabilizations. This simply follows from
(1-\mathbf{V}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{p}})(1-(\bullet)\mathbf{V}_{\mathfrak{p}})=1-\mathbf{V}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}
. Finally, note that
\breve{g}_{x}=\frac{\alpha_{g}\breve{g}^{(\mathfrak{p})}_{\alpha}-\beta_{g}\breve{g}^{(\mathfrak{p})}_{\beta}}{\alpha_{g}-\beta_{g}}
.

Lemma 7.18
.

H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\breve{g}^{[\mathfrak{p}]}_{x})=H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\breve{g}^{(\mathfrak{p})[\mathfrak{p}]}_{\bullet})=\left(1-(\bullet)p^{t_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}\right)H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\breve{g}^{(\mathfrak{p})}_{\bullet}).

Proof.

We have

H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\breve{g}^{(\mathfrak{p})[\mathfrak{p}]}_{\bullet})=H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\breve{g}^{(\mathfrak{p})}_{\bullet})-(\bullet)H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\mathbf{V}_{\mathfrak{p}}\breve{g}^{(\mathfrak{p})}_{\bullet}).

By Lemma
7.17
,

\displaystyle H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\mathbf{V}_{\mathfrak{p}}\breve{g}^{(\mathfrak{p})}_{\bullet})

\displaystyle=H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(p^{t_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathbf{V}_{\mathfrak{p}}\nabla^{t}\breve{g}^{(\mathfrak{p})}_{\bullet})

\displaystyle=p^{t_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\breve{g}^{(\mathfrak{p})}_{\bullet}).

We note that to obtain the last equality we used the fact that
\mathbf{V}_{\mathfrak{p}}
commutes with diagonal restriction. This follows from the fact that on
\Sym^{k}H^{1}_{\text{dR}}
for any classical weight
k
,
\mathbf{V}_{\mathfrak{p}}
is induced (upto multiplication by
p^{-1}
) by the lift of the Frobenius isogeny at
\mathfrak{p}
. The claim follows.
∎

Lemma 7.19
.

Let
\mathscr{E}_{\mathfrak{p}}(g_{x},T)=(1-p^{t_{\mathfrak{p}}}\alpha_{g}T^{-1})(1-p^{t_{\mathfrak{p}}}\beta_{g}T^{-1})
. Then,

H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\breve{g}_{x})=\frac{1}{\mathscr{E}_{\mathfrak{p}}(g_{x},\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{p}})}H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\breve{g}^{[\mathfrak{p}]}_{x}).

Proof.

Follows from a simple computation using

H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\breve{g}_{x})=\frac{\alpha_{g}H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\breve{g}^{(\mathfrak{p})}_{\alpha})-\beta_{g}H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\breve{g}^{(\mathfrak{p})}_{\beta})}{\alpha_{g}-\beta_{g}}.

∎

Let
k=2y+2ct_{F}
. Let
a_{\mathfrak{p}}^{*}
be the
T_{\mathfrak{p}}
eigenvalue of
f_{y}^{*}
. Let
\gamma=e_{\breve{f}^{*}_{y}}H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\breve{g}_{x})
be the projection of
H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\breve{g}_{x})
onto the Hecke eigenspace of
\breve{f}^{*}_{y}
. We have the following lemma.

Lemma 7.20
.

For
\gamma
as above,
\langle\mathbf{V}_{\mathfrak{p}}(\gamma),\breve{f}^{*}_{y}\rangle=\frac{a_{\mathfrak{p}}^{*}\chi(\mathfrak{p})}{p^{k-t_{F}}(q_{\mathfrak{p}}+1)}\langle\gamma,\breve{f}^{*}_{y}\rangle
, where
\chi\colon\mathrm{Cl}^{+}(\mathfrak{A})\to\bar{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}
is the finite part of the central character associated to
f_{y}
, and
q_{\mathfrak{p}}=\#\mathcal{O}_{F}/\mathfrak{p}
.

Proof.

First note that
T_{\mathfrak{p}}\gamma=a_{\mathfrak{p}}^{*}\gamma
by definition. Consider the left action of the double coset operator

\left[K_{1}(\mathfrak{A})\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\varpi_{\mathfrak{p}}&0\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)K_{1}(\mathfrak{A})\right]=\bigsqcup_{j\in\mathcal{O}_{F}/\mathfrak{p}}\begin{pmatrix}\varpi_{\mathfrak{p}}&j\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}K_{1}(\mathfrak{A})\bigsqcup\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&\varpi_{\mathfrak{p}}\end{pmatrix}K_{1}(\mathfrak{A})

on
\gamma
as

\left[K_{1}(\mathfrak{A})\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\varpi_{\mathfrak{p}}&0\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)K_{1}(\mathfrak{A})\right]\gamma(u)=\sum_{j\in\mathcal{O}_{F}/\mathfrak{p}}\gamma\left(u\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\varpi_{\mathfrak{p}}&j\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)+\gamma\left(u\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&0\\
0&\varpi_{\mathfrak{p}}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right).

Then
T_{\mathfrak{p}}\gamma=p^{y-t_{F}}\left[K_{1}(\mathfrak{A})\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\varpi_{\mathfrak{p}}&0\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)K_{1}(\mathfrak{A})\right]\gamma
. For simplicity of notation, let
\alpha=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\varpi_{\mathfrak{p}}&0\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)
,
\beta=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&0\\
0&\varpi_{\mathfrak{p}}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
, and
h_{j}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&j\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)
. We then have

\langle h_{j}\alpha\gamma,\breve{f}^{*}_{y}\rangle=\langle\alpha\gamma,h_{-j}\breve{f}^{*}_{y}\rangle=\langle\alpha\gamma,\breve{f}^{*}_{y}\rangle.

Similarly, writing
\beta=k\alpha k^{\prime}
for
k,k^{\prime}\in K_{1}(\mathfrak{A})
, and
k^{\iota}
for the adjugate matrix of
k
, we have

\langle\beta\gamma,\breve{f}^{*}_{y}\rangle=\langle\alpha k^{\prime}\gamma,k^{\iota}\breve{f}^{*}_{y}\rangle=\langle\alpha\gamma,\breve{f}^{*}_{y}\rangle.

Now noting that
\mathbf{V}_{\mathfrak{p}}(\gamma)=p^{-y}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\varpi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}&0\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\gamma
, the formula follows from a simple computation.
∎

Let
\alpha_{f}^{*}
and
\beta_{f}^{*}
be the two roots of the Hecke polynomial of
T_{\mathfrak{p}}
associated to
f^{*}_{y}
. Letting
\gamma_{\alpha}^{(\mathfrak{p})}=\gamma-\beta_{f}^{*}\mathbf{V}_{\mathfrak{p}}(\gamma)
(and similarly for
\gamma_{\beta}^{(\mathfrak{p})}
) be the two
\mathfrak{p}
-stabilizations, we have the following proposition.

Proposition 7.21
.

Let
E(x,y)=(1-xy^{-1})
. Let
\mathscr{E}_{1}(T)=1-\frac{a_{\mathfrak{p}}^{*}\chi(\mathfrak{p})T}{p^{k-t_{F}}(q_{\mathfrak{p}}+1)}
. Then

\langle H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\breve{g}^{[\mathfrak{p}]}_{x}),\breve{f}^{*}_{y}\rangle=\left[\frac{\mathscr{E}_{\mathfrak{p}}(g_{x},\alpha_{f}^{*})\mathscr{E}_{1}(\beta_{f}^{*})}{E(\beta_{f},\alpha_{f})}+\frac{\mathscr{E}_{\mathfrak{p}}(g_{x},\beta_{f}^{*})\mathscr{E}_{1}(\alpha_{f}^{*})}{E(\alpha_{f},\beta_{f})}\right]\langle H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\breve{g}_{x}),\breve{f}^{*}_{y}\rangle.

Proof.

We have
\breve{f}^{*}_{y}=(\alpha_{f}^{*}-\beta_{f}^{*})^{-1}(\alpha_{f}^{*}\breve{f}^{*(\mathfrak{p})}_{\alpha}-\beta_{f}^{*}\breve{f}^{*(\mathfrak{p})}_{\beta})
for the two
\mathfrak{p}
-stabilizations
\breve{f}^{*(\mathfrak{p})}_{\alpha}
and
\breve{f}^{*(\mathfrak{p})}_{\beta}
of
\breve{f}^{*}_{y}
. The formula then follows by observing that on the
\breve{f}^{*(\mathfrak{p})}_{\star}
-component,
\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}
acts via
\star
, for
\star=\alpha_{f}^{*},\beta_{f}^{*}
.
∎

Split case
: Let
\mathfrak{p}|p
be a prime in
F
that splits as
\mathfrak{p}\mathcal{O}_{L}=\mathfrak{p}_{1}\mathfrak{p}_{2}
in
L
. For
i=1,2
, let
\breve{g}^{(\mathfrak{p}_{i})}_{\bullet}
be the
\mathfrak{p}_{i}
-stabilization of
\breve{g}_{x}
with eigenvalue
\bullet=\alpha_{i},\beta_{i}
.

Lemma 7.22
.

For
\mathfrak{i}\neq\mathfrak{j}
, we have
U\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\mathbf{V}_{\mathfrak{p_{i}}}\breve{g}^{[\mathfrak{p_{j}}]})=0
for any
\breve{g}
. In particular,

H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\mathbf{V}_{\mathfrak{p_{i}}}\breve{g})=H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\mathbf{V}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{p_{j}}}\breve{g}).

Proof.

This is a computation on
q
-expansion done in
[
BF20
, Lemma 3.10]
.
∎

Lemma 7.23
.

For any
\mathfrak{p_{i}}
-stabilization
\breve{g}^{(\mathfrak{p_{i}})}_{\bullet}
, we have
H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\breve{g}^{(\mathfrak{p_{i}})[\mathfrak{p_{j}}]}_{\bullet})=H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\breve{g}^{[\mathfrak{p_{1}},\mathfrak{p_{2}}]}_{x})
for
\mathfrak{i}\neq\mathfrak{j}
. Hence, by taking the appropriate linear combination, we have
H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\breve{g}^{[\mathfrak{p_{j}}]}_{x})=H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\breve{g}^{[\mathfrak{p_{1}},\mathfrak{p_{2}}]}_{x})
for any
\mathfrak{j}=1,2
.

Proof.

We have the following.

\displaystyle H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\breve{g}^{[\mathfrak{p_{1}},\mathfrak{p_{2}}]}_{x})

\displaystyle=H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}(1-\mathbf{V}_{\mathfrak{p_{i}}}\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{p_{i}}})\breve{g}^{(\mathfrak{p_{i}})[\mathfrak{p_{j}}]}_{\bullet})

\displaystyle=H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\breve{g}^{(\mathfrak{p_{i}})[\mathfrak{p_{j}}]}_{\bullet})-(\bullet)H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\mathbf{V}_{\mathfrak{p_{i}}}\breve{g}^{(\mathfrak{p_{i}})[\mathfrak{p_{j}}]}_{\bullet})

\displaystyle=H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\breve{g}^{(\mathfrak{p_{i}})[\mathfrak{p_{j}}]}_{\bullet}).

Here the last equality follows from Lemma
7.22
.
∎

Lemma 7.24
.

We have

H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\breve{g}^{[\mathfrak{p_{1}},\mathfrak{p_{2}}]}_{x})=\frac{\prod\limits_{\bullet,\star\in\{\alpha,\beta\}}1-p^{t_{\mathfrak{p}}}\bullet_{1}\star_{2}\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}}{1-p^{2t_{\mathfrak{p}}}\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-2}}=H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\breve{g}_{x}).

Proof.

We have the following chain of identities.

\displaystyle H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\breve{g}^{[\mathfrak{p_{i}}]}_{x})

\displaystyle=H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\breve{g}^{(\mathfrak{p_{i}})[\mathfrak{p_{i}}]}_{\bullet})

\displaystyle=H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\breve{g}^{(\mathfrak{p_{i}})}_{\bullet})-(\bullet)H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\mathbf{V}_{\mathfrak{p_{i}}}\breve{g}^{(\mathfrak{p_{i}})}_{\bullet})

\displaystyle=H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\breve{g}^{(\mathfrak{p_{i}})}_{\bullet})-(\bullet)H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\mathbf{V}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{p_{j}}}\breve{g}^{(\mathfrak{p_{i}})}_{\bullet})\quad(\text{by Lemma \ref{L720}})

\displaystyle=H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\breve{g}^{(\mathfrak{p_{i}})}_{\bullet})-(\bullet)p^{t_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{p_{j}}}\breve{g}^{(\mathfrak{p_{i}})}_{\bullet})

\displaystyle=\left[1-(\bullet)p^{t_{\mathfrak{p}}}(\alpha_{j}+\beta_{j})\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}\right]H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\breve{g}^{(\mathfrak{p_{i}})}_{\bullet})+(\bullet)p^{t_{\mathfrak{p}}}\alpha_{j}\beta_{j}\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\mathbf{V}_{\mathfrak{p_{j}}}\breve{g}^{(\mathfrak{p_{i}})}_{\bullet})

\displaystyle=\left[1-(\bullet)p^{t_{\mathfrak{p}}}(\alpha_{j}+\beta_{j})\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}+(\bullet)^{2}p^{2t_{\mathfrak{p}}}\alpha_{j}\beta_{j}\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-2}\right]H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\breve{g}^{(\mathfrak{p_{i}})}_{\bullet})

\displaystyle=(1-(\bullet)p^{t_{\mathfrak{p}}}\alpha_{j}\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1})(1-(\bullet)p^{t_{\mathfrak{p}}}\beta_{j}\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1})H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\breve{g}^{(\mathfrak{p_{i}})}_{\bullet}).

Now by Lemma
7.23
, since
H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\breve{g}^{[\mathfrak{p_{i}}]}_{\bullet})=H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\breve{g}^{[\mathfrak{p_{1}},\mathfrak{p_{2}}]}_{x})
, we have

\displaystyle H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\breve{g}_{x})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\alpha_{i}-\beta_{i}}\left[\frac{\alpha_{i}}{(1-p^{t_{\mathfrak{p}}}\alpha_{i}\alpha_{j}\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1})(1-p^{t_{\mathfrak{p}}}\alpha_{i}\beta_{j}\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1})}-\frac{\beta_{i}}{(1-p^{t_{\mathfrak{p}}}\beta_{i}\alpha_{j}\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1})(1-p^{t_{\mathfrak{p}}}\beta_{i}\beta_{j}\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1})}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\times H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\breve{g}^{[\mathfrak{p_{1}},\mathfrak{p_{2}}]}_{x})

\displaystyle=\frac{1-p^{2t_{\mathfrak{p}}}\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-2}}{\prod\limits_{\bullet,\star\in\{\alpha,\beta\}}1-p^{t_{\mathfrak{p}}}\bullet_{1}\star_{2}\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}}H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\breve{g}^{[\mathfrak{p_{1}},\mathfrak{p_{2}}]}_{x}).

∎

Proposition 7.25
.

Let
\mathscr{E}_{\mathfrak{p}}(g_{x},T)=\prod\limits_{\bullet,\star\in\{\alpha,\beta\}}(1-p^{t_{\mathfrak{p}}}\bullet_{1}\star_{2}T^{-1})
for a split prime
\mathfrak{p}
. Let
\mathscr{E}_{0,\mathfrak{p}}(g_{x},T)=1-p^{2t_{\mathfrak{p}}}\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\beta_{1}\beta_{2}T^{-2}
With assumptions as in Proposition
7.21
, we have

\langle H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\breve{g}^{[\mathfrak{p_{1}},\mathfrak{p_{2}}]}_{x}),\breve{f}^{*}_{y}\rangle=\left[\frac{\mathscr{E}_{\mathfrak{p}}(g_{x},\alpha_{f}^{*})\mathscr{E}_{1}(\beta_{f}^{*})}{E(\beta_{f},\alpha_{f})\mathscr{E}_{0,\mathfrak{p}}(g_{x},\alpha_{f}^{*})}+\frac{\mathscr{E}_{\mathfrak{p}}(g_{x},\beta_{f}^{*})\mathscr{E}_{1}(\alpha_{f}^{*})}{E(\alpha_{f},\beta_{f})\mathscr{E}_{0,\mathfrak{p}}(g_{x},\beta_{f}^{*})}\right]\langle H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\breve{g}_{x}),\breve{f}^{*}_{y}\rangle.

Proof.

This is similar to the proof of Proposition
7.21
. Write
\breve{f}^{*}_{y}=(\alpha_{f}^{*}-\beta_{f}^{*})^{-1}(\alpha_{f}^{*}\breve{f}^{*(\mathfrak{p})}_{\alpha}-\beta_{f}^{*}\breve{f}^{*(\mathfrak{p})}_{\beta})
and apply Lemma
7.24
to each of the
\mathfrak{p}
-stabilizations.
∎

Lemma 7.26
.

We have
\langle H^{\dagger,\leq a}\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\breve{g}_{x}),\breve{f}^{*}_{y}\rangle/\langle f^{*}_{y},f^{*}_{y}\rangle=\langle\zeta^{*}(\delta^{t}\breve{g}_{x}),\breve{f}^{*}_{y}\rangle/\langle f^{*}_{y},f^{*}_{y}\rangle
upon base change to
\mathbb{C}
.

Proof.

By
[
DR14
, Lemma 2.5]
, we have
\langle\zeta^{*}(\delta^{t}\breve{g}_{x}),\breve{f}^{*}_{y}\rangle=\langle\Pi^{\text{hol}}\zeta^{*}(\delta^{t}\breve{g}_{x}),\breve{f}^{*}_{y}\rangle

where
\Pi^{\text{hol}}
is the holomorphic projection of the nearly holomorphic modular form
\zeta^{*}(\delta^{t}\breve{g}_{x})
. But the holomorphic projection is by definition the same thing as the overconvergent projection of
\zeta^{*}(\nabla^{t}\breve{g}_{x})

[
DR14
, Proposition 2.8]
. The Petersson inner product cuts out the slope
\leq a
part since
\breve{f}^{*}_{y}
has slope
\leq a
. The lemma then follows from Proposition
7.13
, which shows that the pairing defined algebraically is a scalar multiple of the Petersson inner product.
∎

We can now collect all the results above to state the following theorem.

Theorem 7.27
.

With all the assumptions as above, the value of the
p
-adic
L
-function is related to the classical central
L
-value in the following manner.

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\mathscr{L}^{g}_{p}(\breve{\omega}_{g},\breve{\omega}_{f})((x,c),(y,2c))\\
&=\left(\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\text{ inert}}\left[\frac{\mathscr{E}_{\mathfrak{p}}(g_{x},\alpha_{f}^{*})\mathscr{E}_{1}(\beta_{f}^{*})}{E(\beta_{f},\alpha_{f})}+\frac{\mathscr{E}_{\mathfrak{p}}(g_{x},\beta_{f}^{*})\mathscr{E}_{1}(\alpha_{f}^{*})}{E(\alpha_{f},\beta_{f})}\right]\right)\\
&\times\left(\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\text{ split}}\left[\frac{\mathscr{E}_{\mathfrak{p}}(g_{x},\alpha_{f}^{*})\mathscr{E}_{1}(\beta_{f}^{*})}{E(\beta_{f},\alpha_{f})\mathscr{E}_{0,\mathfrak{p}}(g_{x},\alpha_{f}^{*})}+\frac{\mathscr{E}_{\mathfrak{p}}(g_{x},\beta_{f}^{*})\mathscr{E}_{1}(\alpha_{f}^{*})}{E(\alpha_{f},\beta_{f})\mathscr{E}_{0,\mathfrak{p}}(g_{x},\beta_{f}^{*})}\right]\right)\times\frac{\langle\zeta^{*}(\delta^{t}\breve{g}_{x}),\breve{f}^{*}_{y}\rangle}{\langle f^{*}_{y},f^{*}_{y}\rangle}.\end{aligned}
